LEARNING DICTIONARY

Volume 1  ·  Words 1–2,000

English → Spanish  ·  CEFR A1–C1 word selection

COMPLETE — ALL 2,000 WORDS

A1 + A2 + B1 + B2 + C1 vocabulary — 2,000 fully developed entries

every headword unique, checked, and ready for Volume 2 (words 2,001–4,000)

# About This Edition

This file is now complete: Volume 1 of the English–Spanish Learning Dictionary, all 2,000 words. Every headword — from essential A1 basics to nuanced C1 vocabulary — has a full entry: pronunciation, part of speech, Spanish translation, a learner-friendly definition, an English example sentence with its natural Spanish translation, and collocations or usage notes where they genuinely help.

•  A1 (407 words), A2 (352 words), B1 (469 words), B2 (420 words), and C1 (352 words) — 2,000 words total, every one unique and checked against the others.

•  The Appendix lists all 2,000 headwords alphabetically with their CEFR level, so Volume 2 (words 2,001–4,000) can be built without ever repeating a word from this volume.

•  Ask to start Volume 2 whenever you're ready, and it will pick up exactly where this one leaves off — words 2,001 onward, checked against this complete list.

Keeping the finished headword list stable now means future parts (and future volumes, 2,001–10,000) can be checked against it automatically, so no word is ever defined twice.

# How to Use This Dictionary

## What each entry contains

•  Headword and pronunciation (IPA, General American) and part of speech.

•  CEFR level — a quick guide to how essential or advanced the word is (see below).

•  Spanish translation(s) — the most natural equivalent, with alternatives when the word has more than one common meaning.

•  A short, plain-English definition — not a copy of any commercial dictionary.

•  An English example sentence, with a natural (not word-for-word) Spanish translation underneath.

•  Common collocations or phrases, when they genuinely help (achieve a goal, make a decision).

•  An occasional usage note — for false friends, irregular forms, or common learner mistakes.

## What the CEFR levels mean

•  A1 — essential beginner vocabulary you need from day one.

•  A2 — elementary, everyday vocabulary for simple daily situations.

•  B1 — intermediate vocabulary for expressing opinions and handling most situations.

•  B2 — upper-intermediate vocabulary for detailed, fluent conversation.

•  C1 — advanced vocabulary for nuanced, precise, professional-level English.

## How to study with this book

•  Little and often beats long, rare sessions. Aim for 15–25 new words a day using the 30-Day Study Tracker at the back.

•  Read the example sentence aloud, then cover it and try to say your own sentence with the word.

•  Learn collocations together with the word, not just the word alone — you'll recognize and use it far more naturally in real conversation.

•  Review, don't just learn once. Use the Review Checklist to test recognition, meaning, pronunciation, and free recall — recall without looking is the real test.

•  Keep a running list of words you keep forgetting in the My Difficult Words pages, and revisit that list weekly.

# A1 Vocabulary

Essential beginner vocabulary — 407 words, alphabetical.

AFTERNOON   /ˌæftərˈnuːn/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la tarde

Meaning:  the part of the day between midday and evening

Example:  We usually have lunch in the early afternoon.

→  Normalmente almorzamos a primera hora de la tarde.

Common:  this afternoon • good afternoon • in the afternoon

AIR   /ɛr/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el aire

Meaning:  the mixture of gases around us that we breathe

Example:  Open the window — this room needs some fresh air.

→  Abre la ventana: esta habitación necesita aire fresco.

Common:  fresh air • open air • air conditioning

AIRPORT   /ˈɛrpɔːrt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el aeropuerto

Meaning:  a place where airplanes take off and land, with buildings for passengers

Example:  My flight leaves from the airport at six in the morning.

→  Mi vuelo sale del aeropuerto a las seis de la mañana.

Common:  at the airport • airport security

ALSO   /ˈɔːlsoʊ/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  también

Meaning:  in addition; as well

Example:  I like tea, and I also like coffee.

→  Me gusta el té y también me gusta el café.

Note:  "Also" usually goes before the main verb; "too" usually goes at the end of the sentence.

ALWAYS   /ˈɔːlweɪz/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  siempre

Meaning:  at all times; every time

Example:  She always arrives early for work.

→  Ella siempre llega temprano al trabajo.

Common:  almost always • always late

AND   /ænd/  •  conjunction  •  A1

Spanish:  y

Meaning:  used to connect words or ideas that go together

Example:  We bought bread and milk at the store.

→  Compramos pan y leche en la tienda.

Note:  In Spanish "y" changes to "e" before a word starting with an "i" sound (padre e hijo).

ANIMAL   /ˈænɪməl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el animal

Meaning:  a living creature that is not a plant, such as a dog or a bird

Example:  Elephants are the largest land animals.

→  Los elefantes son los animales terrestres más grandes.

Common:  wild animal • pet animal

ANSWER   /ˈænsər/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  la respuesta; responder

Meaning:  something you say or write to reply to a question; to reply

Example:  I don't know the answer to this question.

→  No sé la respuesta a esta pregunta.

Common:  answer a question • answer the phone

APPLE   /ˈæpəl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la manzana

Meaning:  a round fruit with red, green, or yellow skin

Example:  She eats an apple every morning.

→  Ella come una manzana todas las mañanas.

Common:  apple juice • apple tree

APRIL   /ˈeɪprəl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  abril

Meaning:  the fourth month of the year

Example:  Her birthday is in April.

→  Su cumpleaños es en abril.

Note:  Months are not capitalized in Spanish: "abril", not "Abril".

ARM   /ɑːrm/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el brazo

Meaning:  the part of the body from the shoulder to the hand

Example:  He broke his arm playing football.

→  Se rompió el brazo jugando al fútbol.

Common:  cross your arms

ARRIVE   /əˈraɪv/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  llegar

Meaning:  to reach a place at the end of a journey

Example:  The train arrives at nine o'clock.

→  El tren llega a las nueve en punto.

Common:  arrive on time • arrive late

Note:  Followed by "at" or "in" (arrive at the station / arrive in Madrid) — never "arrive to".

ASK   /æsk/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  preguntar; pedir

Meaning:  to say something because you want information or help

Example:  Can I ask you a question?

→  ¿Puedo hacerte una pregunta?

Common:  ask a question • ask for help

Note:  Use "preguntar" to ask a question and "pedir" to ask for something.

AT   /æt/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  en; a

Meaning:  used to show an exact place, time, or point

Example:  Let's meet at the station at five o'clock.

→  Quedamos en la estación a las cinco.

AUGUST   /ˈɔːɡəst/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  agosto

Meaning:  the eighth month of the year

Example:  Many people go on vacation in August.

→  Mucha gente se va de vacaciones en agosto.

BABY   /ˈbeɪbi/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el bebé

Meaning:  a very young child

Example:  The baby is sleeping in her room.

→  El bebé está durmiendo en su habitación.

Common:  have a baby • baby food

BACK   /bæk/  •  noun; adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  la espalda; de vuelta

Meaning:  the part of the body from the neck to the bottom; returning to a place

Example:  My back hurts after sitting all day.

→  Me duele la espalda después de estar sentado todo el día.

Common:  come back • give something back

BAD   /bæd/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  malo/a

Meaning:  not good; of poor quality

Example:  The weather was really bad yesterday.

→  El tiempo estuvo muy malo ayer.

Note:  Irregular comparative/superlative: bad → worse → worst.

BAG   /bæɡ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la bolsa; el bolso

Meaning:  a container made of paper, plastic, or cloth used for carrying things

Example:  She put the apples in a bag.

→  Ella puso las manzanas en una bolsa.

Common:  shopping bag • plastic bag

BALL   /bɔːl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la pelota; el balón

Meaning:  a round object used in games and sports

Example:  The children were playing with a ball in the park.

→  Los niños estaban jugando con una pelota en el parque.

BANANA   /bəˈnænə/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el plátano; la banana (Latin America)

Meaning:  a long curved yellow fruit

Example:  I had a banana for breakfast.

→  Desayuné un plátano.

Note:  Spain usually says "plátano"; many Latin American countries say "banana".

BANK   /bæŋk/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el banco

Meaning:  a place where people keep and borrow money

Example:  I need to go to the bank to open an account.

→  Necesito ir al banco para abrir una cuenta.

Note:  "El banco" also means "the bench" — check the context.

BE   /biː/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  ser; estar

Meaning:  used to describe what something is, or how or where it is

Example:  She is a doctor, and today she is very tired.

→  Ella es médica, y hoy está muy cansada.

Note:  Use "ser" for permanent things (identity, character) and "estar" for temporary states, location, and feelings.

BEACH   /biːtʃ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la playa

Meaning:  an area of sand or stones next to the sea

Example:  We spent the whole day at the beach.

→  Pasamos todo el día en la playa.

BEAUTIFUL   /ˈbjuːtɪfəl/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  hermoso/a; bonito/a

Meaning:  very pleasant to look at

Example:  The sunset over the mountains was beautiful.

→  La puesta de sol sobre las montañas era hermosa.

BECAUSE   /bɪˈkɔːz/  •  conjunction  •  A1

Spanish:  porque

Meaning:  for the reason that

Example:  I stayed home because it was raining.

→  Me quedé en casa porque estaba lloviendo.

Note:  "Because of" (a causa de) is followed by a noun, not a full sentence.

BED   /bɛd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la cama

Meaning:  a piece of furniture that you sleep on

Example:  I go to bed at eleven every night.

→  Me voy a la cama a las once todas las noches.

Common:  go to bed • make the bed

BEHIND   /bɪˈhaɪnd/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  detrás de

Meaning:  at or to the back of something

Example:  The cat is hiding behind the sofa.

→  El gato se esconde detrás del sofá.

BETWEEN   /bɪˈtwiːn/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  entre

Meaning:  in the space or time separating two things

Example:  The shop is between the bank and the bakery.

→  La tienda está entre el banco y la panadería.

BIG   /bɪɡ/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  grande

Meaning:  large in size

Example:  They live in a big house near the park.

→  Viven en una casa grande cerca del parque.

BIKE   /baɪk/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la bicicleta

Meaning:  a vehicle with two wheels that you ride by pushing pedals

Example:  I ride my bike to school every day.

→  Voy en bicicleta a la escuela todos los días.

Common:  ride a bike • by bike

BIRD   /bɜːrd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el pájaro; el ave

Meaning:  an animal with feathers and wings that can usually fly

Example:  A small bird landed on the windowsill.

→  Un pájaro pequeño se posó en el alféizar de la ventana.

BIRTHDAY   /ˈbɜːrθdeɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el cumpleaños

Meaning:  the day of the year when someone was born

Example:  Happy birthday! How old are you today?

→  ¡Feliz cumpleaños! ¿Cuántos años cumples hoy?

Common:  birthday party • birthday present

BLACK   /blæk/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  negro/a

Meaning:  having the darkest color, like the night sky

Example:  He was wearing a black jacket.

→  Llevaba una chaqueta negra.

BLUE   /bluː/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  azul

Meaning:  having the color of a clear sky

Example:  The sky is a beautiful blue today.

→  El cielo está de un azul precioso hoy.

BODY   /ˈbɒdi/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el cuerpo

Meaning:  the whole physical structure of a person or animal

Example:  Swimming is good exercise for the whole body.

→  Nadar es un buen ejercicio para todo el cuerpo.

BOOK   /bʊk/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  el libro; reservar

Meaning:  a set of printed pages fixed together in a cover; to reserve something

Example:  I'm reading a great book about space.

→  Estoy leyendo un libro genial sobre el espacio.

Common:  read a book • book a table

BORING   /ˈbɔːrɪŋ/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  aburrido/a

Meaning:  not interesting; making you feel tired of something

Example:  The meeting was so boring that I almost fell asleep.

→  La reunión fue tan aburrida que casi me quedo dormido.

Note:  "Boring" describes what causes boredom; "bored" describes how a person feels — in Spanish both are just "aburrido/a".

BORROW   /ˈbɒroʊ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  pedir prestado

Meaning:  to take and use something that belongs to someone else, and return it later

Example:  Can I borrow your pen for a moment?

→  ¿Puedo pedirte prestado el bolígrafo un momento?

Note:  Don't confuse with "lend": I borrow FROM you; you lend TO me.

BOTTLE   /ˈbɒtəl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la botella

Meaning:  a container, usually made of glass or plastic, for holding liquids

Example:  She filled her bottle with water before the run.

→  Ella llenó su botella con agua antes de correr.

BOX   /bɒks/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la caja

Meaning:  a container with straight sides, often made of cardboard

Example:  We packed all the books into a box.

→  Metimos todos los libros en una caja.

BOY   /bɔɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el niño; el chico

Meaning:  a male child or young man

Example:  The boy is playing with his dog in the yard.

→  El niño está jugando con su perro en el patio.

BREAD   /brɛd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el pan

Meaning:  a food made from flour, water, and yeast, baked in an oven

Example:  We buy fresh bread every morning.

→  Compramos pan fresco todas las mañanas.

Common:  a loaf of bread

BREAK   /breɪk/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  romper; el descanso

Meaning:  to separate into pieces suddenly; a short rest

Example:  Be careful, or you'll break the glass.

→  Ten cuidado o romperás el vaso.

Common:  take a break

Note:  Irregular verb: break → broke → broken.

BREAKFAST   /ˈbrɛkfəst/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el desayuno

Meaning:  the first meal of the day, eaten in the morning

Example:  I usually have toast and coffee for breakfast.

→  Normalmente desayuno tostadas y café.

Common:  have breakfast

BRING   /brɪŋ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  traer

Meaning:  to take something or someone with you to a place

Example:  Can you bring your laptop to the meeting?

→  ¿Puedes traer tu portátil a la reunión?

Note:  Irregular: bring → brought → brought. Compare "bring" (toward the speaker) with "take" (away from the speaker).

BROTHER   /ˈbrʌðər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el hermano

Meaning:  a boy or man who has the same parents as you

Example:  My brother is two years older than me.

→  Mi hermano es dos años mayor que yo.

BROWN   /braʊn/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  marrón; café (some countries)

Meaning:  having the color of coffee or wood

Example:  She has beautiful brown eyes.

→  Tiene unos ojos marrones preciosos.

BUS   /bʌs/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el autobús

Meaning:  a large vehicle that carries many passengers along a fixed route

Example:  I take the bus to work every day.

→  Voy en autobús al trabajo todos los días.

Note:  Regional words also exist: camión (Mexico), guagua (Caribbean), colectivo (Argentina).

BUSY   /ˈbɪzi/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  ocupado/a

Meaning:  having a lot of things to do

Example:  I can't talk now — I'm really busy at work.

→  No puedo hablar ahora, estoy muy ocupado en el trabajo.

BUT   /bʌt/  •  conjunction  •  A1

Spanish:  pero

Meaning:  used to introduce something that contrasts with what was just said

Example:  I like the house, but it's too expensive.

→  Me gusta la casa, pero es demasiado cara.

BUY   /baɪ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  comprar

Meaning:  to get something by paying money for it

Example:  I need to buy some new shoes.

→  Necesito comprar unos zapatos nuevos.

Note:  Irregular: buy → bought → bought.

CALL   /kɔːl/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  llamar; la llamada

Meaning:  to speak to someone on the phone; to say something loudly

Example:  I'll call you when I get home.

→  Te llamaré cuando llegue a casa.

Common:  call back • phone call

CAR   /kɑːr/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el coche; el carro (Latin America)

Meaning:  a vehicle with four wheels that people drive

Example:  We drove to the coast in our new car.

→  Fuimos en coche hasta la costa.

Common:  drive a car • by car

Note:  Spain says "coche"; most of Latin America says "carro" or "auto".

CARRY   /ˈkæri/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  llevar; cargar

Meaning:  to hold something and take it somewhere

Example:  He carried the boxes upstairs.

→  Él llevó las cajas al piso de arriba.

CAT   /kæt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el gato

Meaning:  a small furry animal often kept as a pet

Example:  Our cat sleeps most of the day.

→  Nuestro gato duerme casi todo el día.

CHAIR   /tʃɛr/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la silla

Meaning:  a piece of furniture for one person to sit on

Example:  Please sit in this chair.

→  Siéntate en esta silla, por favor.

CHEAP   /tʃiːp/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  barato/a

Meaning:  not costing much money

Example:  This restaurant is cheap but the food is great.

→  Este restaurante es barato, pero la comida es excelente.

CHILD   /tʃaɪld/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el niño; la niña

Meaning:  a young boy or girl

Example:  Every child needs love and attention.

→  Todo niño necesita amor y atención.

Note:  Irregular plural: child → children.

CHURCH   /tʃɜːrtʃ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la iglesia

Meaning:  a building where Christians go to pray

Example:  They got married in a small church.

→  Se casaron en una iglesia pequeña.

CITY   /ˈsɪti/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la ciudad

Meaning:  a large town where many people live and work

Example:  New York is a very busy city.

→  Nueva York es una ciudad muy ajetreada.

CLASS   /klæs/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la clase

Meaning:  a group of students who learn together; a lesson

Example:  Our English class starts at nine.

→  Nuestra clase de inglés empieza a las nueve.

Common:  take a class • miss a class

CLEAN   /kliːn/  •  adjective; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  limpio/a; limpiar

Meaning:  free from dirt; to remove dirt from something

Example:  I need to clean my room this weekend.

→  Necesito limpiar mi habitación este fin de semana.

CLOCK   /klɒk/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el reloj

Meaning:  a device that shows the time, usually on a wall or table

Example:  The clock on the wall says it's noon.

→  El reloj de la pared dice que es mediodía.

Note:  "Reloj" also means "watch" (the one on your wrist).

CLOSE   /kloʊz/ (verb); /kloʊs/ (adjective)  •  verb; adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  cerrar; cerca

Meaning:  to shut something; near in distance or time

Example:  Please close the door — and the bakery is close to my house.

→  Cierra la puerta, por favor; y la panadería está cerca de mi casa.

Common:  close the door • close to

Note:  The verb and adjective are spelled the same but pronounced differently.

CLOTHES   /kloʊz/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la ropa

Meaning:  things people wear, such as shirts and pants

Example:  I need to buy some new clothes for winter.

→  Necesito comprar ropa nueva para el invierno.

Note:  "Clothes" has no singular form; "ropa" in Spanish is also always singular.

CLOUD   /klaʊd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la nube

Meaning:  a white or gray mass in the sky made of water droplets

Example:  Dark clouds mean it might rain soon.

→  Las nubes oscuras significan que puede llover pronto.

COAT   /koʊt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el abrigo

Meaning:  a piece of clothing worn over other clothes to keep warm

Example:  Take a coat — it's cold outside.

→  Llévate un abrigo, hace frío fuera.

COFFEE   /ˈkɔːfi/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el café

Meaning:  a hot drink made from roasted coffee beans

Example:  Would you like a cup of coffee?

→  ¿Quieres una taza de café?

Common:  a cup of coffee

COLD   /koʊld/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  frío/a; el resfriado

Meaning:  having a low temperature; a common mild illness

Example:  It's very cold today, and I think I have a cold too.

→  Hoy hace mucho frío, y creo que también tengo un resfriado.

Common:  catch a cold

COLOR   /ˈkʌlər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el color

Meaning:  the quality that makes things look red, blue, green, etc.

Example:  What color is your new car?

→  ¿De qué color es tu coche nuevo?

Note:  British spelling: "colour".

COME   /kʌm/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  venir

Meaning:  to move toward the speaker or a place

Example:  Can you come to my house tonight?

→  ¿Puedes venir a mi casa esta noche?

Common:  come back • come in

Note:  Irregular: come → came → come.

COMPUTER   /kəmˈpjuːtər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el ordenador (Spain); la computadora (Latin America)

Meaning:  an electronic device used to store and process information

Example:  I write all my documents on my computer.

→  Escribo todos mis documentos en la computadora.

COOK   /kʊk/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  cocinar; el cocinero

Meaning:  to prepare food using heat; a person who prepares food

Example:  My father cooks dinner every Sunday.

→  Mi padre cocina la cena todos los domingos.

COUNTRY   /ˈkʌntri/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el país

Meaning:  a nation with its own government and borders

Example:  How many countries have you visited?

→  ¿Cuántos países has visitado?

CRY   /kraɪ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  llorar

Meaning:  to produce tears, usually because you are sad or hurt

Example:  The baby started to cry when she woke up.

→  El bebé empezó a llorar cuando se despertó.

CUP   /kʌp/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la taza

Meaning:  a small container with a handle, used for drinking

Example:  He drinks two cups of tea every morning.

→  Él bebe dos tazas de té cada mañana.

DANCE   /dæns/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  bailar; el baile

Meaning:  to move your body to music

Example:  They danced all night at the party.

→  Bailaron toda la noche en la fiesta.

DARK   /dɑːrk/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  oscuro/a

Meaning:  having little or no light

Example:  It gets dark very early in winter.

→  Oscurece muy temprano en invierno.

DAUGHTER   /ˈdɔːtər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la hija

Meaning:  a person's female child

Example:  Their daughter just started university.

→  Su hija acaba de empezar la universidad.

DAY   /deɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el día

Meaning:  a period of twenty-four hours; the time when it is light outside

Example:  I have a busy day tomorrow.

→  Mañana tengo un día muy ocupado.

Common:  every day • one day

DECEMBER   /dɪˈsɛmbər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  diciembre

Meaning:  the twelfth and last month of the year

Example:  We usually visit family in December.

→  Normalmente visitamos a la familia en diciembre.

DIFFERENT   /ˈdɪfrənt/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  diferente

Meaning:  not the same as something else

Example:  My sister and I have very different opinions.

→  Mi hermana y yo tenemos opiniones muy diferentes.

Common:  different from

DIFFICULT   /ˈdɪfɪkəlt/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  difícil

Meaning:  not easy to do or understand

Example:  This exercise is too difficult for me.

→  Este ejercicio es demasiado difícil para mí.

DINNER   /ˈdɪnər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la cena

Meaning:  the main meal of the day, usually eaten in the evening

Example:  What do you want for dinner tonight?

→  ¿Qué quieres cenar esta noche?

Common:  have dinner

DIRTY   /ˈdɜːrti/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  sucio/a

Meaning:  not clean

Example:  Take off your dirty shoes before you come in.

→  Quítate los zapatos sucios antes de entrar.

DO   /duː/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  hacer

Meaning:  to perform an action or activity

Example:  What do you do on weekends?

→  ¿Qué haces los fines de semana?

Common:  do homework • do exercise

Note:  Irregular: do → did → done. Also used to form questions and negatives (Do you like it?).

DOCTOR   /ˈdɒktər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el médico; la médica

Meaning:  a person trained to treat sick people

Example:  I need to see a doctor about my headache.

→  Necesito ver a un médico por mi dolor de cabeza.

Common:  see a doctor

DOG   /dɔːɡ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el perro

Meaning:  a common four-legged animal often kept as a pet

Example:  We walk our dog every evening.

→  Paseamos a nuestro perro todas las tardes.

DOOR   /dɔːr/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la puerta

Meaning:  a movable barrier used to open and close an entrance

Example:  Can you close the door, please?

→  ¿Puedes cerrar la puerta, por favor?

DOWN   /daʊn/  •  adverb; preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  abajo; hacia abajo

Meaning:  toward a lower position or place

Example:  She walked down the stairs slowly.

→  Ella bajó las escaleras despacio.

Common:  sit down • go down

DRESS   /drɛs/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  el vestido; vestirse

Meaning:  a piece of clothing worn by women and girls; to put on clothes

Example:  She wore a blue dress to the wedding.

→  Ella llevó un vestido azul a la boda.

DRINK   /drɪŋk/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  beber; la bebida

Meaning:  to take liquid into your mouth and swallow it

Example:  You should drink more water every day.

→  Deberías beber más agua todos los días.

Note:  Irregular: drink → drank → drunk.

DRIVE   /draɪv/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  conducir; manejar (Latin America)

Meaning:  to control and operate a vehicle

Example:  She drives to work every morning.

→  Ella conduce al trabajo todas las mañanas.

Note:  Irregular: drive → drove → driven.

EAR   /ɪr/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la oreja; el oído

Meaning:  the part of the body used for hearing

Example:  He whispered something in her ear.

→  Él le susurró algo al oído.

Note:  "Oreja" is the outer ear; "oído" is the inner ear used for hearing.

EARTH   /ɜːrθ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la Tierra; la tierra

Meaning:  the planet we live on; soil

Example:  The Earth takes a year to travel around the sun.

→  La Tierra tarda un año en dar la vuelta alrededor del sol.

EASY   /ˈiːzi/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  fácil

Meaning:  not difficult; simple to do

Example:  The test was easier than I expected.

→  El examen fue más fácil de lo que esperaba.

EAT   /iːt/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  comer

Meaning:  to put food in your mouth and swallow it

Example:  We usually eat dinner at seven.

→  Normalmente cenamos a las siete.

Note:  Irregular: eat → ate → eaten.

EGG   /ɛɡ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el huevo

Meaning:  an oval object laid by a bird, often eaten as food

Example:  I had two eggs for breakfast.

→  Desayuné dos huevos.

EIGHT   /eɪt/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  ocho

Meaning:  the number 8

Example:  The store opens at eight in the morning.

→  La tienda abre a las ocho de la mañana.

ELEVEN   /ɪˈlɛvən/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  once

Meaning:  the number 11

Example:  My class starts at eleven o'clock.

→  Mi clase empieza a las once en punto.

EMAIL   /ˈiːmeɪl/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  el correo electrónico; enviar un correo

Meaning:  a message sent electronically; to send such a message

Example:  I'll email you the details tomorrow.

→  Te enviaré los detalles por correo electrónico mañana.

Common:  send an email

EMPTY   /ˈɛmpti/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  vacío/a

Meaning:  having nothing inside

Example:  The bottle is empty — we need more water.

→  La botella está vacía, necesitamos más agua.

ENJOY   /ɪnˈdʒɔɪ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  disfrutar de

Meaning:  to get pleasure from something

Example:  I really enjoy reading before bed.

→  Disfruto mucho de leer antes de dormir.

Common:  enjoy yourself

Note:  Followed by a noun or a verb + -ing, never by "to" + verb.

EVENING   /ˈiːvnɪŋ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la tarde; la noche

Meaning:  the part of the day between the end of the afternoon and night

Example:  We're having friends over this evening.

→  Esta tarde-noche vienen unos amigos a casa.

Common:  good evening • this evening

EXAM   /ɪɡˈzæm/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el examen

Meaning:  a formal test of knowledge or skill

Example:  I have a math exam next Monday.

→  Tengo un examen de matemáticas el próximo lunes.

Common:  pass an exam • fail an exam

EXCUSE   /ɪkˈskjuːz/ (verb); /ɪkˈskjuːs/ (noun)  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  disculpar; la excusa

Meaning:  to forgive a small mistake; a reason given for doing something wrong

Example:  Excuse me, could you help me, please?

→  Disculpe, ¿podría ayudarme, por favor?

Common:  excuse me

EXPENSIVE   /ɪkˈspɛnsɪv/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  caro/a

Meaning:  costing a lot of money

Example:  That restaurant is too expensive for us.

→  Ese restaurante es demasiado caro para nosotros.

EYE   /aɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el ojo

Meaning:  the organ of the body used for seeing

Example:  She has bright green eyes.

→  Ella tiene los ojos de un verde brillante.

FACE   /feɪs/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  la cara; enfrentar

Meaning:  the front part of the head; to deal with a difficult situation

Example:  He had a big smile on his face.

→  Tenía una gran sonrisa en la cara.

FAMILY   /ˈfæməli/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la familia

Meaning:  a group of people related to each other, such as parents and children

Example:  We have a big family dinner every Sunday.

→  Hacemos una gran cena familiar todos los domingos.

FAR   /fɑːr/  •  adverb; adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  lejos

Meaning:  at or to a great distance

Example:  The station isn't far from here.

→  La estación no está lejos de aquí.

Common:  far away

FAST   /fæst/  •  adjective; adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  rápido/a

Meaning:  moving or happening with great speed

Example:  He drives too fast on this road.

→  Conduce demasiado rápido por esta carretera.

FATHER   /ˈfɑːðər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el padre

Meaning:  a man in relation to his child

Example:  My father taught me how to swim.

→  Mi padre me enseñó a nadar.

FEBRUARY   /ˈfɛbjuɛri/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  febrero

Meaning:  the second month of the year

Example:  It's usually very cold in February.

→  Normalmente hace mucho frío en febrero.

FEEL   /fiːl/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  sentir; sentirse

Meaning:  to experience a physical or emotional state

Example:  I feel tired after such a long day.

→  Me siento cansado después de un día tan largo.

Common:  feel like

Note:  Irregular: feel → felt → felt.

FIFTY   /ˈfɪfti/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  cincuenta

Meaning:  the number 50

Example:  There were about fifty people at the party.

→  Había unas cincuenta personas en la fiesta.

FILM   /fɪlm/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la película

Meaning:  a story shown at the cinema or on television

Example:  We watched a great film last night.

→  Vimos una película genial anoche.

Note:  American English usually says "movie".

FIND   /faɪnd/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  encontrar

Meaning:  to discover something after looking for it, or by chance

Example:  I can't find my keys anywhere.

→  No encuentro mis llaves por ningún lado.

Note:  Irregular: find → found → found.

FINGER   /ˈfɪŋɡər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el dedo

Meaning:  one of the five parts at the end of the hand

Example:  She cut her finger while cooking.

→  Se cortó el dedo mientras cocinaba.

Note:  "Dedo" is used for both fingers and toes in Spanish.

FIRE   /ˈfaɪər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el fuego; el incendio

Meaning:  the light and heat produced by burning; a destructive burning event

Example:  They sat around the fire and told stories.

→  Se sentaron alrededor del fuego y contaron historias.

FIRST   /fɜːrst/  •  adjective; adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  primero/a

Meaning:  coming before all others in order or time

Example:  This is the first time I've been to Spain.

→  Esta es la primera vez que vengo a España.

Common:  first of all

FISH   /fɪʃ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el pez; el pescado

Meaning:  an animal that lives in water and breathes through gills

Example:  We had grilled fish for dinner.

→  Cenamos pescado a la parrilla.

Note:  "Pez" is the live animal; "pescado" is fish as food.

FIVE   /faɪv/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  cinco

Meaning:  the number 5

Example:  I'll meet you at five o'clock.

→  Nos vemos a las cinco.

FIX   /fɪks/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  arreglar; reparar

Meaning:  to repair something that is broken

Example:  Can you fix my computer? It won't turn on.

→  ¿Puedes arreglarme el ordenador? No enciende.

FLOWER   /ˈflaʊər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la flor

Meaning:  the colorful part of a plant that produces seeds

Example:  She grows flowers in her garden.

→  Ella cultiva flores en su jardín.

FLY   /flaɪ/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  volar; la mosca

Meaning:  to move through the air; a small flying insect

Example:  Birds fly south for the winter.

→  Los pájaros vuelan al sur en invierno.

Note:  Irregular verb: fly → flew → flown.

FOOD   /fuːd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la comida

Meaning:  what people and animals eat

Example:  The food at that restaurant is delicious.

→  La comida de ese restaurante está deliciosa.

FOOT   /fʊt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el pie

Meaning:  the part of the body at the end of the leg

Example:  He hurt his foot playing soccer.

→  Se lastimó el pie jugando al fútbol.

Common:  on foot

Note:  Irregular plural: foot → feet.

FORTY   /ˈfɔːrti/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  cuarenta

Meaning:  the number 40

Example:  She turned forty last week.

→  Cumplió cuarenta años la semana pasada.

FOUR   /fɔːr/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  cuatro

Meaning:  the number 4

Example:  There are four seasons in a year.

→  Hay cuatro estaciones en un año.

FREE   /friː/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  libre; gratis

Meaning:  not costing money; able to do what you want

Example:  Are you free this weekend?

→  ¿Estás libre este fin de semana?

Common:  free time

FRIDAY   /ˈfraɪdeɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el viernes

Meaning:  the fifth day of the week

Example:  See you on Friday afternoon!

→  ¡Nos vemos el viernes por la tarde!

FRIEND   /frɛnd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el amigo; la amiga

Meaning:  a person you know well and like

Example:  She's my best friend from school.

→  Ella es mi mejor amiga del colegio.

Common:  make friends

FRUIT   /fruːt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la fruta

Meaning:  the sweet part of a plant that you can eat, such as an apple

Example:  Try to eat more fruit and vegetables.

→  Intenta comer más fruta y verdura.

FULL   /fʊl/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  lleno/a

Meaning:  holding as much as possible; not hungry anymore

Example:  The train was completely full this morning.

→  El tren iba completamente lleno esta mañana.

FUNNY   /ˈfʌni/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  gracioso/a; divertido/a

Meaning:  making you laugh; strange or unusual

Example:  He told a really funny joke at dinner.

→  Contó un chiste muy gracioso en la cena.

GAME   /ɡeɪm/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el juego; el partido

Meaning:  an activity with rules that people play for fun; a sports match

Example:  The kids are playing a board game.

→  Los niños están jugando a un juego de mesa.

GARDEN   /ˈɡɑːrdən/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el jardín

Meaning:  an area of land next to a house where plants are grown

Example:  We have breakfast in the garden every summer.

→  Desayunamos en el jardín cada verano.

GET   /ɡɛt/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  conseguir; obtener; llegar

Meaning:  to obtain, receive, or become something

Example:  I need to get a new phone soon.

→  Necesito conseguir un móvil nuevo pronto.

Common:  get home • get up

Note:  Irregular: get → got → gotten/got. One of the most common English verbs, with many meanings.

GIFT   /ɡɪft/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el regalo

Meaning:  something you give to someone, usually for a special occasion

Example:  Thank you so much for the gift!

→  ¡Muchísimas gracias por el regalo!

GIRL   /ɡɜːrl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la niña; la chica

Meaning:  a female child or young woman

Example:  The girl is reading a book on the bench.

→  La niña está leyendo un libro en el banco.

GIVE   /ɡɪv/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  dar

Meaning:  to hand something to someone else

Example:  Can you give me a hand with this box?

→  ¿Puedes darme una mano con esta caja?

Common:  give up

Note:  Irregular: give → gave → given.

GO   /ɡoʊ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  ir

Meaning:  to move from one place to another

Example:  We go to the gym three times a week.

→  Vamos al gimnasio tres veces por semana.

Common:  go out • go home

Note:  Irregular: go → went → gone.

GOOD   /ɡʊd/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  bueno/a

Meaning:  having positive qualities; pleasant or enjoyable

Example:  This is really good coffee.

→  Este café está muy bueno.

Common:  good morning • good luck

Note:  Irregular comparative/superlative: good → better → best.

GOODBYE   /ˌɡʊdˈbaɪ/  •  exclamation  •  A1

Spanish:  adiós

Meaning:  used when leaving someone or ending a conversation

Example:  She waved goodbye at the station.

→  Ella se despidió con la mano en la estación.

GRAY   /ɡreɪ/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  gris

Meaning:  having a color between black and white

Example:  The sky turned gray before the storm.

→  El cielo se volvió gris antes de la tormenta.

Note:  British spelling: "grey".

GREEN   /ɡriːn/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  verde

Meaning:  having the color of grass or leaves

Example:  She was wearing a green dress.

→  Ella llevaba un vestido verde.

HAIR   /hɛr/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el pelo; el cabello

Meaning:  the thin threads that grow on the head and body

Example:  He has short brown hair.

→  Él tiene el pelo corto y castaño.

HAND   /hænd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la mano

Meaning:  the part of the body at the end of the arm

Example:  She held her daughter's hand crossing the street.

→  Sostuvo la mano de su hija al cruzar la calle.

Common:  shake hands

HAPPY   /ˈhæpi/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  feliz; contento/a

Meaning:  feeling or showing pleasure or satisfaction

Example:  She looked really happy at her graduation.

→  Ella se veía muy feliz en su graduación.

HAT   /hæt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el sombrero; la gorra

Meaning:  a piece of clothing worn on the head

Example:  He always wears a hat in the sun.

→  Él siempre lleva sombrero cuando hace sol.

Note:  "Gorra" refers to a cap; "sombrero" to a wider-brimmed hat.

HAVE   /hæv/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  tener

Meaning:  to possess, own, or experience something

Example:  Do you have any brothers or sisters?

→  ¿Tienes hermanos o hermanas?

Common:  have breakfast • have fun

Note:  Irregular: have → had → had.

HEAD   /hɛd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la cabeza

Meaning:  the top part of the body that contains the brain, eyes, and mouth

Example:  I have a terrible headache — my head really hurts.

→  Tengo un dolor de cabeza terrible; me duele mucho la cabeza.

HEAVY   /ˈhɛvi/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  pesado/a

Meaning:  weighing a lot; difficult to lift or carry

Example:  This suitcase is really heavy.

→  Esta maleta pesa mucho.

HELLO   /həˈloʊ/  •  exclamation  •  A1

Spanish:  hola

Meaning:  used as a greeting

Example:  Hello! It's nice to meet you.

→  ¡Hola! Encantado de conocerte.

HELP   /hɛlp/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  ayudar; la ayuda

Meaning:  to make something easier for someone by doing part of the work

Example:  Can you help me carry these bags?

→  ¿Puedes ayudarme a llevar estas bolsas?

HERE   /hɪr/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  aquí; acá

Meaning:  in, at, or to this place

Example:  Come here and look at this!

→  ¡Ven aquí y mira esto!

HOLIDAY   /ˈhɒlɪdeɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  las vacaciones; el día festivo

Meaning:  a period of rest from work or school; a special day, like a national celebration

Example:  We're going on holiday to Italy next month.

→  Nos vamos de vacaciones a Italia el mes que viene.

Common:  go on holiday

Note:  American English usually says "vacation" for a trip and "holiday" for a festive day.

HOME   /hoʊm/  •  noun; adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  el hogar; la casa; a casa

Meaning:  the place where you live; to or at the place where you live

Example:  I usually get home around six.

→  Normalmente llego a casa sobre las seis.

Common:  go home • at home

HOMEWORK   /ˈhoʊmwɜːrk/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  los deberes; la tarea

Meaning:  school work that students do at home

Example:  I still have to finish my homework tonight.

→  Todavía tengo que terminar los deberes esta noche.

Common:  do homework

HOPE   /hoʊp/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  esperar; la esperanza

Meaning:  to want something to happen or be true

Example:  I hope you feel better soon.

→  Espero que te sientas mejor pronto.

Note:  Don't confuse with "wait" (esperar also means to wait) — context makes the meaning clear.

HOSPITAL   /ˈhɒspɪtəl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el hospital

Meaning:  a place where sick or injured people are treated

Example:  He was taken to the hospital after the accident.

→  Lo llevaron al hospital después del accidente.

HOT   /hɒt/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  caliente; caluroso/a

Meaning:  having a high temperature

Example:  Be careful, the soup is very hot.

→  Ten cuidado, la sopa está muy caliente.

Note:  Use "caluroso" for hot weather and "caliente" for hot objects or food.

HOTEL   /hoʊˈtɛl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el hotel

Meaning:  a place where travelers pay to sleep and stay

Example:  We booked a hotel near the beach.

→  Reservamos un hotel cerca de la playa.

HOUR   /aʊər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la hora

Meaning:  a period of sixty minutes

Example:  The meeting lasted about an hour.

→  La reunión duró aproximadamente una hora.

Note:  The "h" is silent in English.

HOUSE   /haʊs/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la casa

Meaning:  a building where people live

Example:  They bought a house near the park.

→  Compraron una casa cerca del parque.

HUNDRED   /ˈhʌndrəd/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  cien; ciento

Meaning:  the number 100

Example:  There were about a hundred guests at the wedding.

→  Había unos cien invitados en la boda.

HUNGRY   /ˈhʌŋɡri/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  hambriento/a

Meaning:  feeling that you want or need to eat

Example:  I'm really hungry — let's eat now.

→  Tengo mucha hambre; comamos ya.

Note:  More natural in Spanish as "tener hambre" (to have hunger) than as an adjective.

HUSBAND   /ˈhʌzbənd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el esposo; el marido

Meaning:  the man a woman is married to

Example:  Her husband works as an engineer.

→  Su esposo trabaja como ingeniero.

ICE   /aɪs/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el hielo

Meaning:  frozen water

Example:  Would you like some ice in your drink?

→  ¿Quieres hielo en tu bebida?

IMPORTANT   /ɪmˈpɔːrtənt/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  importante

Meaning:  having great value, meaning, or effect

Example:  It's important to drink enough water.

→  Es importante beber suficiente agua.

IN   /ɪn/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  en

Meaning:  used to show location inside something, or a period of time

Example:  The keys are in my bag.

→  Las llaves están en mi bolso.

INTERESTING   /ˈɪntrəstɪŋ/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  interesante

Meaning:  holding your attention; not boring

Example:  That was a really interesting documentary.

→  Ese documental fue muy interesante.

INTERNET   /ˈɪntərnɛt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  internet

Meaning:  the global network used to connect computers and share information

Example:  I don't have internet at home right now.

→  Ahora mismo no tengo internet en casa.

Note:  In Spanish "internet" is usually used without an article and often not capitalized.

JANUARY   /ˈdʒænjuɛri/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  enero

Meaning:  the first month of the year

Example:  School starts again in January.

→  Las clases empiezan de nuevo en enero.

JOB   /dʒɒb/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el trabajo; el empleo

Meaning:  the work someone does regularly to earn money

Example:  She found a new job last month.

→  Ella encontró un nuevo trabajo el mes pasado.

JULY   /dʒʊˈlaɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  julio

Meaning:  the seventh month of the year

Example:  It's usually very hot in July.

→  Normalmente hace mucho calor en julio.

JUNE   /dʒuːn/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  junio

Meaning:  the sixth month of the year

Example:  We're getting married in June.

→  Nos casamos en junio.

KEY   /kiː/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la llave

Meaning:  a small metal object used to open a lock

Example:  I forgot my house key at the office.

→  Me olvidé la llave de casa en la oficina.

KITCHEN   /ˈkɪtʃɪn/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la cocina

Meaning:  a room used for cooking food

Example:  He was cooking dinner in the kitchen.

→  Él estaba cocinando la cena en la cocina.

KNOW   /noʊ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  saber; conocer

Meaning:  to have information or knowledge about something; to be familiar with someone or something

Example:  I don't know her name.

→  No sé su nombre.

Note:  Use "saber" for facts and information, and "conocer" for people and places. Irregular: know → knew → known.

LATER   /ˈleɪtər/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  más tarde; luego

Meaning:  after the present time; afterward

Example:  I'll call you later this evening.

→  Te llamaré más tarde esta noche.

Common:  see you later

LAUGH   /læf/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  reír; la risa

Meaning:  to make sounds and movements that show you find something funny

Example:  The whole class laughed at his joke.

→  Toda la clase se rio de su broma.

LEARN   /lɜːrn/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  aprender

Meaning:  to gain knowledge or a skill through study or experience

Example:  I'm learning Spanish this year.

→  Este año estoy aprendiendo español.

LEAVE   /liːv/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  salir; irse; dejar

Meaning:  to go away from a place; to let something stay where it is

Example:  What time does the bus leave?

→  ¿A qué hora sale el autobús?

Note:  Irregular: leave → left → left.

LEFT   /lɛft/  •  adjective; adverb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  izquierdo/a; a la izquierda

Meaning:  on or toward the side opposite to the right

Example:  Turn left at the next corner.

→  Gira a la izquierda en la próxima esquina.

Common:  turn left

LEG   /lɛɡ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la pierna

Meaning:  one of the two long parts of the body used for walking

Example:  She broke her leg while skiing.

→  Se rompió la pierna esquiando.

LEND   /lɛnd/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  prestar

Meaning:  to give something to someone for a period of time, expecting it back

Example:  Could you lend me some money until Friday?

→  ¿Podrías prestarme dinero hasta el viernes?

Note:  Irregular: lend → lent → lent. Compare with "borrow" (the opposite action).

LESSON   /ˈlɛsən/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la lección; la clase

Meaning:  a period of time for teaching or learning something

Example:  I have a guitar lesson every Tuesday.

→  Tengo clase de guitarra todos los martes.

LETTER   /ˈlɛtər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la carta; la letra

Meaning:  a written message sent to someone; a symbol used in writing, like A or B

Example:  She wrote a letter to her grandmother.

→  Ella le escribió una carta a su abuela.

LIGHT   /laɪt/  •  noun; adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  la luz; ligero/a; claro/a

Meaning:  the natural or artificial brightness that lets us see; not heavy; pale in color

Example:  Please turn on the light — it's dark in here.

→  Enciende la luz, por favor, aquí está oscuro.

Common:  turn on the light

LIKE   /laɪk/  •  verb; preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  gustar; como

Meaning:  to find something pleasant or enjoyable; similar to

Example:  I really like this song.

→  Me gusta mucho esta canción.

Note:  With "gustar", the thing liked is the grammatical subject: "I like it" = "Me gusta."

LISTEN   /ˈlɪsən/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  escuchar

Meaning:  to pay attention in order to hear something

Example:  I like to listen to music while I work.

→  Me gusta escuchar música mientras trabajo.

Common:  listen to

Note:  Always followed by "to" before the thing you hear: listen to music.

LIVE   /lɪv/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  vivir

Meaning:  to have your home in a particular place

Example:  They live in a small apartment downtown.

→  Viven en un apartamento pequeño en el centro.

LONG   /lɔːŋ/  •  adjective; adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  largo/a

Meaning:  measuring a great distance or time from one end to the other

Example:  It was a long flight from London to Tokyo.

→  Fue un vuelo largo de Londres a Tokio.

Common:  how long

LOOK   /lʊk/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  mirar; parecer

Meaning:  to turn your eyes toward something; to seem or appear

Example:  Look at that beautiful sunset!

→  ¡Mira esa puesta de sol tan bonita!

Common:  look for • look like

Note:  "Look for" means to search; "look at" means to direct your eyes at something.

LOSE   /luːz/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  perder

Meaning:  to stop having something; to not win a game or competition

Example:  I always lose my umbrella.

→  Siempre pierdo el paraguas.

Note:  Irregular: lose → lost → lost. Don't confuse with "loose" (suelto/a), a different word.

LOUD   /laʊd/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  ruidoso/a; alto/a (volumen)

Meaning:  producing a lot of noise; not quiet

Example:  The music was too loud to hear each other.

→  La música estaba demasiado alta para oírnos.

LOVE   /lʌv/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  amar; encantar; el amor

Meaning:  to have very strong positive feelings for someone or something

Example:  I love spending time with my family.

→  Me encanta pasar tiempo con mi familia.

Common:  fall in love

LUNCH   /lʌntʃ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el almuerzo; la comida

Meaning:  a meal eaten in the middle of the day

Example:  Let's have lunch together tomorrow.

→  Almorcemos juntos mañana.

Common:  have lunch

MAKE   /meɪk/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  hacer

Meaning:  to create or produce something

Example:  She makes really good pizza.

→  Ella hace una pizza muy buena.

Common:  make a decision • make friends

Note:  Irregular: make → made → made. Compare with "do": make usually creates something new; do refers to activities/tasks.

MAN   /mæn/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el hombre

Meaning:  an adult male person

Example:  There's a man waiting outside.

→  Hay un hombre esperando afuera.

Note:  Irregular plural: man → men.

MARCH   /mɑːrtʃ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  marzo

Meaning:  the third month of the year

Example:  Spring usually begins in late March.

→  La primavera suele empezar a finales de marzo.

MARKET   /ˈmɑːrkɪt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el mercado

Meaning:  a place where people buy and sell goods, often outdoors

Example:  We buy fresh vegetables at the market.

→  Compramos verduras frescas en el mercado.

MAY   /meɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  mayo

Meaning:  the fifth month of the year

Example:  The flowers bloom in May.

→  Las flores florecen en mayo.

MEAT   /miːt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la carne

Meaning:  the flesh of animals eaten as food

Example:  She doesn't eat meat or fish.

→  Ella no come carne ni pescado.

MEET   /miːt/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  conocer; reunirse con

Meaning:  to see and speak to someone for the first time, or by arrangement

Example:  Nice to meet you!

→  ¡Encantado de conocerte!

Note:  Irregular: meet → met → met. Use "conocer" for meeting someone for the first time and "reunirse con" for meeting by arrangement.

MENU   /ˈmɛnjuː/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el menú; la carta

Meaning:  a list of food and drinks available in a restaurant

Example:  Can I see the menu, please?

→  ¿Puedo ver la carta, por favor?

MILK   /mɪlk/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la leche

Meaning:  a white liquid produced by cows and other animals, used as food or drink

Example:  Do you take milk in your coffee?

→  ¿Tomas leche en el café?

MINUTE   /ˈmɪnɪt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el minuto

Meaning:  a period of sixty seconds

Example:  Wait a minute, I'm almost ready.

→  Espera un minuto, ya casi estoy listo.

MOMENT   /ˈmoʊmənt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el momento

Meaning:  a very short period of time

Example:  Could you wait for a moment, please?

→  ¿Podrías esperar un momento, por favor?

MONDAY   /ˈmʌndeɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el lunes

Meaning:  the first day of the working week

Example:  I hate getting up early on Mondays.

→  Odio levantarme temprano los lunes.

MONEY   /ˈmʌni/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el dinero

Meaning:  coins and notes used to buy things

Example:  They saved money for their trip to Peru.

→  Ahorraron dinero para su viaje a Perú.

MONTH   /mʌnθ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el mes

Meaning:  one of the twelve periods that make up a year

Example:  We're moving next month.

→  Nos mudamos el mes que viene.

MOON   /muːn/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la luna

Meaning:  the object that moves around the Earth and shines at night

Example:  The moon looks beautiful tonight.

→  La luna se ve preciosa esta noche.

MORNING   /ˈmɔːrnɪŋ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la mañana

Meaning:  the early part of the day, before noon

Example:  I always go for a run in the morning.

→  Siempre salgo a correr por la mañana.

Common:  good morning • this morning

MOTHER   /ˈmʌðər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la madre

Meaning:  a woman in relation to her child

Example:  My mother calls me every Sunday.

→  Mi madre me llama todos los domingos.

MOUNTAIN   /ˈmaʊntən/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la montaña

Meaning:  a very high area of land, higher than a hill

Example:  We hiked up the mountain all morning.

→  Subimos la montaña caminando toda la mañana.

MOUTH   /maʊθ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la boca

Meaning:  the part of the face used for eating and speaking

Example:  Don't talk with food in your mouth.

→  No hables con comida en la boca.

MUSIC   /ˈmjuːzɪk/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la música

Meaning:  sounds arranged to be pleasant to listen to, often with instruments

Example:  She listens to music while she studies.

→  Ella escucha música mientras estudia.

NAME   /neɪm/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  el nombre; nombrar

Meaning:  the word used to identify a person, place, or thing

Example:  What's your name?

→  ¿Cómo te llamas?

Common:  first name • last name

NEAR   /nɪr/  •  preposition; adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  cerca de

Meaning:  not far from a place in distance or time

Example:  The hotel is near the train station.

→  El hotel está cerca de la estación de tren.

NEED   /niːd/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  necesitar; la necesidad

Meaning:  to require something because it is necessary

Example:  I need more time to finish this project.

→  Necesito más tiempo para terminar este proyecto.

NEVER   /ˈnɛvər/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  nunca; jamás

Meaning:  at no time; not ever

Example:  I've never been to Japan.

→  Nunca he estado en Japón.

NEW   /nuː/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  nuevo/a

Meaning:  recently made, bought, or created

Example:  I bought a new phone last week.

→  Compré un móvil nuevo la semana pasada.

NICE   /naɪs/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  agradable; simpático/a; bonito/a

Meaning:  pleasant, kind, or good

Example:  It was so nice to see you again.

→  Fue muy agradable volver a verte.

Common:  nice to meet you

NIGHT   /naɪt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la noche

Meaning:  the time between evening and morning, when it is dark

Example:  We stayed up late talking last night.

→  Anoche nos quedamos hablando hasta tarde.

Common:  good night • last night

NINE   /naɪn/  •  number  •  A1

Spanish:  nueve

Meaning:  the number 9

Example:  The store closes at nine.

→  La tienda cierra a las nueve.

NO   /noʊ/  •  adverb; determiner  •  A1

Spanish:  no; ningún/ninguna

Meaning:  used to give a negative answer; not any

Example:  No, thank you, I'm not hungry.

→  No, gracias, no tengo hambre.

NOSE   /noʊz/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la nariz

Meaning:  the part of the face used for breathing and smelling

Example:  My nose is blocked because of my cold.

→  Tengo la nariz tapada por el resfriado.

NOVEMBER   /noʊˈvɛmbər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  noviembre

Meaning:  the eleventh month of the year

Example:  It starts getting cold in November.

→  Empieza a hacer frío en noviembre.

NOW   /naʊ/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  ahora

Meaning:  at the present time

Example:  I have to leave now or I'll be late.

→  Tengo que irme ahora o llegaré tarde.

Common:  right now

NUMBER   /ˈnʌmbər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el número

Meaning:  a word or symbol used for counting or showing an amount

Example:  Can I have your phone number?

→  ¿Me das tu número de teléfono?

Common:  phone number

NURSE   /nɜːrs/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el enfermero; la enfermera

Meaning:  a person trained to care for sick people, usually in a hospital

Example:  The nurse checked his blood pressure.

→  La enfermera le tomó la presión.

OCTOBER   /ɒkˈtoʊbər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  octubre

Meaning:  the tenth month of the year

Example:  The leaves start falling in October.

→  Las hojas empiezan a caer en octubre.

OFFICE   /ˈɒfɪs/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la oficina

Meaning:  a room or building where people do administrative work

Example:  She works in an office downtown.

→  Ella trabaja en una oficina en el centro.

OFTEN   /ˈɔːfən/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  a menudo; frecuentemente

Meaning:  many times; frequently

Example:  We often go for a walk after dinner.

→  A menudo salimos a caminar después de cenar.

Common:  how often

OLD   /oʊld/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  viejo/a; mayor; antiguo/a

Meaning:  having lived or existed for a long time; not young or new

Example:  My grandfather is eighty years old.

→  Mi abuelo tiene ochenta años.

Common:  how old

Note:  For people, "mayor" often sounds more polite than "viejo".

ON   /ɒn/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  en; sobre

Meaning:  touching the surface of something; used with days and dates

Example:  The book is on the table.

→  El libro está sobre la mesa.

ONE   /wʌn/  •  number; pronoun  •  A1

Spanish:  uno; una

Meaning:  the number 1; used to refer to a single thing or person already mentioned

Example:  I only need one ticket.

→  Solo necesito un billete.

OPEN   /ˈoʊpən/  •  verb; adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  abrir; abierto/a

Meaning:  to move something so it is no longer closed; not closed

Example:  Can you open the window, please?

→  ¿Puedes abrir la ventana, por favor?

OR   /ɔːr/  •  conjunction  •  A1

Spanish:  o

Meaning:  used to connect alternatives or choices

Example:  Would you like tea or coffee?

→  ¿Quieres té o café?

Note:  Changes to "u" before a word starting with "o" (siete u ocho).

ORANGE   /ˈɒrɪndʒ/  •  noun; adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  la naranja; naranja (color)

Meaning:  a round citrus fruit; having the color between red and yellow

Example:  I squeezed some fresh orange juice.

→  Exprimí un poco de zumo de naranja fresco.

Common:  orange juice

OVER   /ˈoʊvər/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  sobre; por encima de

Meaning:  above and covering something, often not touching it

Example:  A plane flew over the city.

→  Un avión voló por encima de la ciudad.

PAPER   /ˈpeɪpər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el papel

Meaning:  thin material used for writing, printing, or wrapping

Example:  I need a piece of paper to write this down.

→  Necesito un trozo de papel para anotar esto.

PARENT   /ˈpɛrənt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el padre; la madre; (plural) los padres

Meaning:  a mother or father

Example:  Both of my parents are teachers.

→  Mis dos padres son profesores.

PARK   /pɑːrk/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  el parque; aparcar/estacionar

Meaning:  a public area with grass and trees; to leave a vehicle somewhere

Example:  We had a picnic in the park.

→  Hicimos un pícnic en el parque.

Note:  "Aparcar" is used in Spain, "estacionar" in Latin America.

PAY   /peɪ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  pagar

Meaning:  to give money for something you buy or a service you receive

Example:  Can I pay by card?

→  ¿Puedo pagar con tarjeta?

Note:  Irregular: pay → paid → paid.

PEN   /pɛn/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el bolígrafo; la pluma

Meaning:  a tool used for writing with ink

Example:  Can I borrow your pen for a second?

→  ¿Me prestas tu bolígrafo un segundo?

PENCIL   /ˈpɛnsəl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el lápiz

Meaning:  a tool used for writing or drawing, usually with a wooden case and graphite

Example:  The teacher asked us to bring a pencil.

→  La profesora nos pidió que trajéramos un lápiz.

PEOPLE   /ˈpiːpəl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la gente; las personas

Meaning:  more than one person

Example:  There were a lot of people at the concert.

→  Había mucha gente en el concierto.

Note:  "Gente" is grammatically singular in Spanish: "la gente ES", not "la gente SON".

PERSON   /ˈpɜːrsən/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la persona

Meaning:  a human being

Example:  She's a very kind person.

→  Ella es una persona muy amable.

Note:  "Persona" is always feminine in Spanish, even when referring to a man.

PHONE   /foʊn/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  el teléfono; el móvil; llamar

Meaning:  a device used to talk to people who are not near you

Example:  I left my phone at home this morning.

→  Me dejé el móvil en casa esta mañana.

Common:  phone number • on the phone

PHOTO   /ˈfoʊtoʊ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la foto

Meaning:  a picture made with a camera

Example:  Can you take a photo of us?

→  ¿Puedes hacernos una foto?

Common:  take a photo

PINK   /pɪŋk/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  rosa; rosado/a

Meaning:  having a pale red color

Example:  She painted her room pink.

→  Pintó su habitación de rosa.

PLATE   /pleɪt/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el plato

Meaning:  a flat dish used for eating food

Example:  Please put your plate in the sink.

→  Pon tu plato en el fregadero, por favor.

PLAY   /pleɪ/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  jugar; tocar (un instrumento); la obra de teatro

Meaning:  to take part in a game or sport; to perform music; a piece of theater

Example:  The kids love to play in the garden.

→  A los niños les encanta jugar en el jardín.

Common:  play a game • play music

Note:  "Jugar" is for games/sports; "tocar" is for musical instruments.

PLEASE   /pliːz/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  por favor

Meaning:  used to make a request more polite

Example:  Could you help me, please?

→  ¿Podrías ayudarme, por favor?

POLICE   /pəˈliːs/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la policía

Meaning:  the official organization responsible for enforcing the law

Example:  They called the police after the accident.

→  Llamaron a la policía después del accidente.

POOR   /pʊr/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  pobre

Meaning:  having very little money; deserving sympathy

Example:  They grew up poor but happy.

→  Crecieron pobres pero felices.

PRESENT   /ˈprɛzənt/  •  noun; adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  el regalo; presente

Meaning:  a gift; existing or happening now

Example:  Thank you for the birthday present.

→  Gracias por el regalo de cumpleaños.

PURPLE   /ˈpɜːrpəl/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  morado/a; púrpura

Meaning:  having a color between red and blue

Example:  She was wearing a purple scarf.

→  Llevaba una bufanda morada.

PUT   /pʊt/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  poner

Meaning:  to move something into a particular place or position

Example:  Put your bags over there, please.

→  Pon tus bolsas allí, por favor.

Common:  put on • put away

Note:  Irregular: put → put → put (no change).

QUESTION   /ˈkwɛstʃən/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la pregunta; la cuestión

Meaning:  something you ask to get information

Example:  Do you have any questions?

→  ¿Tenéis alguna pregunta?

Common:  ask a question

QUIET   /ˈkwaɪət/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  tranquilo/a; silencioso/a

Meaning:  making little or no noise; calm

Example:  It's very quiet in the library.

→  Está muy silenciosa la biblioteca.

Note:  Don't confuse with "quite" (bastante), a completely different word.

RADIO   /ˈreɪdioʊ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la radio

Meaning:  a device used to listen to broadcast programs and music

Example:  I listen to the radio while driving.

→  Escucho la radio mientras conduzco.

RAIN   /reɪn/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  la lluvia; llover

Meaning:  water falling from clouds; to fall as rain

Example:  Take an umbrella — it might rain later.

→  Llévate un paraguas, puede que llueva más tarde.

READ   /riːd/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  leer

Meaning:  to look at written words and understand their meaning

Example:  I read two books last month.

→  Leí dos libros el mes pasado.

Note:  Irregular: read → read → read (spelled the same, pronounced differently in the past: /rɛd/).

RED   /rɛd/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  rojo/a

Meaning:  having the color of blood or fire

Example:  She was wearing a bright red dress.

→  Llevaba un vestido rojo brillante.

RELAX   /rɪˈlæks/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  relajarse; descansar

Meaning:  to rest and stop worrying or working

Example:  I just want to relax at home this weekend.

→  Solo quiero relajarme en casa este fin de semana.

REST   /rɛst/  •  noun; verb  •  A1

Spanish:  el descanso; descansar; el resto

Meaning:  a period of relaxing or sleeping; to relax or sleep; what remains

Example:  You should rest after such a long trip.

RESTAURANT   /ˈrɛstərɒnt/  •  noun  •  A1

RICE   /raɪs/  •  noun  •  A1

RICH   /rɪtʃ/  •  adjective  •  A1

RIGHT   /raɪt/  •  adjective; adverb; noun  •  A1

RUN   /rʌn/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

SAD   /sæd/  •  adjective  •  A1

SCHOOL   /skuːl/  •  noun  •  A1

SHIRT   /ʃɜːrt/  •  noun  •  A1

SHORT   /ʃɔːrt/  •  adjective  •  A1

SISTER   /ˈsɪstər/  •  noun  •  A1

SIX   /sɪks/  •  number  •  A1

SLOW   /sloʊ/  •  adjective  •  A1

TEN   /tɛn/  •  number  •  A1

TOO   /tuː/  •  adverb  •  A1

TOOTH   /tuːθ/  •  noun  •  A1

UGLY   /ˈʌɡli/  •  adjective  •  A1

UNDER   /ˈʌndər/  •  preposition  •  A1
