→  El gato está durmiendo debajo de la mesa.

UP   /ʌp/  •  adverb; preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  arriba; hacia arriba

Meaning:  toward a higher place or position

Example:  She ran up the stairs.

→  Ella subió corriendo las escaleras.

Common:  stand up • wake up

USE   /juːz/ (verb); /juːs/ (noun)  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  usar; utilizar; el uso

Meaning:  to do something with an object for a purpose

Example:  Can I use your phone for a minute?

→  ¿Puedo usar tu móvil un momento?

USUALLY   /ˈjuːʒuəli/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  normalmente; generalmente

Meaning:  most of the time; on most occasions

Example:  I usually get up at seven.

→  Normalmente me levanto a las siete.

VEGETABLE   /ˈvɛdʒtəbəl/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la verdura; el vegetal

Meaning:  a plant, such as a carrot or potato, eaten as food

Example:  You should eat more vegetables.

→  Deberías comer más verduras.

VERY   /ˈvɛri/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  muy

Meaning:  to a great extent; extremely

Example:  This soup is very good.

→  Esta sopa está muy buena.

VISIT   /ˈvɪzɪt/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  visitar; la visita

Meaning:  to go to see a person or place

Example:  We visited my grandparents last weekend.

→  Visitamos a mis abuelos el fin de semana pasado.

WAIT   /weɪt/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  esperar

Meaning:  to stay somewhere until something happens

Example:  I'll wait for you outside the cinema.

→  Te esperaré fuera del cine.

Common:  wait for

Note:  Followed by "for" before the person or thing you wait for.

WALK   /wɔːk/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  caminar; andar; el paseo

Meaning:  to move on foot; a trip made on foot

Example:  We walked to the market this morning.

→  Caminamos hasta el mercado esta mañana.

Common:  go for a walk

WANT   /wɒnt/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  querer

Meaning:  to have a desire for something

Example:  Do you want something to drink?

→  ¿Quieres algo de beber?

WASH   /wɒʃ/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  lavar; lavarse

Meaning:  to clean something with water and soap

Example:  Please wash your hands before dinner.

→  Lávate las manos antes de cenar, por favor.

Common:  wash the dishes

WATCH   /wɒtʃ/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  ver; mirar; el reloj (de pulsera)

Meaning:  to look at something for a period of time; a small clock worn on the wrist

Example:  We watched a movie last night.

→  Vimos una película anoche.

Common:  watch television

Note:  For TV/films/sports use "watch", but for people or static scenes use "look at".

WATER   /ˈwɔːtər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el agua

Meaning:  the clear liquid that falls as rain and fills rivers and seas

Example:  Can I have a glass of water, please?

→  ¿Me pones un vaso de agua, por favor?

Note:  "Agua" is feminine but uses "el" in the singular for pronunciation reasons: el agua, but las aguas.

WEAK   /wiːk/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  débil

Meaning:  not physically strong

Example:  He still felt weak after the flu.

→  Todavía se sentía débil después de la gripe.

Note:  Sounds identical to "week" but has a different meaning and spelling.

WEAR   /wɛr/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  llevar puesto; vestir

Meaning:  to have clothes or accessories on your body

Example:  She wears glasses for reading.

→  Ella lleva gafas para leer.

Note:  Irregular: wear → wore → worn.

WEATHER   /ˈwɛðər/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el tiempo; el clima

Meaning:  the condition of the sky and air at a certain time and place

Example:  The weather is lovely today.

→  Hace un tiempo precioso hoy.

WEDNESDAY   /ˈwɛnzdeɪ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el miércoles

Meaning:  the third day of the week

Example:  We have yoga class on Wednesdays.

→  Tenemos clase de yoga los miércoles.

WEEK   /wiːk/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la semana

Meaning:  a period of seven days

Example:  I go to the gym twice a week.

→  Voy al gimnasio dos veces por semana.

WEEKEND   /ˈwiːkɛnd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el fin de semana

Meaning:  Saturday and Sunday

Example:  What are your plans for the weekend?

→  ¿Qué planes tienes para el fin de semana?

WELL   /wɛl/  •  adverb; adjective; exclamation  •  A1

Spanish:  bien

Meaning:  in a good or satisfactory way; in good health

Example:  She speaks French very well.

→  Ella habla francés muy bien.

Common:  as well

Note:  "Well" is the adverb form of "good": She is a good singer / She sings well.

WHITE   /waɪt/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  blanco/a

Meaning:  having the color of milk or snow

Example:  He was wearing a white shirt.

→  Llevaba una camisa blanca.

WIFE   /waɪf/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la esposa

Meaning:  the woman a man is married to

Example:  His wife works at the hospital.

→  Su esposa trabaja en el hospital.

Note:  Irregular plural: wife → wives.

WIN   /wɪn/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  ganar

Meaning:  to be the best or first in a competition or game

Example:  Our team won the match yesterday.

→  Nuestro equipo ganó el partido ayer.

Note:  Irregular: win → won → won.

WIND   /wɪnd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el viento

Meaning:  a natural movement of air

Example:  The wind was really strong on the beach today.

→  El viento estaba muy fuerte en la playa hoy.

WINDOW   /ˈwɪndoʊ/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la ventana

Meaning:  an opening in a wall or vehicle, usually covered with glass

Example:  Could you open the window? It's hot in here.

→  ¿Podrías abrir la ventana? Hace calor aquí.

WITH   /wɪð/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  con

Meaning:  in the company of; using

Example:  I went to the cinema with my friends.

→  Fui al cine con mis amigos.

WITHOUT   /wɪˈðaʊt/  •  preposition  •  A1

Spanish:  sin

Meaning:  not having or using something

Example:  I can't live without coffee in the morning.

→  No puedo vivir sin café por la mañana.

WOMAN   /ˈwʊmən/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la mujer

Meaning:  an adult female person

Example:  The woman at the desk was very helpful.

→  La mujer del mostrador fue muy amable.

Note:  Irregular plural: woman → women /ˈwɪmɪn/.

WORD   /wɜːrd/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  la palabra

Meaning:  a unit of language that has meaning

Example:  I don't understand this word.

→  No entiendo esta palabra.

WORK   /wɜːrk/  •  verb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  trabajar; funcionar; el trabajo

Meaning:  to do a job; to function correctly

Example:  This machine doesn't work anymore.

→  Esta máquina ya no funciona.

Common:  go to work

Note:  Use "trabajar" for people doing a job and "funcionar" for machines and systems.

WORLD   /wɜːrld/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el mundo

Meaning:  the Earth, along with all its countries and people

Example:  She has traveled all over the world.

→  Ella ha viajado por todo el mundo.

WRITE   /raɪt/  •  verb  •  A1

Spanish:  escribir

Meaning:  to put words on paper or a screen

Example:  Can you write your name here, please?

→  ¿Puedes escribir tu nombre aquí, por favor?

Note:  Irregular: write → wrote → written.

WRONG   /rɔːŋ/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  incorrecto/a; equivocado/a

Meaning:  not correct; not what is expected or wanted

Example:  Sorry, I think you have the wrong number.

→  Perdón, creo que se ha equivocado de número.

YEAR   /jɪr/  •  noun  •  A1

Spanish:  el año

Meaning:  a period of twelve months

Example:  I started this job three years ago.

→  Empecé este trabajo hace tres años.

YELLOW   /ˈjɛloʊ/  •  adjective; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  amarillo/a

Meaning:  having the color of a lemon or the sun

Example:  She painted the kitchen yellow.

→  Pintó la cocina de amarillo.

YES   /jɛs/  •  adverb  •  A1

Spanish:  sí

Meaning:  used to give a positive answer

Example:  Yes, I'd love to come.

→  Sí, me encantaría ir.

YESTERDAY   /ˈjɛstərdeɪ/  •  adverb; noun  •  A1

Spanish:  ayer

Meaning:  on the day before today

Example:  I saw her yesterday at the market.

→  La vi ayer en el mercado.

YOUNG   /jʌŋ/  •  adjective  •  A1

Spanish:  joven

Meaning:  having lived only a short time; not old

Example:  They have two young children.

→  Tienen dos hijos pequeños.

# A2 Vocabulary

Elementary, everyday vocabulary — 352 words, alphabetical.

ABILITY   /əˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la capacidad; la habilidad

Meaning:  the power or skill to do something

Example:  She has a real ability to make people laugh.

→  Ella tiene una verdadera habilidad para hacer reír a la gente.

Common:  the ability to do something

ACCEPT   /əkˈsɛpt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  aceptar

Meaning:  to agree to take something, or to say yes to an offer

Example:  She accepted the job offer right away.

→  Ella aceptó la oferta de trabajo enseguida.

ACCIDENT   /ˈæksɪdənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el accidente

Meaning:  an unexpected event that causes damage or injury

Example:  There was a car accident on the highway this morning.

→  Hubo un accidente de coche en la autopista esta mañana.

Common:  by accident

ADVERTISE   /ˈædvərtaɪz/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  anunciar; hacer publicidad de

Meaning:  to tell people about a product or service to persuade them to buy it

Example:  They advertised the concert on social media.

→  Anunciaron el concierto en las redes sociales.

ADVERTISEMENT   /ˌædvərˈtaɪzmənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el anuncio

Meaning:  a public notice that promotes a product, service, or event

Example:  I saw an advertisement for cheap flights to Rome.

→  Vi un anuncio de vuelos baratos a Roma.

Note:  Often shortened to "ad" in speech.

AGREE   /əˈɡriː/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  estar de acuerdo; acordar

Meaning:  to have the same opinion as someone else, or to accept a plan

Example:  I agree with you completely.

→  Estoy completamente de acuerdo contigo.

Common:  agree with • agree to

ALLOW   /əˈlaʊ/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  permitir

Meaning:  to let someone do something, or let something happen

Example:  They don't allow pets in this building.

→  No permiten mascotas en este edificio.

AMBULANCE   /ˈæmbjələns/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la ambulancia

Meaning:  a vehicle used to take sick or injured people to the hospital

Example:  They called an ambulance right after the fall.

→  Llamaron a una ambulancia justo después de la caída.

Common:  call an ambulance

AMOUNT   /əˈmaʊnt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la cantidad

Meaning:  a quantity of something, especially money

Example:  They spent a huge amount of money on the wedding.

→  Gastaron una cantidad enorme de dinero en la boda.

Common:  a large amount of

ANGRY   /ˈæŋɡri/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  enfadado/a; enojado/a

Meaning:  feeling strong displeasure toward someone or something

Example:  He got really angry when the flight was delayed.

→  Se enfadó mucho cuando el vuelo se retrasó.

Common:  angry at/with

ANNOYED   /əˈnɔɪd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  molesto/a; fastidiado/a

Meaning:  feeling slightly angry about something

Example:  She was annoyed that he was late again.

→  Estaba molesta porque él llegó tarde otra vez.

APARTMENT   /əˈpɑːrtmənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el apartamento; el piso (Spain)

Meaning:  a set of rooms for living in, usually on one floor of a building

Example:  They just moved into a new apartment downtown.

→  Se acaban de mudar a un apartamento nuevo en el centro.

APPEARANCE   /əˈpɪrəns/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la apariencia; el aspecto

Meaning:  the way someone or something looks

Example:  He changed his appearance a lot after the haircut.

→  Cambió mucho de aspecto después del corte de pelo.

APPLICATION   /ˌæplɪˈkeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la solicitud; la aplicación

Meaning:  a formal request for a job, course, or program; a computer program

Example:  I submitted my application for the scholarship yesterday.

→  Envié mi solicitud para la beca ayer.

Common:  job application

Note:  Often shortened to "app" when referring to phone software.

APPOINTMENT   /əˈpɔɪntmənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la cita

Meaning:  an arrangement to meet someone or see a professional at a specific time

Example:  I have a dentist appointment on Thursday.

→  Tengo una cita con el dentista el jueves.

Common:  make an appointment

ARRANGE   /əˈreɪndʒ/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  organizar; arreglar

Meaning:  to plan or organize something in advance

Example:  We arranged to meet at the café at noon.

→  Organizamos vernos en el café al mediodía.

Common:  arrange a meeting

ARTICLE   /ˈɑːrtɪkəl/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el artículo

Meaning:  a piece of writing in a newspaper, magazine, or website

Example:  I read an interesting article about climate change.

→  Leí un artículo interesante sobre el cambio climático.

ASHAMED   /əˈʃeɪmd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  avergonzado/a

Meaning:  feeling embarrassed or guilty about something you did

Example:  He felt ashamed after losing his temper.

→  Se sintió avergonzado después de perder los estribos.

ATTITUDE   /ˈætɪtuːd/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la actitud

Meaning:  the way someone thinks or feels about something, shown in their behavior

Example:  I really like her positive attitude toward life.

→  Me encanta su actitud positiva ante la vida.

AUDIENCE   /ˈɔːdiəns/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el público; la audiencia

Meaning:  the people watching or listening to a performance or event

Example:  The audience clapped for almost a minute.

→  El público aplaudió durante casi un minuto.

AVERAGE   /ˈævərɪdʒ/  •  adjective; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  promedio; medio/a; el promedio

Meaning:  typical or usual; the result of adding numbers and dividing by how many there are

Example:  On average, I sleep about seven hours a night.

→  De media, duermo unas siete horas por noche.

Common:  on average

BARN   /bɑːrn/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el granero

Meaning:  a large farm building used to store crops or keep animals

Example:  The horses spend the night in the barn.

→  Los caballos pasan la noche en el granero.

BATHROOM   /ˈbæθruːm/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el baño; el cuarto de baño

Meaning:  a room with a toilet, sink, and usually a shower or bath

Example:  The bathroom is at the end of the hallway.

→  El baño está al final del pasillo.

BATTERY   /ˈbætəri/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la pila; la batería

Meaning:  a device that stores electricity to power something

Example:  My phone battery is almost dead.

→  La batería de mi móvil está casi agotada.

Note:  "Pila" is used for small batteries (remote controls); "batería" for larger ones (cars, phones).

BEDROOM   /ˈbɛdruːm/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el dormitorio; la habitación

Meaning:  a room used for sleeping

Example:  Their apartment has two bedrooms.

→  Su apartamento tiene dos dormitorios.

BEHAVIOR   /bɪˈheɪvjər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el comportamiento

Meaning:  the way a person or animal acts

Example:  The teacher praised the students' good behavior.

→  La profesora elogió el buen comportamiento de los estudiantes.

Note:  British spelling: "behaviour".

BELIEF   /bɪˈliːf/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la creencia

Meaning:  a strong feeling that something is true or real

Example:  It's her belief that hard work always pays off.

→  Es su creencia que el esfuerzo siempre da sus frutos.

BORDER   /ˈbɔːrdər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la frontera

Meaning:  the line dividing two countries or areas

Example:  The town is right on the border between the two countries.

→  El pueblo está justo en la frontera entre los dos países.

BRAND   /brænd/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la marca

Meaning:  a product made by a particular company, with its own name

Example:  Which brand of coffee do you usually buy?

→  ¿Qué marca de café sueles comprar?

BRAVE   /breɪv/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  valiente

Meaning:  willing to do something dangerous or difficult without showing fear

Example:  It was brave of her to speak in front of so many people.

→  Fue muy valiente por su parte hablar delante de tanta gente.

BROADCAST   /ˈbrɔːdkæst/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  emitir; transmitir; la emisión

Meaning:  to send out a program on television or radio

Example:  The match will be broadcast live tonight.

→  El partido se emitirá en directo esta noche.

Note:  Irregular: broadcast → broadcast → broadcast (no change).

BROCHURE   /broʊˈʃʊr/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el folleto

Meaning:  a small booklet or leaflet giving information about a product or place

Example:  We picked up a brochure about local hiking trails.

→  Cogimos un folleto sobre las rutas de senderismo locales.

BUSINESS   /ˈbɪznəs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el negocio; la empresa

Meaning:  the activity of buying and selling goods or services; a company

Example:  She started her own business two years ago.

→  Ella empezó su propio negocio hace dos años.

CANCEL   /ˈkænsəl/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  cancelar

Meaning:  to decide that a planned event will not happen

Example:  We had to cancel our trip because of the storm.

→  Tuvimos que cancelar el viaje por la tormenta.

CAREER   /kəˈrɪr/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la carrera profesional

Meaning:  the series of jobs someone has in a particular field over their life

Example:  She's had a long career in journalism.

→  Ella ha tenido una larga carrera en el periodismo.

Note:  Don't confuse with "carrera" meaning a university degree program.

CAREFUL   /ˈkɛrfəl/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  cuidadoso/a

Meaning:  paying close attention to avoid mistakes or danger

Example:  Be careful on the stairs, they're wet.

→  Ten cuidado en las escaleras, están mojadas.

Common:  be careful

CAREFULLY   /ˈkɛrfəli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  con cuidado; cuidadosamente

Meaning:  in a way that pays close attention to avoid mistakes

Example:  Please read the instructions carefully.

→  Lee las instrucciones con cuidado, por favor.

CARELESS   /ˈkɛrləs/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  descuidado/a

Meaning:  not paying enough attention; making avoidable mistakes

Example:  That was a careless mistake — check your work next time.

→  Ese fue un error por descuido; revisa tu trabajo la próxima vez.

CEILING   /ˈsiːlɪŋ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el techo

Meaning:  the surface that forms the top of a room

Example:  There's a big crack in the ceiling.

→  Hay una grieta grande en el techo.

CELEBRATE   /ˈsɛləbreɪt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  celebrar

Meaning:  to do something special to mark a happy event

Example:  We celebrated her promotion with a dinner out.

→  Celebramos su ascenso con una cena fuera.

CEREMONY   /ˈsɛrəmoʊni/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la ceremonia

Meaning:  a formal event held to mark an important occasion

Example:  The wedding ceremony lasted about an hour.

→  La ceremonia de la boda duró aproximadamente una hora.

CERTIFICATE   /sərˈtɪfɪkət/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el certificado; el diploma

Meaning:  an official document proving a fact, such as completing a course

Example:  She received a certificate after finishing the course.

→  Ella recibió un certificado al terminar el curso.

CHANGE   /tʃeɪndʒ/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  cambiar; el cambio

Meaning:  to make or become different

Example:  Things have changed a lot since I last visited.

→  Las cosas han cambiado mucho desde mi última visita.

Note:  "Cambio" also means "loose change" (coins).

CHANNEL   /ˈtʃænəl/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el canal

Meaning:  a television or radio station; a way something travels or is sent

Example:  Can you change the channel? This show is boring.

→  ¿Puedes cambiar de canal? Este programa es aburrido.

CHARACTER   /ˈkærɪktər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el carácter; el personaje

Meaning:  the qualities that make someone who they are; a person in a book, film, or play

Example:  My favorite character in the film was the grandmother.

→  Mi personaje favorito de la película era la abuela.

CHARGER   /ˈtʃɑːrdʒər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el cargador

Meaning:  a device used to add electricity to a battery

Example:  Did you bring your phone charger?

→  ¿Trajiste el cargador del móvil?

CHECK   /tʃɛk/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  comprobar; revisar; el cheque

Meaning:  to make sure something is correct or safe; a written order to pay money

Example:  Can you check if the door is locked?

→  ¿Puedes comprobar si la puerta está cerrada con llave?

Common:  check in

Note:  American "check" (payment) = British "cheque".

CHOOSE   /tʃuːz/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  elegir; escoger

Meaning:  to decide which of several things you want

Example:  It's hard to choose between these two options.

→  Es difícil elegir entre estas dos opciones.

Note:  Irregular: choose → chose → chosen.

CLIENT   /ˈklaɪənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el cliente; la clienta

Meaning:  a person who pays for a service from a professional or business

Example:  The lawyer met with a new client this morning.

→  El abogado se reunió con un cliente nuevo esta mañana.

CLIMATE   /ˈklaɪmət/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el clima

Meaning:  the usual weather conditions of a place over a long period

Example:  The climate here is mild all year round.

→  El clima aquí es templado todo el año.

Common:  climate change

COLLEAGUE   /ˈkɒliːɡ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el colega; el compañero de trabajo

Meaning:  a person you work with

Example:  I had lunch with a colleague from the sales team.

→  Almorcé con un compañero del equipo de ventas.

COLLECT   /kəˈlɛkt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  coleccionar; recoger

Meaning:  to bring things together over time, often as a hobby; to gather or pick up

Example:  He collects old vinyl records.

→  Él colecciona discos de vinilo antiguos.

COMPANY   /ˈkʌmpəni/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la empresa; la compañía

Meaning:  a business that makes or sells goods or services

Example:  She works for a large technology company.

→  Ella trabaja para una gran empresa de tecnología.

COMPARE   /kəmˈpɛr/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  comparar

Meaning:  to look at two or more things to see how they are similar or different

Example:  Let's compare prices before we decide.

→  Comparemos precios antes de decidir.

Common:  compare to/with

COMPLETE   /kəmˈpliːt/  •  adjective; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  completo/a; completar

Meaning:  having all its parts; to finish something

Example:  Please complete the form before Friday.

→  Completa el formulario antes del viernes, por favor.

CONFIDENT   /ˈkɒnfɪdənt/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  seguro/a de sí mismo/a; confiado/a

Meaning:  feeling sure about your abilities or about something happening

Example:  She felt confident before the job interview.

→  Ella se sentía segura de sí misma antes de la entrevista de trabajo.

CONFIRMATION   /ˌkɒnfərˈmeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la confirmación

Meaning:  a statement or document proving that something is true or has been arranged

Example:  I received a confirmation email for the hotel booking.

→  Recibí un correo de confirmación de la reserva del hotel.

CONFUSED   /kənˈfjuːzd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  confundido/a

Meaning:  unable to understand or think clearly

Example:  I'm a bit confused about the instructions.

→  Estoy un poco confundido con las instrucciones.

CONNECTION   /kəˈnɛkʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la conexión

Meaning:  a link between two things or people; an internet or network link

Example:  My internet connection has been really slow today.

→  Mi conexión a internet ha estado muy lenta hoy.

CONTINUE   /kənˈtɪnjuː/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  continuar; seguir

Meaning:  to keep doing something without stopping

Example:  Please continue with your work — I'll wait.

→  Continúa con tu trabajo, por favor, yo espero.

CORRECTLY   /kəˈrɛktli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  correctamente

Meaning:  in a way that is right or accurate

Example:  Make sure you spell her name correctly.

→  Asegúrate de escribir su nombre correctamente.

COUGH   /kɔːf/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  toser; la tos

Meaning:  to force air out of your lungs suddenly, with a short loud sound

Example:  He couldn't stop coughing all night.

→  No paró de toser en toda la noche.

COUNTRYSIDE   /ˈkʌntrisaɪd/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el campo

Meaning:  land outside cities and towns, with fields and farms

Example:  We spent the weekend in the countryside.

→  Pasamos el fin de semana en el campo.

COURSE   /kɔːrs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el curso

Meaning:  a series of lessons on a particular subject

Example:  She's taking a Spanish course this semester.

→  Ella está haciendo un curso de español este semestre.

Common:  of course

COURT   /kɔːrt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el tribunal; el juzgado; la pista (deportes)

Meaning:  a place where legal cases are decided; a marked area for playing certain sports

Example:  The case will go to court next month.

→  El caso irá a juicio el mes que viene.

CRIME   /kraɪm/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el delito; el crimen

Meaning:  an illegal act that can be punished by law

Example:  Crime in this neighborhood has gone down a lot.

→  El delito en este barrio ha bajado mucho.

CRIMINAL   /ˈkrɪmɪnəl/  •  noun; adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  el criminal; criminal

Meaning:  a person who has committed a crime; related to crime

Example:  The criminal was arrested near the station.

→  El criminal fue arrestado cerca de la estación.

CROP   /krɒp/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la cosecha; el cultivo

Meaning:  a plant grown in large amounts for food, such as wheat or corn

Example:  The farmers lost most of their crop after the storm.

→  Los agricultores perdieron la mayor parte de la cosecha tras la tormenta.

CRUEL   /ˈkruːəl/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  cruel

Meaning:  causing pain or suffering to others deliberately

Example:  It was cruel of him to say that.

→  Fue cruel de su parte decir eso.

CULTURE   /ˈkʌltʃər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la cultura

Meaning:  the customs, art, and beliefs of a particular society or group

Example:  I love learning about different cultures.

→  Me encanta aprender sobre diferentes culturas.

CURIOUS   /ˈkjʊriəs/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  curioso/a

Meaning:  eager to know or learn about something

Example:  Kids are naturally curious about the world.

→  Los niños son naturalmente curiosos sobre el mundo.

CURRENCY   /ˈkɜːrənsi/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la moneda; la divisa

Meaning:  the money used in a particular country

Example:  What's the local currency in Japan?

→  ¿Cuál es la moneda local en Japón?

CUSTOM   /ˈkʌstəm/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la costumbre

Meaning:  a traditional way of behaving in a particular society or group

Example:  It's a local custom to greet neighbors every morning.

→  Es una costumbre local saludar a los vecinos cada mañana.

Note:  Don't confuse with "customs" (aduana), the border control area.

CUSTOMER   /ˈkʌstəmər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el cliente; la clienta

Meaning:  a person who buys goods or services from a business

Example:  The shop was full of customers on Saturday.

→  La tienda estaba llena de clientes el sábado.

CUSTOMS   /ˈkʌstəmz/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la aduana

Meaning:  the place at a border where officials check goods entering a country

Example:  We had to wait a long time at customs.

→  Tuvimos que esperar mucho tiempo en la aduana.

DAMAGE   /ˈdæmɪdʒ/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el daño; dañar

Meaning:  harm caused to something; to cause harm to something

Example:  The storm caused a lot of damage to the roof.

→  La tormenta causó muchos daños al techo.

DECIDE   /dɪˈsaɪd/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  decidir

Meaning:  to choose something after thinking about it

Example:  We haven't decided where to go on vacation yet.

→  Todavía no hemos decidido adónde ir de vacaciones.

DEGREE   /dɪˈɡriː/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el título universitario; el grado

Meaning:  a qualification from a university; a unit for measuring temperature or angles

Example:  She has a degree in economics.

→  Ella tiene un título en economía.

DELIGHTED   /dɪˈlaɪtɪd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  encantado/a

Meaning:  very pleased about something

Example:  We were delighted to hear the good news.

→  Estábamos encantados de escuchar la buena noticia.

DELIVER   /dɪˈlɪvər/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  entregar; repartir

Meaning:  to take something to the place or person it is meant for

Example:  The package should be delivered tomorrow.

→  El paquete debería entregarse mañana.

DENTIST   /ˈdɛntɪst/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el dentista; la dentista

Meaning:  a person trained to treat teeth

Example:  I have a dentist appointment tomorrow.

→  Tengo cita con el dentista mañana.

DEPARTMENT   /dɪˈpɑːrtmənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el departamento

Meaning:  a section of a company, university, or store dealing with a specific area

Example:  She works in the marketing department.

→  Ella trabaja en el departamento de marketing.

DESCRIBE   /dɪˈskraɪb/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  describir

Meaning:  to say what someone or something is like

Example:  Can you describe the man you saw?

→  ¿Puedes describir al hombre que viste?

DESIGN   /dɪˈzaɪn/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el diseño; diseñar

Meaning:  a plan or drawing showing how something will look or work; to create such a plan

Example:  I love the design of this new building.

→  Me encanta el diseño de este edificio nuevo.

DESSERT   /dɪˈzɜːrt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el postre

Meaning:  a sweet food eaten after the main part of a meal

Example:  What's for dessert tonight?

→  ¿Qué hay de postre esta noche?

DEVELOP   /dɪˈvɛləp/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  desarrollar; desarrollarse

Meaning:  to grow or change into something more advanced

Example:  The company developed a new app last year.

→  La empresa desarrolló una nueva aplicación el año pasado.

DEVICE   /dɪˈvaɪs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el dispositivo; el aparato

Meaning:  a machine or tool made for a particular purpose

Example:  This device measures your heart rate.

→  Este dispositivo mide tu ritmo cardíaco.

DIET   /ˈdaɪət/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la dieta; la alimentación

Meaning:  the food a person or animal usually eats

Example:  A balanced diet includes plenty of fruit and vegetables.

→  Una dieta equilibrada incluye mucha fruta y verdura.

DIPLOMA   /dɪˈploʊmə/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el diploma

Meaning:  an official document showing you have completed a course of study

Example:  He framed his diploma and hung it on the wall.

→  Enmarcó su diploma y lo colgó en la pared.

DIRECTION   /dɪˈrɛkʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la dirección

Meaning:  the way something moves or points toward

Example:  We were walking in the wrong direction.

→  Estábamos caminando en la dirección equivocada.

Common:  give directions

DISAGREE   /ˌdɪsəˈɡriː/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  no estar de acuerdo; discrepar

Meaning:  to have a different opinion from someone else

Example:  I disagree with you on this one.

→  No estoy de acuerdo contigo en esto.

DISAPPOINTED   /ˌdɪsəˈpɔɪntɪd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  decepcionado/a

Meaning:  feeling sad because something was not as good as you hoped

Example:  She was disappointed with her exam results.

→  Ella estaba decepcionada con sus resultados del examen.

DISCOUNT   /ˈdɪskaʊnt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el descuento

Meaning:  a reduction in the usual price of something

Example:  We got a 20% discount on our hotel room.

→  Nos hicieron un descuento del 20 % en la habitación del hotel.

DISCOVER   /dɪˈskʌvər/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  descubrir

Meaning:  to find or learn something for the first time

Example:  Scientists discovered a new species of frog.

→  Los científicos descubrieron una nueva especie de rana.

DISCUSS   /dɪˈskʌs/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  discutir; hablar de

Meaning:  to talk about a subject with someone in detail

Example:  Let's discuss this over lunch.

→  Hablemos de esto durante el almuerzo.

Note:  In Spanish, "discutir" can also mean "to argue" — check the context.

DISEASE   /dɪˈziːz/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la enfermedad

Meaning:  an illness affecting a person, animal, or plant

Example:  The disease is spread by mosquitoes.

→  La enfermedad se transmite por mosquitos.

DISHONEST   /dɪsˈɒnɪst/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  deshonesto/a

Meaning:  not truthful or fair

Example:  It would be dishonest to say I agree when I don't.

→  Sería deshonesto decir que estoy de acuerdo cuando no lo estoy.

DISTANCE   /ˈdɪstəns/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la distancia

Meaning:  the amount of space between two places or things

Example:  What's the distance between the two cities?

→  ¿Cuál es la distancia entre las dos ciudades?

DOWNTOWN   /ˌdaʊnˈtaʊn/  •  adverb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el centro (de la ciudad)

Meaning:  the central business area of a city

Example:  We're meeting downtown after work.

→  Nos vemos en el centro después del trabajo.

EMBARRASSED   /ɪmˈbærəst/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  avergonzado/a

Meaning:  feeling shy, awkward, or ashamed about something

Example:  I was so embarrassed when I forgot his name.

→  Me dio mucha vergüenza cuando olvidé su nombre.

EMOTION   /ɪˈmoʊʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la emoción

Meaning:  a strong feeling, such as love, fear, or joy

Example:  She couldn't hide her emotions during the film.

→  Ella no pudo ocultar sus emociones durante la película.

EMPLOY   /ɪmˈplɔɪ/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  emplear; contratar

Meaning:  to pay someone to work for you

Example:  The factory employs about two hundred people.

→  La fábrica emplea a unas doscientas personas.

EMPLOYEE   /ɪmˈplɔɪiː/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el empleado; la empleada

Meaning:  a person who works for a company or another person

Example:  All new employees get training in their first week.

→  Todos los empleados nuevos reciben formación en su primera semana.

EMPLOYER   /ɪmˈplɔɪər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el empleador; el jefe

Meaning:  a person or company that pays others to work for them

Example:  Her employer offered her a promotion.

→  Su empleador le ofreció un ascenso.

ENERGY   /ˈɛnərdʒi/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la energía

Meaning:  the power needed to do work, or the strength to be active

Example:  I don't have the energy to go out tonight.

→  No tengo energía para salir esta noche.

ENOUGH   /ɪˈnʌf/  •  determiner; adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  suficiente; bastante

Meaning:  as much as is needed

Example:  We don't have enough time to finish today.

→  No tenemos suficiente tiempo para terminar hoy.

ENVIRONMENT   /ɪnˈvaɪrənmənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el medio ambiente; el entorno

Meaning:  the natural world, or the conditions surrounding a person

Example:  We should all try to protect the environment.

→  Todos deberíamos intentar proteger el medio ambiente.

EVENTUALLY   /ɪˈvɛntʃuəli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  finalmente; con el tiempo

Meaning:  after some time; in the end

Example:  We got lost, but eventually we found the hotel.

→  Nos perdimos, pero finalmente encontramos el hotel.

Note:  False friend: "eventualmente" in Spanish means "possibly", not "eventually".

EXCHANGE   /ɪksˈtʃeɪndʒ/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  intercambiar; cambiar; el intercambio

Meaning:  to give something and receive something else in return

Example:  Can I exchange this shirt for a bigger size?

→  ¿Puedo cambiar esta camisa por una talla más grande?

Common:  exchange rate

EXHAUSTED   /ɪɡˈzɔːstɪd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  agotado/a

Meaning:  extremely tired

Example:  I was exhausted after the long flight.

→  Estaba agotado después del vuelo tan largo.

EXPECT   /ɪkˈspɛkt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  esperar; prever

Meaning:  to believe that something will happen

Example:  We expect the package to arrive on Friday.

→  Esperamos que el paquete llegue el viernes.

EXPERIENCE   /ɪkˈspɪriəns/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  la experiencia; experimentar

Meaning:  knowledge gained by doing something; to have something happen to you

Example:  She has a lot of experience working with children.

→  Ella tiene mucha experiencia trabajando con niños.

EXPLAIN   /ɪkˈspleɪn/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  explicar

Meaning:  to make something clear or easy to understand

Example:  Can you explain how this works?

→  ¿Puedes explicar cómo funciona esto?

EXPRESS   /ɪkˈsprɛs/  •  verb; adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  expresar; expreso/a

Meaning:  to show your thoughts or feelings using words or actions

Example:  It's healthy to express your feelings.

→  Es saludable expresar tus sentimientos.

FACTORY   /ˈfæktəri/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la fábrica

Meaning:  a building where goods are made in large quantities

Example:  He works in a car factory.

→  Él trabaja en una fábrica de coches.

FAIL   /feɪl/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  fallar; suspender (un examen)

Meaning:  to not succeed at something you tried to do

Example:  She failed her driving test twice before passing.

→  Suspendió el examen de conducir dos veces antes de aprobarlo.

FARE   /fɛr/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la tarifa (de transporte)

Meaning:  the money you pay to travel by bus, train, plane, or taxi

Example:  The bus fare went up last month.

→  La tarifa del autobús subió el mes pasado.

FARM   /fɑːrm/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la granja

Meaning:  an area of land used to grow crops or raise animals

Example:  They grew up on a small farm in the countryside.

→  Crecieron en una pequeña granja en el campo.

FARMER   /ˈfɑːrmər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el agricultor; la agricultora; el granjero

Meaning:  a person who owns or manages a farm

Example:  The farmer gets up before sunrise every day.

→  El agricultor se levanta antes del amanecer todos los días.

FEELING   /ˈfiːlɪŋ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el sentimiento; la sensación

Meaning:  an emotional state, or a physical sensation

Example:  I had a strange feeling that something was wrong.

→  Tuve una sensación extraña de que algo iba mal.

Common:  hurt someone's feelings

FESTIVAL   /ˈfɛstəvəl/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el festival

Meaning:  an organized event or celebration, often with music, food, or art

Example:  We're going to a music festival this weekend.

→  Vamos a un festival de música este fin de semana.

FEVER   /ˈfiːvər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la fiebre

Meaning:  a body temperature that is higher than normal, usually from illness

Example:  He stayed home with a fever.

→  Se quedó en casa con fiebre.

FEWER   /ˈfjuːər/  •  determiner  •  A2

Spanish:  menos

Meaning:  a smaller number of things

Example:  Fewer people came to the meeting than expected.

→  Vino menos gente de la esperada a la reunión.

Note:  Used with countable nouns (fewer people); use "less" with uncountable nouns (less time).

FIELD   /fiːld/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el campo

Meaning:  an area of land, often used for growing crops or playing sports

Example:  The cows were grazing in the field.

→  Las vacas pastaban en el campo.

FINALLY   /ˈfaɪnəli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  finalmente; por fin

Meaning:  after a long time or delay

Example:  The bus finally arrived after a thirty-minute wait.

→  El autobús por fin llegó después de esperar treinta minutos.

FINE   /faɪn/  •  adjective; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  bien; fino/a; la multa

Meaning:  acceptable or good; money paid as a punishment

Example:  He got a fine for parking in the wrong place.

→  Le pusieron una multa por aparcar en el sitio equivocado.

FINISH   /ˈfɪnɪʃ/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  terminar; acabar

Meaning:  to complete something; to reach the end of an activity

Example:  What time do you finish work today?

→  ¿A qué hora terminas de trabajar hoy?

FLAVOR   /ˈfleɪvər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el sabor

Meaning:  the particular taste of a food or drink

Example:  What flavor of ice cream do you want?

→  ¿Qué sabor de helado quieres?

Note:  British spelling: "flavour".

FLIGHT   /flaɪt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el vuelo

Meaning:  a journey made by airplane

Example:  Our flight leaves at six in the morning.

→  Nuestro vuelo sale a las seis de la mañana.

FLOOR   /flɔːr/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el suelo; el piso

Meaning:  the surface you walk on inside a building; a level of a building

Example:  The keys fell on the floor.

→  Las llaves se cayeron al suelo.

Note:  "Piso" also refers to a floor level, like "the third floor".

FOG   /fɒɡ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la niebla

Meaning:  a thick cloud of tiny water drops close to the ground

Example:  The fog made it hard to see the road.

→  La niebla dificultaba ver la carretera.

FOLLOW   /ˈfɒloʊ/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  seguir

Meaning:  to move behind someone; to obey instructions; to watch someone's updates online

Example:  Just follow me, I know the way.

→  Solo sígueme, yo conozco el camino.

Common:  follow instructions

FORBID   /fərˈbɪd/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  prohibir

Meaning:  to order that something must not be done

Example:  Her parents forbid her to stay out past midnight.

→  Sus padres le prohíben quedarse fuera después de medianoche.

Note:  Irregular: forbid → forbade → forbidden.

FORECAST   /ˈfɔːrkæst/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el pronóstico; pronosticar

Meaning:  a statement about what is likely to happen in the future, especially the weather

Example:  The forecast says it will rain all weekend.

→  El pronóstico dice que lloverá todo el fin de semana.

Common:  weather forecast

FORGET   /fərˈɡɛt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  olvidar

Meaning:  to fail to remember something

Example:  I always forget where I put my keys.

→  Siempre olvido dónde pongo las llaves.

Note:  Irregular: forget → forgot → forgotten.

FORGIVE   /fərˈɡɪv/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  perdonar

Meaning:  to stop being angry with someone for something they did wrong

Example:  She forgave him after he apologized.

→  Ella lo perdonó después de que él se disculpara.

Note:  Irregular: forgive → forgave → forgiven.

FRIENDLY   /ˈfrɛndli/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  amistoso/a; amigable

Meaning:  behaving in a kind and pleasant way toward others

Example:  Everyone in the town was really friendly to us.

→  Todo el mundo en el pueblo fue muy amable con nosotros.

FURNITURE   /ˈfɜːrnɪtʃər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  los muebles

Meaning:  objects such as tables, chairs, and beds used in a room

Example:  We're buying new furniture for the living room.

→  Estamos comprando muebles nuevos para el salón.

Note:  "Furniture" has no plural form in English, but "muebles" is plural in Spanish.

GARAGE   /ɡəˈrɑːʒ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el garaje

Meaning:  a building where you keep a car, or where cars are repaired

Example:  He parks his car in the garage every night.

→  Él aparca el coche en el garaje todas las noches.

GENEROUS   /ˈdʒɛnərəs/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  generoso/a

Meaning:  willing to give more money, time, or help than is expected

Example:  It was very generous of you to help us move.

→  Fue muy generoso de tu parte ayudarnos a mudarnos.

GRADE   /ɡreɪd/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la nota; el grado (escolar)

Meaning:  a mark showing how well someone did on a test or in a course

Example:  She got a good grade on her final exam.

→  Ella sacó una buena nota en su examen final.

GRADUATE   /ˈɡrædʒueɪt/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  graduarse; el graduado

Meaning:  to complete a course of study at a school or university

Example:  She graduated from university last spring.

→  Ella se graduó de la universidad la primavera pasada.

GRATEFUL   /ˈɡreɪtfəl/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  agradecido/a

Meaning:  feeling thankful for something someone did for you

Example:  I'm really grateful for all your help.

→  Estoy muy agradecido por toda tu ayuda.

GREET   /ɡriːt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  saludar

Meaning:  to welcome someone with words or a gesture

Example:  She greeted us with a big smile.

→  Ella nos saludó con una gran sonrisa.

GREETING   /ˈɡriːtɪŋ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el saludo

Meaning:  a word or gesture used to welcome someone

Example:  "Good morning" is a common greeting.

→  "Buenos días" es un saludo habitual.

GUARANTEE   /ˌɡærənˈtiː/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  la garantía; garantizar

GUESS   /ɡɛs/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

GUIDE   /ɡaɪd/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

HABIT   /ˈhæbɪt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el hábito; la costumbre

HEADACHE   /ˈhɛdeɪk/  •  noun  •  A2

HOBBY   /ˈhɒbi/  •  noun  •  A2

HUMID   /ˈhjuːmɪd/  •  adjective  •  A2

HURT   /hɜːrt/  •  verb; adjective  •  A2

ILLNESS   /ˈɪlnəs/  •  noun  •  A2

IMAGINATION   /ɪˌmædʒɪˈneɪʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

INCOME   /ˈɪnkʌm/  •  noun  •  A2

PATIENT   /ˈpeɪʃənt/  •  adjective; noun  •  A2

POLLUTION   /pəˈluːʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

PRACTICE   /ˈpræktɪs/  •  noun; verb  •  A2
