PREFER   /prɪˈfɜːr/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  preferir

Meaning:  to like one thing more than another

Example:  I prefer tea to coffee.

→  Prefiero el té al café.

Common:  prefer... to...

PREPARE   /prɪˈpɛr/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  preparar

Meaning:  to get something ready for use

Example:  She's preparing dinner for six people tonight.

→  Ella está preparando la cena para seis personas esta noche.

PRETEND   /prɪˈtɛnd/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  fingir; pretender (aparentar)

Meaning:  to behave as if something is true when it isn't

Example:  He pretended not to notice her.

→  Él fingió no darse cuenta de ella.

Note:  False friend: Spanish "pretender" usually means "to intend" or "to seek", not "to pretend".

PRICE   /praɪs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el precio

Meaning:  the amount of money you must pay for something

Example:  The price of the tickets went up last month.

→  El precio de las entradas subió el mes pasado.

PRISON   /ˈprɪzən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la prisión; la cárcel

Meaning:  a building where criminals are kept as punishment

Example:  He spent five years in prison.

→  Pasó cinco años en la cárcel.

PRODUCE   /prəˈduːs/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  producir

Meaning:  to make or create something

Example:  The factory produces about a thousand cars a week.

→  La fábrica produce unos mil coches a la semana.

PRODUCT   /ˈprɒdʌkt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el producto

Meaning:  something made to be sold

Example:  This is one of our most popular products.

→  Este es uno de nuestros productos más populares.

PROFESSION   /prəˈfɛʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la profesión

Meaning:  a job that needs special training or education

Example:  Teaching is a very demanding profession.

→  La enseñanza es una profesión muy exigente.

PROGRAM   /ˈproʊɡræm/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el programa; programar

Meaning:  a planned series of events, or a set of instructions for a computer

Example:  We watched an interesting program about space.

→  Vimos un programa interesante sobre el espacio.

Note:  British spelling: "programme" (for TV/events); "program" is used for computers in both varieties.

PROMISE   /ˈprɒmɪs/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  prometer; la promesa

Meaning:  to say you will definitely do something

Example:  He promised to call as soon as he landed.

→  Prometió llamar en cuanto aterrizara.

PROTECT   /prəˈtɛkt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  proteger

Meaning:  to keep someone or something safe from harm

Example:  Sunscreen protects your skin from the sun.

→  El protector solar protege tu piel del sol.

PROUD   /praʊd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  orgulloso/a

Meaning:  feeling pleased about something you or someone else has achieved

Example:  We're very proud of you.

→  Estamos muy orgullosos de ti.

Common:  proud of

PROVIDE   /prəˈvaɪd/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  proporcionar; proveer

Meaning:  to give someone something they need

Example:  The hotel provides free breakfast for all guests.

→  El hotel proporciona desayuno gratis para todos los huéspedes.

PUBLISH   /ˈpʌblɪʃ/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  publicar

Meaning:  to prepare and print a book, magazine, or article for the public

Example:  Her first novel was published last year.

→  Su primera novela se publicó el año pasado.

PULL   /pʊl/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  tirar de; jalar (Latin America)

Meaning:  to move something toward yourself using force

Example:  Pull the door — it doesn't push open.

→  Tira de la puerta, no se abre empujando.

PUSH   /pʊʃ/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  empujar

Meaning:  to move something away from yourself using force

Example:  Push the door to open it.

→  Empuja la puerta para abrirla.

QUALITY   /ˈkwɒləti/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la calidad

Meaning:  how good or bad something is

Example:  The quality of this fabric is excellent.

→  La calidad de esta tela es excelente.

QUANTITY   /ˈkwɒntəti/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la cantidad

Meaning:  an amount of something that can be counted or measured

Example:  They ordered a large quantity of supplies.

→  Pidieron una gran cantidad de suministros.

QUEUE   /kjuː/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  la cola; hacer cola

Meaning:  a line of people waiting for something; to stand in such a line

Example:  There was a long queue outside the bakery.

→  Había una cola larga fuera de la panadería.

Note:  British English; American English usually says "line".

QUICKLY   /ˈkwɪkli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  rápidamente; deprisa

Meaning:  at great speed; without delay

Example:  She finished her homework quickly.

→  Ella terminó los deberes rápidamente.

RAISE   /reɪz/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  levantar; criar; aumentar; el aumento

Meaning:  to lift something up; to bring up children; to increase an amount

Example:  Raise your hand if you have a question.

→  Levanta la mano si tienes una pregunta.

REACH   /riːtʃ/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  alcanzar; llegar a

Meaning:  to arrive at a place; to stretch out your hand to touch or get something

Example:  We finally reached the top of the mountain.

→  Finalmente llegamos a la cima de la montaña.

REALIZE   /ˈriːəlaɪz/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  darse cuenta de

Meaning:  to become aware of something you didn't know before

Example:  I realized I had left my phone at home.

→  Me di cuenta de que había dejado el móvil en casa.

Note:  British spelling: "realise".

RECEIPT   /rɪˈsiːt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el recibo; el ticket

Meaning:  a piece of paper proving that you paid for something

Example:  Keep your receipt in case you need to return it.

→  Guarda el recibo por si necesitas devolverlo.

Note:  The "p" is silent.

RECEIVE   /rɪˈsiːv/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  recibir

Meaning:  to get or be given something

Example:  I received your email this morning.

→  Recibí tu correo esta mañana.

RECENTLY   /ˈriːsəntli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  recientemente

Meaning:  not long ago

Example:  We recently moved to a new house.

→  Nos mudamos recientemente a una casa nueva.

RECIPE   /ˈrɛsəpi/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la receta (de cocina)

Meaning:  a set of instructions for cooking a dish

Example:  Can you send me the recipe for this cake?

→  ¿Puedes enviarme la receta de este pastel?

Note:  For a medical prescription, use "receta médica", not this word alone.

RECORD   /rɪˈkɔːrd/ (verb); /ˈrɛkərd/ (noun)  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  grabar; el registro; el récord

Meaning:  to store sound, video, or information; the best result ever achieved

Example:  She recorded the whole concert on her phone.

→  Ella grabó todo el concierto con el móvil.

RECYCLE   /riːˈsaɪkəl/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  reciclar

Meaning:  to process used materials so they can be used again

Example:  We recycle paper, glass, and plastic at home.

→  Reciclamos papel, vidrio y plástico en casa.

REDUCE   /rɪˈduːs/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  reducir

Meaning:  to make something smaller in size, amount, or degree

Example:  We're trying to reduce our energy use at home.

→  Estamos intentando reducir el consumo de energía en casa.

REFUND   /ˈriːfʌnd/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el reembolso; reembolsar

Meaning:  money given back to you, usually because you returned a product

Example:  I asked for a refund because the item was broken.

→  Pedí un reembolso porque el artículo estaba roto.

REFUSE   /rɪˈfjuːz/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  negarse a; rechazar

Meaning:  to say clearly that you will not do or accept something

Example:  He refused to answer any questions.

→  Él se negó a responder ninguna pregunta.

REGARDS   /rɪˈɡɑːrdz/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  saludos

Meaning:  good wishes sent to someone, often at the end of a letter or email

Example:  Please give my regards to your family.

→  Dale saludos a tu familia de mi parte.

Common:  best regards

RELAXED   /rɪˈlækst/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  relajado/a

Meaning:  calm and free from worry or tension

Example:  She looked much more relaxed after her vacation.

→  Ella se veía mucho más relajada después de sus vacaciones.

RELIGION   /rɪˈlɪdʒən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la religión

Meaning:  the belief in and worship of a god or gods

Example:  They studied the history of world religions.

→  Estudiaron la historia de las religiones del mundo.

REMEMBER   /rɪˈmɛmbər/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  recordar; acordarse de

Meaning:  to keep something in your mind, or bring it back to mind

Example:  I don't remember where I parked the car.

→  No recuerdo dónde aparqué el coche.

REMOVE   /rɪˈmuːv/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  quitar; eliminar

Meaning:  to take something away or off

Example:  Please remove your shoes before entering.

→  Quítate los zapatos antes de entrar, por favor.

REPAIR   /rɪˈpɛr/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  reparar; la reparación

Meaning:  to fix something that is broken or damaged

Example:  It took two weeks to repair the roof.

→  Tardaron dos semanas en reparar el techo.

REPEAT   /rɪˈpiːt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  repetir

Meaning:  to say or do something again

Example:  Could you repeat that, please? I didn't hear you.

→  ¿Podrías repetir eso, por favor? No te escuché.

REPLACE   /rɪˈpleɪs/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  reemplazar; sustituir

Meaning:  to put a new or different thing in the place of another

Example:  We need to replace the broken window.

→  Necesitamos reemplazar la ventana rota.

REPLY   /rɪˈplaɪ/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  responder; la respuesta

Meaning:  to answer someone in speech or writing

Example:  She replied to my message right away.

→  Ella respondió a mi mensaje enseguida.

REPORT   /rɪˈpɔːrt/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el informe; el reportaje; informar

Meaning:  a written or spoken description of something; to give information about an event

Example:  She wrote a report about the local economy.

→  Ella escribió un informe sobre la economía local.

REPRESENT   /ˌrɛprɪˈzɛnt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  representar

Meaning:  to speak or act officially for another person or group

Example:  She will represent our company at the conference.

→  Ella representará a nuestra empresa en la conferencia.

REQUIRE   /rɪˈkwaɪər/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  requerir; necesitar

Meaning:  to need something, or to make something necessary

Example:  This job requires a lot of patience.

→  Este trabajo requiere mucha paciencia.

RESCUE   /ˈrɛskjuː/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  rescatar; el rescate

Meaning:  to save someone from danger

Example:  Firefighters rescued the family from the burning building.

→  Los bomberos rescataron a la familia del edificio en llamas.

RESERVATION   /ˌrɛzərˈveɪʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la reserva

Meaning:  an arrangement to have a table, room, or seat kept for you

Example:  I have a reservation for two at eight o'clock.

→  Tengo una reserva para dos a las ocho.

Common:  make a reservation

RESERVE   /rɪˈzɜːrv/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  reservar

Meaning:  to arrange for something to be kept for you in advance

Example:  We reserved a table for Saturday night.

→  Reservamos una mesa para el sábado por la noche.

RETURN   /rɪˈtɜːrn/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  volver; regresar; devolver; la vuelta

Meaning:  to come or go back to a place; to give something back

Example:  He returned home late last night.

→  Volvió a casa tarde anoche.

REVEAL   /rɪˈviːl/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  revelar

Meaning:  to make something known that was previously secret

Example:  She revealed the surprise at the end of the party.

→  Ella reveló la sorpresa al final de la fiesta.

RIDE   /raɪd/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  montar; ir en; el paseo; el trayecto

Meaning:  to sit on and control a bicycle, horse, or vehicle; a trip in a vehicle

Example:  Can you give me a ride to the airport?

→  ¿Puedes llevarme al aeropuerto?

Note:  Irregular: ride → rode → ridden.

ROOF   /ruːf/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el tejado; el techo

Meaning:  the covering on top of a building

Example:  There's a leak in the roof.

→  Hay una gotera en el tejado.

RUDE   /ruːd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  maleducado/a; grosero/a

Meaning:  not polite; showing a lack of respect

Example:  It was rude of him to interrupt the meeting.

→  Fue de mala educación por su parte interrumpir la reunión.

SALARY   /ˈsæləri/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el salario; el sueldo

Meaning:  the fixed amount of money someone is paid regularly for their job

Example:  She got a salary increase after her promotion.

→  Le subieron el sueldo después de su ascenso.

SAUCE   /sɔːs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la salsa

Meaning:  a thick liquid served with food to add flavor

Example:  This pasta has a delicious tomato sauce.

→  Esta pasta tiene una salsa de tomate deliciosa.

SAVE   /seɪv/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  ahorrar; guardar; salvar

Meaning:  to keep money for later use; to keep something for later; to rescue someone

Example:  I'm trying to save money for a new car.

→  Estoy intentando ahorrar dinero para un coche nuevo.

SCHOLARSHIP   /ˈskɒlərʃɪp/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la beca

Meaning:  money given to a student to help pay for their studies

Example:  She won a scholarship to study abroad.

→  Ella ganó una beca para estudiar en el extranjero.

SCREEN   /skriːn/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la pantalla

Meaning:  the flat surface on a device or television that shows images

Example:  The screen on my phone cracked.

→  Se agrietó la pantalla de mi móvil.

SEARCH   /sɜːrtʃ/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  buscar; la búsqueda

Meaning:  to look carefully for something

Example:  We searched everywhere for the missing keys.

→  Buscamos por todas partes las llaves perdidas.

Common:  search for

SEEM   /siːm/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  parecer

Meaning:  to appear to be a certain way, based on what you can see or hear

Example:  She seems really happy in her new job.

→  Ella parece muy feliz en su nuevo trabajo.

SELFISH   /ˈsɛlfɪʃ/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  egoísta

Meaning:  caring only about yourself, not about other people

Example:  It was selfish of him not to share.

→  Fue egoísta por su parte no compartir.

SERVICE   /ˈsɜːrvɪs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el servicio

Meaning:  the help or work provided by a business for its customers

Example:  The service at that restaurant was excellent.

→  El servicio en ese restaurante fue excelente.

SEVERAL   /ˈsɛvərəl/  •  determiner  •  A2

Spanish:  varios/as

Meaning:  more than two but not very many

Example:  I've read this book several times.

→  He leído este libro varias veces.

SHAPE   /ʃeɪp/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la forma

Meaning:  the outer form or outline of something, such as a circle or square

Example:  The cookies come in different shapes.

→  Las galletas vienen en diferentes formas.

SHOUT   /ʃaʊt/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  gritar; el grito

Meaning:  to say something very loudly

Example:  He shouted for help when he saw the fire.

→  Gritó pidiendo ayuda cuando vio el fuego.

SHY   /ʃaɪ/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  tímido/a

Meaning:  nervous or uncomfortable around other people

Example:  He's quite shy when he meets new people.

→  Él es bastante tímido cuando conoce a gente nueva.

SIDEWALK   /ˈsaɪdwɔːk/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la acera

Meaning:  a paved path for pedestrians beside a road

Example:  Please keep your bike on the sidewalk.

→  Mantén tu bicicleta en la acera, por favor.

Note:  British English uses "pavement".

SIGN   /saɪn/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  la señal; el letrero; firmar

Meaning:  a board or symbol giving information; to write your name on a document

Example:  Please sign here to confirm your order.

→  Firma aquí para confirmar tu pedido, por favor.

SINCERELY   /sɪnˈsɪrli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  sinceramente; atentamente

Meaning:  in an honest and genuine way; used to end a formal letter

Example:  Sincerely, Maria Lopez.

→  Atentamente, María López.

SIZE   /saɪz/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la talla; el tamaño

Meaning:  how big or small something is

Example:  Do you have this shirt in a smaller size?

→  ¿Tienes esta camisa en una talla más pequeña?

Note:  "Talla" is for clothes; "tamaño" is for general size.

SKILL   /skɪl/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la habilidad; la destreza

Meaning:  the ability to do something well, usually gained through training or practice

Example:  Cooking is a useful skill to learn.

→  Cocinar es una habilidad útil para aprender.

SLOWLY   /ˈsloʊli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  despacio; lentamente

Meaning:  at a slow speed

Example:  Please speak more slowly.

→  Habla más despacio, por favor.

SMELL   /smɛl/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  oler; el olor

Meaning:  to notice something with your nose; a quality noticed by the nose

Example:  This soup smells amazing.

→  Esta sopa huele increíble.

SNACK   /snæk/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el aperitivo; el tentempié

Meaning:  a small amount of food eaten between meals

Example:  I usually have a snack in the afternoon.

→  Normalmente como un tentempié por la tarde.

SOFTWARE   /ˈsɔːftwɛr/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el software; los programas

Meaning:  the programs used to make a computer perform tasks

Example:  We need to update the software on this computer.

→  Necesitamos actualizar el software de este ordenador.

SPICE   /spaɪs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la especia

Meaning:  a substance made from a plant, used to add flavor to food

Example:  This curry has a lot of spice in it.

→  Este curry lleva muchas especias.

STAIRS   /stɛrz/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  las escaleras

Meaning:  a set of steps leading from one level of a building to another

Example:  She ran up the stairs to catch the phone.

→  Subió corriendo las escaleras para coger el teléfono.

STORM   /stɔːrm/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la tormenta

Meaning:  a period of very bad weather with strong wind and rain

Example:  A storm is expected later tonight.

→  Se espera una tormenta más tarde esta noche.

STRESSED   /strɛst/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  estresado/a

Meaning:  feeling worried or under pressure

Example:  She's been really stressed about the exam.

→  Ella ha estado muy estresada por el examen.

STUBBORN   /ˈstʌbərn/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  terco/a; testarudo/a

Meaning:  refusing to change your mind or actions

Example:  He's too stubborn to admit he's wrong.

→  Es demasiado terco para admitir que se equivoca.

SUBJECT   /ˈsʌbdʒɪkt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el tema; la asignatura

Meaning:  the topic being discussed; a course studied at school

Example:  Math is my favorite subject.

→  Las matemáticas son mi asignatura favorita.

SUBURB   /ˈsʌbɜːrb/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  las afueras; el suburbio

Meaning:  a residential area outside the center of a city

Example:  They live in a quiet suburb of Madrid.

→  Viven en un suburbio tranquilo de Madrid.

SUDDENLY   /ˈsʌdənli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  de repente; de pronto

Meaning:  quickly and unexpectedly

Example:  Suddenly, the lights went out.

→  De repente, se fueron las luces.

SUGGEST   /səɡˈdʒɛst/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  sugerir

Meaning:  to put forward an idea for someone to consider

Example:  She suggested we try the new restaurant downtown.

→  Ella sugirió que probáramos el restaurante nuevo del centro.

SUITCASE   /ˈsuːtkeɪs/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la maleta

Meaning:  a rectangular bag used for carrying clothes when traveling

Example:  I packed my suitcase the night before the flight.

→  Hice la maleta la noche antes del vuelo.

TALENT   /ˈtælənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el talento

Meaning:  a natural ability to do something well

Example:  She has a real talent for painting.

→  Ella tiene un verdadero talento para la pintura.

TASTE   /teɪst/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el sabor; el gusto; probar; saber a

Meaning:  the flavor of food or drink; to try food to check its flavor

Example:  This soup has a great taste.

→  Esta sopa tiene un sabor buenísimo.

TECHNOLOGY   /tɛkˈnɒlədʒi/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la tecnología

Meaning:  the use of science to create tools, machines, and systems

Example:  Technology has changed the way we work.

→  La tecnología ha cambiado la forma en que trabajamos.

TEMPERATURE   /ˈtɛmprətʃər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la temperatura

Meaning:  how hot or cold something is, especially the air

Example:  The temperature dropped a lot overnight.

→  La temperatura bajó mucho durante la noche.

TERRIFIED   /ˈtɛrɪfaɪd/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  aterrorizado/a

Meaning:  extremely frightened

Example:  She was terrified of spiders as a child.

→  De niña, ella estaba aterrorizada de las arañas.

THUNDER   /ˈθʌndər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el trueno

Meaning:  the loud noise heard during a storm, after lightning

Example:  The thunder woke up the whole house.

→  El trueno despertó a toda la casa.

TICKET   /ˈtɪkɪt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el billete; el boleto; la entrada

Meaning:  a small piece of paper or digital pass allowing you to travel or enter somewhere

Example:  I bought two tickets for the concert.

→  Compré dos entradas para el concierto.

Note:  "Billete"/"boleto" for transport; "entrada" for events.

TOUCH   /tʌtʃ/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  tocar; el tacto

Meaning:  to put your hand or fingers on something

Example:  Please don't touch the paintings.

→  Por favor, no toques los cuadros.

Common:  keep in touch

TOURIST   /ˈtʊrɪst/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el turista; la turista

Meaning:  a person who travels to visit a place for pleasure

Example:  The old town is full of tourists in summer.

→  El casco antiguo está lleno de turistas en verano.

TRACTOR   /ˈtræktər/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el tractor

Meaning:  a powerful vehicle used on farms to pull heavy equipment

Example:  The farmer drove his tractor across the field.

→  El agricultor condujo su tractor por el campo.

TRADITION   /trəˈdɪʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la tradición

Meaning:  a custom or belief passed down through generations

Example:  It's a family tradition to cook together on Sundays.

→  Es una tradición familiar cocinar juntos los domingos.

TRAFFIC   /ˈtræfɪk/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el tráfico

Meaning:  the vehicles moving along a road

Example:  There was a lot of traffic on the way home.

→  Había mucho tráfico de camino a casa.

Common:  traffic jam

TRANSLATE   /trænzˈleɪt/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  traducir

Meaning:  to change words from one language into another

Example:  Can you translate this sentence into Spanish?

→  ¿Puedes traducir esta frase al español?

TRANSPORT   /ˈtrænspɔːrt/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el transporte; transportar

Meaning:  the movement of people or goods from one place to another

Example:  Public transport in this city is very reliable.

→  El transporte público en esta ciudad es muy fiable.

Note:  American English often says "transportation" for the noun.

TREATMENT   /ˈtriːtmənt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el tratamiento

Meaning:  medical care given to a patient; the way someone is treated

Example:  He's receiving treatment for his back pain.

→  Está recibiendo tratamiento para el dolor de espalda.

TRIP   /trɪp/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el viaje

Meaning:  a journey to a place, usually returning afterward

Example:  We're planning a trip to Portugal in June.

→  Estamos planeando un viaje a Portugal en junio.

TRUST   /trʌst/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  confiar en; la confianza

Meaning:  to believe that someone is honest or reliable

Example:  I trust her completely.

→  Confío completamente en ella.

UNFRIENDLY   /ʌnˈfrɛndli/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  antipático/a; poco amistoso/a

Meaning:  not friendly or welcoming

Example:  The staff there were surprisingly unfriendly.

→  El personal allí fue sorprendentemente antipático.

UNIVERSITY   /ˌjuːnɪˈvɜːrsəti/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la universidad

Meaning:  a place of higher education where people study for degrees

Example:  She's studying biology at university.

→  Ella estudia biología en la universidad.

VACATION   /veɪˈkeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  las vacaciones

Meaning:  a period of rest or travel away from work or school

Example:  We're going on vacation to Mexico next month.

→  Nos vamos de vacaciones a México el mes que viene.

Common:  go on vacation

Note:  British English usually says "holiday" instead.

VICTIM   /ˈvɪktɪm/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la víctima

Meaning:  a person harmed or hurt as a result of a crime or accident

Example:  The victims of the accident were taken to hospital.

→  Las víctimas del accidente fueron trasladadas al hospital.

VILLAGE   /ˈvɪlɪdʒ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el pueblo; la aldea

Meaning:  a small community in the countryside, smaller than a town

Example:  They live in a peaceful mountain village.

→  Viven en un pueblo tranquilo de montaña.

WALL   /wɔːl/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la pared; el muro

Meaning:  a vertical structure that divides or encloses an area

Example:  They hung a big painting on the wall.

→  Colgaron un cuadro grande en la pared.

WARN   /wɔːrn/  •  verb  •  A2

Spanish:  advertir

Meaning:  to tell someone about a possible danger or problem in advance

Example:  I warned him the road was icy.

→  Le advertí que la carretera estaba helada.

WASTE   /weɪst/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  desperdiciar; malgastar; el desperdicio

Meaning:  to use something carelessly or without a good result; material thrown away

Example:  Don't waste your money on things you don't need.

→  No malgastes tu dinero en cosas que no necesitas.

Common:  waste time

WEBSITE   /ˈwɛbsaɪt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el sitio web; la página web

Meaning:  a set of connected pages available on the internet

Example:  You can find all the information on our website.

→  Puedes encontrar toda la información en nuestra página web.

WEIGHT   /weɪt/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el peso

Meaning:  how heavy someone or something is

Example:  The suitcase was over the weight limit.

→  La maleta superaba el límite de peso.

Common:  lose weight

WIDTH   /wɪdθ/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  el ancho; la anchura

Meaning:  the distance from one side of something to the other

Example:  What's the width of this table?

→  ¿Cuál es el ancho de esta mesa?

WILDLIFE   /ˈwaɪldlaɪf/  •  noun  •  A2

Spanish:  la fauna; la vida silvestre

Meaning:  wild animals living in their natural environment

Example:  The national park protects local wildlife.

→  El parque nacional protege la fauna local.

WISE   /waɪz/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  sabio/a

Meaning:  showing good judgment based on experience and knowledge

Example:  My grandmother always gives wise advice.

→  Mi abuela siempre da consejos sabios.

WITNESS   /ˈwɪtnəs/  •  noun; verb  •  A2

Spanish:  el testigo; la testigo; presenciar

Meaning:  a person who sees an event happen; to see something happen

Example:  A witness described the accident to the police.

→  Un testigo describió el accidente a la policía.

WORRIED   /ˈwɜːrid/  •  adjective  •  A2

Spanish:  preocupado/a

Meaning:  feeling anxious about a problem or possible danger

Example:  She's worried about her upcoming exams.

→  Ella está preocupada por sus próximos exámenes.

Common:  worried about

WORRY   /ˈwɜːri/  •  verb; noun  •  A2

Spanish:  preocupar; preocuparse; la preocupación

Meaning:  to feel anxious about something; a cause of anxiety

Example:  Don't worry, everything will be fine.

→  No te preocupes, todo saldrá bien.

WRONGLY   /ˈrɔːŋli/  •  adverb  •  A2

Spanish:  incorrectamente; erróneamente

Meaning:  in a mistaken or incorrect way

Example:  He was wrongly accused of lying.

→  Se le acusó erróneamente de mentir.

# B1 Vocabulary

Intermediate vocabulary — 469 words, alphabetical.

ABSOLUTELY   /ˈæbsəluːtli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  totalmente; absolutamente

Meaning:  completely; used to strongly agree with something

Example:  I absolutely love this city.

→  Me encanta totalmente esta ciudad.

ACCOMPLISH   /əˈkɒmplɪʃ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  lograr; conseguir

Meaning:  to succeed in doing something, often after effort

Example:  She accomplished a lot during her first year in the job.

→  Ella logró mucho durante su primer año en el trabajo.

Common:  accomplish a goal

ACHIEVE   /əˈtʃiːv/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  lograr; conseguir

Meaning:  to successfully do or reach something you worked for

Example:  She worked hard to achieve her goal.

→  Trabajó mucho para lograr su objetivo.

Common:  achieve a goal • achieve success

ACHIEVEMENT   /əˈtʃiːvmənt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el logro

Meaning:  something successfully accomplished, often through hard work

Example:  Winning the award was a huge achievement for the team.

→  Ganar el premio fue un gran logro para el equipo.

ACQUIRE   /əˈkwaɪər/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  adquirir

Meaning:  to obtain or get something, often gradually

Example:  He acquired most of his skills on the job.

→  Él adquirió la mayoría de sus habilidades en el trabajo.

ACTUALLY   /ˈæktʃuəli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  en realidad; de hecho

Meaning:  used to say what is really true, especially when it's surprising

Example:  Actually, I've never been to Paris.

→  En realidad, nunca he estado en París.

Note:  False friend: doesn't mean "actualmente" (currently).

ADAPT   /əˈdæpt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  adaptarse

Meaning:  to change your behavior to suit a new situation

Example:  It took a while to adapt to the new schedule.

→  Tardé un tiempo en adaptarme al nuevo horario.

Common:  adapt to

ADJUST   /əˈdʒʌst/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  ajustar; adaptarse

Meaning:  to change something slightly to make it fit better or work correctly

Example:  You may need to adjust the volume.

→  Puede que necesites ajustar el volumen.

ADMIT   /ədˈmɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  admitir; reconocer

Meaning:  to agree, often reluctantly, that something is true

Example:  He admitted that he had made a mistake.

→  Admitió que había cometido un error.

ADVANTAGE   /ədˈvæntɪdʒ/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la ventaja

Meaning:  something that helps you succeed or gives you a better chance

Example:  Speaking two languages gives her an advantage at work.

→  Hablar dos idiomas le da una ventaja en el trabajo.

Common:  take advantage of

ADVISE   /ədˈvaɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  aconsejar

Meaning:  to give someone your opinion about what they should do

Example:  The doctor advised her to rest for a week.

→  El médico le aconsejó descansar una semana.

Note:  Note the spelling difference from the noun "advice".

AFFORD   /əˈfɔːrd/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  permitirse (pagar algo)

Meaning:  to have enough money or time for something

Example:  We can't afford a new car right now.

→  Ahora mismo no podemos permitirnos un coche nuevo.

Common:  can afford

AMBITION   /æmˈbɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la ambición

Meaning:  a strong desire to achieve something

Example:  Her ambition is to open her own restaurant one day.

→  Su ambición es abrir su propio restaurante algún día.

ANALYZE   /ˈænəlaɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  analizar

Meaning:  to examine something carefully to understand it better

Example:  The team analyzed the results before making a decision.

→  El equipo analizó los resultados antes de tomar una decisión.

Note:  British spelling: "analyse".

ANTICIPATE   /ænˈtɪsɪpeɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  anticipar; prever

Meaning:  to expect something and prepare for it

Example:  We anticipate a busy weekend at the store.

→  Anticipamos un fin de semana ocupado en la tienda.

APOLOGIZE   /əˈpɒlədʒaɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  disculparse

Meaning:  to say sorry for something you did wrong

Example:  He apologized for being late.

→  Se disculpó por llegar tarde.

Common:  apologize for

Note:  British spelling: "apologise".

APPARENTLY   /əˈpærəntli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  al parecer; por lo visto

Meaning:  used to say something seems to be true, based on what you've heard

Example:  Apparently, the shop closed down last month.

→  Al parecer, la tienda cerró el mes pasado.

APPRECIATE   /əˈpriːʃieɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  agradecer; apreciar

Meaning:  to be grateful for something, or to recognize its value

Example:  I really appreciate your help with this.

→  Agradezco mucho tu ayuda con esto.

APPROPRIATE   /əˈproʊpriət/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  apropiado/a; adecuado/a

Meaning:  suitable or correct for a particular situation

Example:  That joke wasn't really appropriate for the meeting.

→  Esa broma no fue muy apropiada para la reunión.

APPROVE   /əˈpruːv/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  aprobar

Meaning:  to officially agree to something, or to think something is good

Example:  The manager approved the new budget.

→  El gerente aprobó el nuevo presupuesto.

Common:  approve of

ARGUE   /ˈɑːrɡjuː/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  discutir; argumentar

Meaning:  to disagree with someone, often angrily; to give reasons for an opinion

Example:  They argued about money the whole trip.

→  Discutieron por dinero todo el viaje.

ASSUME   /əˈsuːm/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  suponer

Meaning:  to believe something is true without checking

Example:  I assumed you already knew about the change.

→  Supuse que ya sabías del cambio.

ASSURE   /əˈʃʊr/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  asegurar

Meaning:  to tell someone something is true in order to remove their doubt

Example:  I can assure you the food is excellent here.

→  Te puedo asegurar que la comida aquí es excelente.

ATMOSPHERE   /ˈætməsfɪr/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el ambiente; la atmósfera

Meaning:  the mood or feeling of a place; the air surrounding the Earth

Example:  The restaurant has a really cozy atmosphere.

→  El restaurante tiene un ambiente muy acogedor.

ATTACH   /əˈtætʃ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  adjuntar; sujetar

Meaning:  to connect or fasten one thing to another

Example:  I've attached the file to this email.

→  He adjuntado el archivo a este correo.

ATTEMPT   /əˈtɛmpt/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  intentar; el intento

Meaning:  to try to do something difficult

Example:  She attempted to fix the printer herself.

→  Ella intentó arreglar la impresora ella misma.

ATTRACT   /əˈtrækt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  atraer

Meaning:  to make someone interested in or want to go somewhere

Example:  The festival attracts thousands of visitors every year.

→  El festival atrae a miles de visitantes cada año.

AUTHORITY   /əˈθɔːrəti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la autoridad

Meaning:  the power to give orders or make decisions; a government body

Example:  The local authority is responsible for the park's maintenance.

→  La autoridad local es responsable del mantenimiento del parque.

AVAILABLE   /əˈveɪləbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  disponible

Meaning:  able to be obtained, used, or seen

Example:  Is this table available for tonight?

→  ¿Está disponible esta mesa para esta noche?

AVOID   /əˈvɔɪd/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  evitar

Meaning:  to stay away from something, or prevent something from happening

Example:  We left early to avoid the traffic.

→  Salimos temprano para evitar el tráfico.

AWARE   /əˈwɛr/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  consciente

Meaning:  knowing that something exists or is happening

Example:  Are you aware of the new office policy?

→  ¿Eres consciente de la nueva política de la oficina?

Common:  aware of

BACKGROUND   /ˈbækɡraʊnd/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el fondo; los antecedentes

Meaning:  a person's past experiences; the area behind the main subject in an image

Example:  She comes from a musical background.

→  Ella viene de un entorno familiar musical.

BALANCE   /ˈbæləns/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el equilibrio; equilibrar

Meaning:  a stable state where things are in the right proportion; to keep something steady

Example:  It's hard to find a balance between work and family.

→  Es difícil encontrar un equilibrio entre el trabajo y la familia.

BASICALLY   /ˈbeɪsɪkli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  básicamente

Meaning:  used to give the most important or simple explanation of something

Example:  Basically, we need more time to finish the project.

→  Básicamente, necesitamos más tiempo para terminar el proyecto.

BEHAVE   /bɪˈheɪv/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  comportarse

Meaning:  to act in a particular way

Example:  The children behaved really well at the restaurant.

→  Los niños se comportaron muy bien en el restaurante.

BELIEVE   /bɪˈliːv/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  creer

Meaning:  to think something is true, or to have faith in something

Example:  I believe she's telling the truth.

→  Creo que ella está diciendo la verdad.

BELONG   /bɪˈlɔːŋ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  pertenecer

Meaning:  to be the property of someone, or to be a member of a group

Example:  This book belongs to my sister.

→  Este libro le pertenece a mi hermana.

Common:  belong to

BENEFIT   /ˈbɛnəfɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el beneficio; beneficiar; beneficiarse

Meaning:  an advantage gained from something; to gain an advantage

Example:  Regular exercise has many health benefits.

→  El ejercicio regular tiene muchos beneficios para la salud.

BLAME   /bleɪm/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  culpar; la culpa

Meaning:  to say or think that someone is responsible for something bad

Example:  Don't blame yourself for what happened.

→  No te culpes por lo que pasó.

BOAST   /boʊst/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  presumir; alardear

Meaning:  to talk with too much pride about your own achievements

Example:  He loves to boast about his new car.

→  A él le encanta presumir de su coche nuevo.

BOTHER   /ˈbɒðər/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  molestar; molestarse (en hacer algo)

Meaning:  to annoy someone, or to make the effort to do something

Example:  Sorry to bother you, but do you have a moment?

→  Perdona que te moleste, ¿tienes un momento?

BUDGET   /ˈbʌdʒɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el presupuesto; presupuestar

Meaning:  a plan for how money will be spent

Example:  We need to stick to our budget this month.

→  Tenemos que ajustarnos a nuestro presupuesto este mes.

CALCULATE   /ˈkælkjəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  calcular

Meaning:  to find an answer using math

Example:  Can you calculate the total cost for me?

→  ¿Puedes calcularme el costo total?

CAMPAIGN   /kæmˈpeɪn/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la campaña; hacer campaña

Meaning:  a series of planned actions to achieve a particular goal

Example:  The company launched a new advertising campaign.

→  La empresa lanzó una nueva campaña publicitaria.

CANDIDATE   /ˈkændɪdət/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el candidato; la candidata

Meaning:  a person applying for a job, or running in an election

Example:  There were five candidates for the position.

→  Había cinco candidatos para el puesto.

CAPABLE   /ˈkeɪpəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  capaz

Meaning:  able to do something well

Example:  She's fully capable of handling this project alone.

→  Ella es totalmente capaz de manejar este proyecto sola.

Common:  capable of

CAPACITY   /kəˈpæsəti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la capacidad

Meaning:  the maximum amount something can hold or produce; the ability to do something

Example:  The stadium has a capacity of fifty thousand people.

→  El estadio tiene una capacidad de cincuenta mil personas.

CAPTURE   /ˈkæptʃər/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  capturar

Meaning:  to catch someone or something; to record something, like an image or feeling

Example:  The photo really captures the beauty of the sunset.

→  La foto captura de verdad la belleza de la puesta de sol.

CATEGORY   /ˈkætəɡɔːri/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la categoría

Meaning:  a group of things that share similar features

Example:  This book falls into the mystery category.

→  Este libro entra en la categoría de misterio.

CAUSE   /kɔːz/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la causa; causar

Meaning:  the reason something happens; to make something happen

Example:  The cause of the delay is still unknown.

→  La causa del retraso todavía se desconoce.

CHALLENGE   /ˈtʃælɪndʒ/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el desafío; el reto; desafiar

Meaning:  something that requires effort and determination; to question something

Example:  Learning a new language is a real challenge.

→  Aprender un idioma nuevo es un verdadero desafío.

CIRCUMSTANCE   /ˈsɜːrkəmstæns/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la circunstancia

Meaning:  a fact or condition connected with an event or situation

Example:  Under different circumstances, we might have said yes.

→  En circunstancias diferentes, quizás habríamos dicho que sí.

CLAIM   /kleɪm/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  afirmar; reclamar; la afirmación

Meaning:  to say something is true; to formally ask for something you believe is yours

Example:  He claims he never received the email.

→  Él afirma que nunca recibió el correo.

CLARIFY   /ˈklærɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  aclarar

Meaning:  to make something clearer or easier to understand

Example:  Could you clarify what you mean by that?

→  ¿Podrías aclarar qué quieres decir con eso?

COMBINE   /kəmˈbaɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  combinar

Meaning:  to join two or more things together

Example:  You can combine both flavors in one dish.

→  Puedes combinar ambos sabores en un plato.

COMMENT   /ˈkɒmɛnt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el comentario; comentar

Meaning:  a spoken or written opinion or observation

Example:  She left a nice comment on my photo.

→  Ella dejó un comentario bonito en mi foto.

COMMIT   /kəˈmɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  comprometerse; cometer

Meaning:  to promise to do something; to do something wrong, like a crime

Example:  She's committed to finishing the course this year.

→  Ella está comprometida a terminar el curso este año.

Common:  commit to

COMMITMENT   /kəˈmɪtmənt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el compromiso

Meaning:  a promise or strong dedication to something

Example:  Marriage requires real commitment from both people.

→  El matrimonio requiere un verdadero compromiso de ambas personas.

COMMUNICATE   /kəˈmjuːnɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  comunicar; comunicarse

Meaning:  to share information, ideas, or feelings with others

Example:  It's important to communicate clearly at work.

→  Es importante comunicarse con claridad en el trabajo.

COMMUNITY   /kəˈmjuːnəti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la comunidad

Meaning:  a group of people living in the same area or sharing common interests

Example:  The whole community helped rebuild the school.

→  Toda la comunidad ayudó a reconstruir la escuela.

COMPETE   /kəmˈpiːt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  competir

Meaning:  to try to win or be more successful than others

Example:  Ten teams will compete in the final round.

→  Diez equipos competirán en la ronda final.

COMPETITIVE   /kəmˈpɛtətɪv/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  competitivo/a

Meaning:  eager to win or succeed; offering good value compared to others

Example:  He's always been very competitive in sports.

→  Él siempre ha sido muy competitivo en los deportes.

COMPLAIN   /kəmˈpleɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  quejarse

Meaning:  to say you are unhappy or dissatisfied about something

Example:  She complained about the noise from the street.

→  Ella se quejó del ruido de la calle.

Common:  complain about

COMPLEX   /kəmˈplɛks/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  complejo/a

Meaning:  made of many connected parts; difficult to understand

Example:  The instructions were more complex than I expected.

→  Las instrucciones eran más complejas de lo que esperaba.

COMPLICATE   /ˈkɒmplɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  complicar

Meaning:  to make something more difficult to deal with

Example:  Let's not complicate things any further.

→  No compliquemos más las cosas.

CONCENTRATE   /ˈkɒnsəntreɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  concentrarse

Meaning:  to focus all your attention on one thing

Example:  I can't concentrate with all this noise.

→  No puedo concentrarme con todo este ruido.

Common:  concentrate on

CONCEPT   /ˈkɒnsɛpt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el concepto

Meaning:  a general idea or principle

Example:  The concept of time can be hard for children to understand.

→  El concepto del tiempo puede ser difícil de entender para los niños.

CONCERN   /kənˈsɜːrn/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la preocupación; preocupar; concernir

Meaning:  a feeling of worry; to affect or worry someone

Example:  Her main concern is finding a stable job.

→  Su principal preocupación es encontrar un trabajo estable.

CONCLUDE   /kənˈkluːd/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  concluir

→  El informe concluye que se necesita más financiación.

CONCLUSION   /kənˈkluːʒən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la conclusión

Meaning:  a final decision or opinion reached after thinking; the end of something

→  Llegamos a la conclusión de que necesitábamos más tiempo.

CONDITION   /kənˈdɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

→  El coche está en excelente estado.

CONFERENCE   /ˈkɒnfərəns/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la conferencia

CONFIDENCE   /ˈkɒnfɪdəns/  •  noun  •  B1

CONFIRM   /kənˈfɜːrm/  •  verb  •  B1

CONFLICT   /ˈkɒnflɪkt/  •  noun  •  B1

CONFRONT   /kənˈfrʌnt/  •  verb  •  B1

CONGRATULATE   /kənˈɡrætʃəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

CONNECT   /kəˈnɛkt/  •  verb  •  B1

CONSCIOUS   /ˈkɒnʃəs/  •  adjective  •  B1

CONSEQUENCE   /ˈkɒnsəkwəns/  •  noun  •  B1

CONSIDER   /kənˈsɪdər/  •  verb  •  B1

CONSIST   /kənˈsɪst/  •  verb  •  B1

CONSISTENT   /kənˈsɪstənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

CONSTANT   /ˈkɒnstənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

CONSTRUCT   /kənˈstrʌkt/  •  verb  •  B1

→  Están construyendo un puente nuevo sobre el río.

CONSULT   /kənˈsʌlt/  •  verb  •  B1

CONTACT   /ˈkɒntækt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

CONTAIN   /kənˈteɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

CONTRACT   /ˈkɒntrækt/  •  noun  •  B1

CONTRAST   /ˈkɒntræst/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

→  Hay un gran contraste entre los dos barrios.

CONTRIBUTE   /kənˈtrɪbjuːt/  •  verb  •  B1

CONTRIBUTION   /ˌkɒntrɪˈbjuːʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

CONTROVERSY   /ˈkɒntrəvɜːrsi/  •  noun  •  B1

Example:  The new policy caused a lot of controversy.

CONVENIENCE   /kənˈviːniəns/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la comodidad; la conveniencia

→  Me encanta la comodidad de comprar en línea.

CONVENIENT   /kənˈviːniənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  conveniente; cómodo/a

CONVINCE   /kənˈvɪns/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  convencer

Example:  It took a while to convince her to join us.

COOPERATE   /koʊˈɒpəreɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

COPE   /koʊp/  •  verb  •  B1

CORRECT   /kəˈrɛkt/  •  adjective; verb  •  B1

CRISIS   /ˈkraɪsɪs/  •  noun  •  B1

CRITICISM   /ˈkrɪtɪsɪzəm/  •  noun  •  B1

CRITICIZE   /ˈkrɪtɪsaɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

CRUCIAL   /ˈkruːʃəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

CURE   /kjʊr/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

DEAL   /diːl/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

DEBATE   /dɪˈbeɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

DECADE   /ˈdɛkeɪd/  •  noun  •  B1

DECEIVE   /dɪˈsiːv/  •  verb  •  B1

DEFINE   /dɪˈfaɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

DELAY   /dɪˈleɪ/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

DEMAND   /dɪˈmænd/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

DEPEND   /dɪˈpɛnd/  •  verb  •  B1

DESERVE   /dɪˈzɜːrv/  •  verb  •  B1

DETERMINE   /dɪˈtɜːrmɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

DEVOTE   /dɪˈvoʊt/  •  verb  •  B1

DIFFER   /ˈdɪfər/  •  verb  •  B1

DISCIPLINE   /ˈdɪsəplɪn/  •  noun  •  B1

DONATE   /ˈdoʊneɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

DOUBT   /daʊt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

EARN   /ɜːrn/  •  verb  •  B1

ECONOMY   /ɪˈkɒnəmi/  •  noun  •  B1

EFFICIENCY   /ɪˈfɪʃənsi/  •  noun  •  B1

EFFICIENT   /ɪˈfɪʃənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

ELEMENT   /ˈɛlɪmənt/  •  noun  •  B1

ELIMINATE   /ɪˈlɪmɪneɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

EMPHASIS   /ˈɛmfəsɪs/  •  noun  •  B1

EMPHASIZE   /ˈɛmfəsaɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

EMPLOYMENT   /ɪmˈplɔɪmənt/  •  noun  •  B1

ENCOURAGE   /ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/  •  verb  •  B1

Meaning:  to give someone support or confidence to do something

ENDURE   /ɪnˈdʊr/  •  verb  •  B1

ENFORCE   /ɪnˈfɔːrs/  •  verb  •  B1

ENORMOUS   /ɪˈnɔːrməs/  •  adjective  •  B1

ENSURE   /ɪnˈʃʊr/  •  verb  •  B1

ENTERTAIN   /ˌɛntərˈteɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

ENTHUSIASM   /ɪnˈθuːziæzəm/  •  noun  •  B1

Meaning:  a strong feeling of excitement and interest

Example:  She talked about her new job with real enthusiasm.

EQUIPMENT   /ɪˈkwɪpmənt/  •  noun  •  B1

Example:  We need to buy new camping equipment.

ESSENTIAL   /ɪˈsɛnʃəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

ESTABLISH   /ɪˈstæblɪʃ/  •  verb  •  B1

Example:  The company was established in 1995.

→  La empresa se estableció en 1995.

ESTIMATE   /ˈɛstɪmeɪt/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

EVALUATE   /ɪˈvæljueɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

EVIDENCE   /ˈɛvɪdəns/  •  noun  •  B1

Meaning:  facts or information that show whether something is true

EXAMINE   /ɪɡˈzæmɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

EXCEED   /ɪkˈsiːd/  •  verb  •  B1

→  Los resultados superaron nuestras expectativas.

EXIST   /ɪɡˈzɪst/  •  verb  •  B1

EXPAND   /ɪkˈspænd/  •  verb  •  B1

EXPENSE   /ɪkˈspɛns/  •  noun  •  B1

EXPERIMENT   /ɪkˈspɛrɪmənt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

EXPERT   /ˈɛkspɜːrt/  •  noun; adjective  •  B1

EXTEND   /ɪkˈstɛnd/  •  verb  •  B1

Meaning:  to make something longer, larger, or last longer

→  Decidimos ampliar nuestra estancia dos días.

EXTREME   /ɪkˈstriːm/  •  adjective  •  B1

Meaning:  very great in degree; far from what is usual

Example:  The desert has extreme temperatures.

EXTREMELY   /ɪkˈstriːmli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  extremadamente

Meaning:  to a very great degree

Example:  The exam was extremely difficult this year.

→  El examen fue extremadamente difícil este año.

FACTOR   /ˈfæktər/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el factor

→  El precio fue un factor importante en nuestra decisión.

FAIR   /fɛr/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  justo/a

Meaning:  treating people equally and reasonably

→  No es justo que ella tenga que hacer todo el trabajo.

FAITH   /feɪθ/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la fe; la confianza

Meaning:  strong trust in someone or something; religious belief

Example:  I have complete faith in your judgment.

→  Tengo total confianza en tu criterio.

FAMILIAR   /fəˈmɪljər/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  familiar; conocido/a

Meaning:  well-known or easily recognized

Example:  That song sounds familiar to me.

→  Esa canción me suena familiar.

Common:  familiar with

FASCINATE   /ˈfæsɪneɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  fascinar

Meaning:  to attract and interest someone very strongly

Example:  Ancient history really fascinates him.

FAULT   /fɔːlt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la culpa; el defecto

FEATURE   /ˈfiːtʃər/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la característica; la función

Meaning:  a distinctive part or quality of something

Example:  This phone has some great new features.

→  Este móvil tiene algunas funciones nuevas geniales.

FIGURE   /ˈfɪɡjər/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la cifra; la figura; suponer

→  Las cifras de ventas fueron más altas de lo esperado este año.

Common:  figure out

FLEXIBILITY   /ˌflɛksəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la flexibilidad

Meaning:  the ability to change or adapt easily

Example:  Working from home gives me more flexibility.

→  Trabajar desde casa me da más flexibilidad.

FLEXIBLE   /ˈflɛksəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  flexible

Meaning:  able to change or adapt easily to new conditions

Example:  My manager is pretty flexible about work hours.

→  Mi jefe es bastante flexible con el horario laboral.

FOCUS   /ˈfoʊkəs/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  enfocarse; concentrarse; el enfoque

Meaning:  to give all your attention to one thing

→  Enfoquémonos en terminar esta tarea primero.

FORCE   /fɔːrs/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la fuerza; forzar; obligar

Meaning:  physical strength or power; to make someone do something against their will

Example:  The wind blew the door open with great force.

→  El viento abrió la puerta con mucha fuerza.

FORTUNATELY   /ˈfɔːrtʃənətli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  afortunadamente

Meaning:  used to say that something good happened

→  Afortunadamente, nadie resultó herido en el accidente.

FREQUENT   /ˈfriːkwənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  frecuente

Meaning:  happening often

Example:  There have been frequent delays on this train line.

→  Ha habido retrasos frecuentes en esta línea de tren.

FREQUENTLY   /ˈfriːkwəntli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Meaning:  often; many times

→  Ella viaja con frecuencia por trabajo.

FUNCTION   /ˈfʌŋkʃən/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la función; funcionar

Meaning:  the purpose of something; to work or operate

Example:  The main function of this button is to restart the device.

→  La función principal de este botón es reiniciar el dispositivo.

GAIN   /ɡeɪn/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  ganar; obtener; la ganancia

Meaning:  to get more of something, such as knowledge or confidence

→  Ella ganó mucha experiencia durante sus prácticas.

GATHER   /ˈɡæðər/  •  verb  •  B1

GENERATE   /ˈdʒɛnəreɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

→  Los paneles solares generan electricidad a partir de la luz solar.

GENUINE   /ˈdʒɛnjuɪn/  •  adjective  •  B1

Meaning:  real and sincere, not fake

GOAL   /ɡoʊl/  •  noun  •  B1

GOVERN   /ˈɡʌvərn/  •  verb  •  B1

→  El país ha sido gobernado por el mismo partido durante una década.

GRADUAL   /ˈɡrædʒuəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  gradual

Meaning:  happening slowly over time, rather than suddenly

Example:  There's been a gradual increase in prices this year.

→  Ha habido un aumento gradual de los precios este año.

GRADUALLY   /ˈɡrædʒuəli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  gradualmente; poco a poco

→  Ella se fue acostumbrando poco a poco al nuevo horario.

GUILTY   /ˈɡɪlti/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  culpable

Meaning:  having done something wrong; feeling responsible for a mistake

HANDLE   /ˈhændəl/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

HARDLY   /ˈhɑːrdli/  •  adverb  •  B1

HESITATE   /ˈhɛzɪteɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  dudar; vacilar

Example:  Don't hesitate to call if you need anything.

→  No dudes en llamar si necesitas algo.

IDEAL   /aɪˈdiːəl/  •  adjective; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  ideal

Meaning:  perfect or the best possible for a particular purpose

IDENTICAL   /aɪˈdɛntɪkəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  idéntico/a

Meaning:  exactly the same

IDENTIFY   /aɪˈdɛntɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B1

Meaning:  to recognize or determine what something or someone is

→  El testigo identificó al hombre de la foto.

IGNORE   /ɪɡˈnɔːr/  •  verb  •  B1

ILLUSTRATE   /ˈɪləstreɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

IMMEDIATE   /ɪˈmiːdiət/  •  adjective  •  B1

IMPRESS   /ɪmˈprɛs/  •  verb  •  B1

INCIDENT   /ˈɪnsɪdənt/  •  noun  •  B1

INCLUDE   /ɪnˈkluːd/  •  verb  •  B1

INCREASE   /ɪnˈkriːs/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

INCREASINGLY   /ɪnˈkriːsɪŋli/  •  adverb  •  B1

INDEPENDENT   /ˌɪndɪˈpɛndənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

INDICATE   /ˈɪndɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

INDIVIDUAL   /ˌɪndɪˈvɪdʒuəl/  •  noun; adjective  •  B1

Example:  Each individual has their own way of learning.

INEVITABLE   /ɪnˈɛvɪtəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

INFLUENCE   /ˈɪnfluəns/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la influencia; influir en

INFORM   /ɪnˈfɔːrm/  •  verb  •  B1

INHERIT   /ɪnˈhɛrɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

INITIAL   /ɪˈnɪʃəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  inicial

INSIST   /ɪnˈsɪst/  •  verb  •  B1

INSPECT   /ɪnˈspɛkt/  •  verb  •  B1

INSTALL   /ɪnˈstɔːl/  •  verb  •  B1

INSTITUTION   /ˌɪnstɪˈtuːʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

INSTRUCT   /ɪnˈstrʌkt/  •  verb  •  B1

INSULT   /ɪnˈsʌlt/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

INTEND   /ɪnˈtɛnd/  •  verb  •  B1

INTENSE   /ɪnˈtɛns/  •  adjective  •  B1

INTENTION   /ɪnˈtɛnʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

INTERFERE   /ˌɪntərˈfɪr/  •  verb  •  B1

Example:  I don't want to interfere in their decision.

→  No quiero interferir en su decisión.

INTERPRET   /ɪnˈtɜːrprɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Example:  It's hard to interpret his silence.

INTERRUPT   /ˌɪntəˈrʌpt/  •  verb  •  B1

INVEST   /ɪnˈvɛst/  •  verb  •  B1

INVESTIGATE   /ɪnˈvɛstɪɡeɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

JUSTICE   /ˈdʒʌstɪs/  •  noun  •  B1

JUSTIFY   /ˈdʒʌstɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B1

KNOWLEDGE   /ˈnɒlɪdʒ/  •  noun  •  B1

Example:  She has a deep knowledge of European history.

LABOR   /ˈleɪbər/  •  noun  •  B1

→  El proyecto requirió mucho trabajo manual.

LAUNCH   /lɔːntʃ/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  lanzar; el lanzamiento

Meaning:  to start something new, such as a product or project

→  La empresa lanzó un nuevo móvil la semana pasada.

LEAD   /liːd/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  liderar; dirigir; conducir a; la ventaja

Example:  She leads a team of twelve people.

LEGAL   /ˈliːɡəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  legal

LEGISLATION   /ˌlɛdʒɪsˈleɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la legislación

LIBERTY   /ˈlɪbərti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la libertad

Meaning:  the freedom to live and act as you choose

Example:  The statue represents liberty and hope.

→  La estatua representa la libertad y la esperanza.

LIKELY   /ˈlaɪkli/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  probable

Meaning:  expected to happen; probable

→  Es probable que llueva más tarde hoy.

LIMIT   /ˈlɪmɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el límite; limitar

Meaning:  the greatest amount or extent allowed; to keep something within limits

Example:  There's a speed limit of thirty on this street.

→  Hay un límite de velocidad de treinta en esta calle.

LIMITATION   /ˌlɪmɪˈteɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la limitación

Meaning:  something that restricts or controls what can be done

LOCATE   /ˈloʊkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Meaning:  to find the exact place where something is

→  Finalmente localizamos el paquete perdido.

LOGIC   /ˈlɒdʒɪk/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la lógica

Meaning:  a reasonable and sensible way of thinking about something

Example:  I don't understand the logic behind that decision.

→  No entiendo la lógica detrás de esa decisión.

LOYALTY   /ˈlɔɪəlti/  •  noun  •  B1

Meaning:  strong support and faithfulness toward a person or cause

Example:  She showed great loyalty to her friends.

→  Ella mostró gran lealtad hacia sus amigos.

MAINLY   /ˈmeɪnli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  principalmente

Example:  The class is mainly made up of beginners.

→  La clase está compuesta principalmente por principiantes.

MAINTAIN   /meɪnˈteɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

Example:  It's important to maintain a healthy diet.

→  Es importante mantener una dieta saludable.

MAJORITY   /məˈdʒɔːrəti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la mayoría

Meaning:  more than half of a group

Example:  The majority of students passed the exam.

→  La mayoría de los estudiantes aprobó el examen.

MANUFACTURE   /ˌmænjəˈfæktʃər/  •  verb  •  B1

Meaning:  to make goods in large quantities using machines

Example:  The factory manufactures car parts.

→  La fábrica fabrica piezas de coche.

MATURE   /məˈtʃʊr/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  maduro/a

Meaning:  fully developed, or behaving in a sensible, adult way

Example:  She's very mature for her age.

→  Ella es muy madura para su edad.

MEASURE   /ˈmɛʒər/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

MINOR   /ˈmaɪnər/  •  adjective; noun  •  B1

Meaning:  not very important or serious

Example:  It's just a minor problem, nothing to worry about.

MINORITY   /maɪˈnɔːrəti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la minoría

Example:  Only a minority of people voted against the plan.

MISLEAD   /mɪsˈliːd/  •  verb  •  B1

Example:  The advertisement misled a lot of customers.

→  El anuncio engañó a muchos clientes.

MORAL   /ˈmɔːrəl/  •  adjective; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  moral; la moraleja

Example:  She always tries to do the moral thing.

→  Ella siempre intenta hacer lo moralmente correcto.

MOTIVATE   /ˈmoʊtɪveɪt/  •  verb  •  B1
