Meaning:  to make someone want to do something

Example:  Good teachers know how to motivate their students.

→  Los buenos profesores saben cómo motivar a sus estudiantes.

MOTIVATION   /ˌmoʊtɪˈveɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la motivación

Meaning:  a reason or desire to do something

Example:  I need some motivation to start exercising again.

→  Necesito algo de motivación para volver a hacer ejercicio.

MUTUAL   /ˈmjuːtʃuəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  mutuo/a

Meaning:  felt or done equally by two or more people

Example:  They have mutual respect for each other.

→  Se tienen respeto mutuo.

NEARLY   /ˈnɪrli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  casi

Meaning:  almost; not quite

Example:  We're nearly finished with the project.

→  Casi hemos terminado el proyecto.

NEGATIVE   /ˈnɛɡətɪv/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  negativo/a

Meaning:  not good, hopeful, or positive

Example:  Try not to focus on the negative side of things.

→  Intenta no centrarte en el lado negativo de las cosas.

NEGOTIATE   /nɪˈɡoʊʃieɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  negociar

Meaning:  to discuss something in order to reach an agreement

Example:  They negotiated a better price for the car.

→  Negociaron un mejor precio por el coche.

NEUTRAL   /ˈnuːtrəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  neutral

Meaning:  not supporting either side in a disagreement or conflict

Example:  The country remained neutral during the war.

→  El país se mantuvo neutral durante la guerra.

NOTE   /noʊt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la nota; anotar; notar

Meaning:  a short written message or record; to pay attention to something

Example:  Please note that the office closes at five.

→  Ten en cuenta que la oficina cierra a las cinco.

OBJECT   /ˈɒbdʒɪkt/ (noun); /əbˈdʒɛkt/ (verb)  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el objeto; oponerse

Meaning:  a physical thing; to express disagreement

Example:  There's a strange object on the table.

→  Hay un objeto extraño sobre la mesa.

Common:  object to

OBJECTIVE   /əbˈdʒɛktɪv/  •  noun; adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  el objetivo; objetivo/a

Meaning:  a goal you are trying to achieve; based on facts, not feelings

Example:  Our main objective is to increase sales this quarter.

→  Nuestro objetivo principal es aumentar las ventas este trimestre.

OBSERVE   /əbˈzɜːrv/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  observar

Meaning:  to watch someone or something carefully

Example:  Scientists observed the animals in their natural habitat.

→  Los científicos observaron a los animales en su hábitat natural.

OBTAIN   /əbˈteɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  obtener

Meaning:  to get or acquire something

Example:  You need to obtain permission before entering.

→  Necesitas obtener permiso antes de entrar.

OBVIOUS   /ˈɒbviəs/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  obvio/a; evidente

Meaning:  easy to see or understand; clear

Example:  It was obvious that she was upset.

→  Era obvio que ella estaba molesta.

OBVIOUSLY   /ˈɒbviəsli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  obviamente

Meaning:  in a way that is easy to notice or understand

Example:  Obviously, we need to plan this more carefully.

→  Obviamente, necesitamos planear esto con más cuidado.

OCCASIONALLY   /əˈkeɪʒənəli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  ocasionalmente; de vez en cuando

Meaning:  sometimes, but not often

Example:  We occasionally eat out on weekends.

→  De vez en cuando comemos fuera los fines de semana.

OCCUR   /əˈkɜːr/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  ocurrir; suceder

Meaning:  to happen

Example:  The accident occurred late at night.

→  El accidente ocurrió tarde por la noche.

Common:  occur to

OPPORTUNITY   /ˌɒpərˈtuːnəti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la oportunidad

Meaning:  a chance to do something you want or need to do

Example:  This job is a great opportunity for her career.

→  Este trabajo es una gran oportunidad para su carrera.

OPPOSE   /əˈpoʊz/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  oponerse a

Meaning:  to disagree strongly with a plan or idea and try to stop it

Example:  Many residents opposed the new construction project.

→  Muchos residentes se opusieron al nuevo proyecto de construcción.

OPTIMISTIC   /ˌɒptɪˈmɪstɪk/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  optimista

Meaning:  hopeful about the future or the outcome of something

Example:  She's optimistic about finding a new job soon.

→  Ella es optimista sobre encontrar un nuevo trabajo pronto.

OPTION   /ˈɒpʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la opción

Meaning:  a choice between two or more possibilities

Example:  We have several options for dinner tonight.

→  Tenemos varias opciones para cenar esta noche.

OUTCOME   /ˈaʊtkʌm/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el resultado

Meaning:  the result of an action, event, or process

Example:  We're waiting to hear the outcome of the election.

→  Estamos esperando saber el resultado de las elecciones.

OVERALL   /ˌoʊvərˈɔːl/  •  adjective; adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  general; en general

Meaning:  including everything; considering everything together

Example:  Overall, the trip was a great success.

→  En general, el viaje fue un gran éxito.

OVERCOME   /ˌoʊvərˈkʌm/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  superar

Meaning:  to succeed in dealing with a problem or difficulty

Example:  She overcame her fear of flying.

→  Ella superó su miedo a volar.

Note:  Irregular: overcome → overcame → overcome.

OVERLOOK   /ˌoʊvərˈlʊk/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  pasar por alto; ignorar; tener vistas a

Meaning:  to fail to notice something; to have a view over a place from above

Example:  It's easy to overlook small details when you're in a hurry.

→  Es fácil pasar por alto pequeños detalles cuando tienes prisa.

PARTICIPANT   /pɑːrˈtɪsɪpənt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el participante; la participante

Meaning:  a person who takes part in an activity or event

Example:  There were fifty participants in the study.

→  Hubo cincuenta participantes en el estudio.

PARTICIPATE   /pɑːrˈtɪsɪpeɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  participar

Meaning:  to take part in an activity or event

Example:  All students are encouraged to participate in the discussion.

→  Se anima a todos los estudiantes a participar en el debate.

Common:  participate in

PARTICULARLY   /pərˈtɪkjələrli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  particularmente; especialmente

Meaning:  more than usual or more than others; especially

Example:  It's particularly cold this morning.

→  Hace especialmente frío esta mañana.

PARTLY   /ˈpɑːrtli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  en parte

Meaning:  to some extent, but not completely

Example:  The delay was partly due to bad weather.

→  El retraso se debió en parte al mal tiempo.

PERFORM   /pərˈfɔːrm/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  actuar; interpretar; realizar

Meaning:  to do a piece of work, task, or entertainment in front of others

Example:  The band performed for almost two hours.

→  La banda actuó durante casi dos horas.

PERMANENT   /ˈpɜːrmənənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  permanente

Meaning:  lasting forever or for a very long time

Example:  She finally got a permanent contract at work.

→  Finalmente consiguió un contrato permanente en el trabajo.

PERMIT   /pərˈmɪt/ (verb); /ˈpɜːrmɪt/ (noun)  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  permitir; el permiso

Meaning:  to allow something; an official document giving permission

Example:  Smoking is not permitted inside the building.

→  No se permite fumar dentro del edificio.

PERSPECTIVE   /pərˈspɛktɪv/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la perspectiva; el punto de vista

Meaning:  a particular way of thinking about or seeing something

Example:  Try to see it from her perspective.

→  Intenta verlo desde su perspectiva.

PERSUADE   /pərˈsweɪd/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  convencer; persuadir

Meaning:  to make someone agree to do something by giving good reasons

Example:  She persuaded him to try the new restaurant.

→  Ella lo convenció de probar el restaurante nuevo.

PHASE   /feɪz/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la fase; la etapa

Meaning:  a stage in a process of change or development

Example:  The project is now in its final phase.

→  El proyecto está ahora en su fase final.

POLICY   /ˈpɒləsi/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la política (norma); la póliza (seguros)

Meaning:  a plan of action agreed by a government or organization

Example:  The company changed its return policy last month.

→  La empresa cambió su política de devoluciones el mes pasado.

POPULATION   /ˌpɒpjəˈleɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la población

Meaning:  all the people living in a particular area

Example:  The population of the city has doubled in ten years.

→  La población de la ciudad se ha duplicado en diez años.

POSITIVE   /ˈpɒzətɪv/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  positivo/a

Meaning:  good, hopeful, or constructive; certain about something

Example:  Try to stay positive during difficult times.

→  Intenta mantenerte positivo durante los momentos difíciles.

POSSIBILITY   /ˌpɒsəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la posibilidad

Meaning:  a chance that something might happen or be true

Example:  There's a possibility we'll move next year.

→  Existe la posibilidad de que nos mudemos el año que viene.

POTENTIAL   /pəˈtɛnʃəl/  •  adjective; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  potencial

Meaning:  possible but not yet certain; the ability to develop in the future

Example:  He has a lot of potential as a musician.

→  Él tiene mucho potencial como músico.

POVERTY   /ˈpɒvərti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la pobreza

Meaning:  the state of being very poor

Example:  The program aims to reduce poverty in rural areas.

→  El programa busca reducir la pobreza en zonas rurales.

PRACTICAL   /ˈpræktɪkəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  práctico/a

Meaning:  suitable for real situations; concerned with actual experience

Example:  That's a very practical solution to the problem.

→  Esa es una solución muy práctica al problema.

PRESUMABLY   /prɪˈzuːməbli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  presumiblemente; se supone que

Meaning:  used to say you think something is probably true

Example:  Presumably, she's still on her way.

→  Se supone que todavía está de camino.

PREVENT   /prɪˈvɛnt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  prevenir; evitar

Meaning:  to stop something from happening

Example:  Regular checkups can help prevent health problems.

→  Los chequeos regulares pueden ayudar a prevenir problemas de salud.

Common:  prevent from

PRIMARY   /ˈpraɪmɛri/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  principal; primario/a

Meaning:  most important; first in order

Example:  Our primary goal is customer satisfaction.

→  Nuestro objetivo principal es la satisfacción del cliente.

PRIOR   /ˈpraɪər/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  previo/a; anterior

Meaning:  existing or happening before something else

Example:  You need prior experience for this job.

→  Necesitas experiencia previa para este trabajo.

Common:  prior to

PRIORITY   /praɪˈɔːrəti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la prioridad

Meaning:  something considered more important than other things

Example:  Family is her top priority.

→  La familia es su máxima prioridad.

PROBABLY   /ˈprɒbəbli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  probablemente

Meaning:  almost certainly; likely to be true

Example:  I'll probably be home late tonight.

→  Probablemente llegue tarde a casa esta noche.

PROCEDURE   /prəˈsiːdʒər/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el procedimiento

Meaning:  a series of actions done in a particular order

Example:  There's a strict procedure for handling complaints.

→  Hay un procedimiento estricto para gestionar las quejas.

PROCEED   /prəˈsiːd/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  proceder; continuar

Meaning:  to continue doing something, often after a pause

Example:  Let's proceed with the meeting.

→  Continuemos con la reunión.

PROCESS   /ˈprɒsɛs/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el proceso; procesar

Meaning:  a series of steps taken to achieve a result

Example:  The application process takes about two weeks.

→  El proceso de solicitud tarda unas dos semanas.

PROFESSIONAL   /prəˈfɛʃənəl/  •  adjective; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  profesional

Meaning:  relating to a job requiring training; a person with a skilled job

Example:  She always gives very professional advice.

→  Ella siempre da consejos muy profesionales.

PROFITABLE   /ˈprɒfɪtəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  rentable

Meaning:  making a financial profit

Example:  The new store has become very profitable.

→  La nueva tienda se ha vuelto muy rentable.

PROGRESS   /ˈprɒɡrɛs/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el progreso; progresar; avanzar

Meaning:  forward movement toward a goal; to improve or move forward

Example:  She's made great progress with her Spanish.

→  Ella ha hecho grandes progresos con su español.

PROJECT   /ˈprɒdʒɛkt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el proyecto

Meaning:  a planned piece of work with a specific goal

Example:  We're working on a new project at school.

→  Estamos trabajando en un nuevo proyecto en la escuela.

PROMOTE   /prəˈmoʊt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  promover; ascender

Meaning:  to encourage or support something; to give someone a higher position

Example:  She was promoted to manager last month.

→  Ella fue ascendida a gerente el mes pasado.

PROMPT   /prɒmpt/  •  adjective; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  rápido/a; puntual; provocar; motivar

Meaning:  done quickly, without delay; to cause someone to do something

Example:  Thank you for your prompt reply.

→  Gracias por tu rápida respuesta.

PROPER   /ˈprɒpər/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  apropiado/a; adecuado/a

Meaning:  correct, suitable, or accepted as right

Example:  You should get proper training before using this machine.

→  Deberías recibir una formación adecuada antes de usar esta máquina.

PROPORTION   /prəˈpɔːrʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la proporción

Meaning:  a part of a whole, or the relationship in size between things

Example:  A large proportion of the budget goes to salaries.

→  Una gran proporción del presupuesto va a los salarios.

PROPOSE   /prəˈpoʊz/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  proponer; proponer matrimonio

Meaning:  to suggest an idea or plan

Example:  She proposed a new schedule for the team.

→  Ella propuso un nuevo horario para el equipo.

PROSPECT   /ˈprɒspɛkt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la perspectiva; la posibilidad

Meaning:  the possibility of something happening in the future

Example:  The job offers good prospects for growth.

→  El trabajo ofrece buenas perspectivas de crecimiento.

PROVE   /pruːv/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  demostrar; probar

Meaning:  to show that something is true using facts or evidence

Example:  Can you prove that you were there?

→  ¿Puedes demostrar que estuviste allí?

Note:  Irregular: prove → proved → proved/proven.

PUNISH   /ˈpʌnɪʃ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  castigar

Meaning:  to make someone suffer a penalty for doing something wrong

Example:  The company was punished for breaking environmental laws.

→  La empresa fue castigada por incumplir las leyes ambientales.

PURPOSE   /ˈpɜːrpəs/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el propósito

Meaning:  the reason something is done or exists

Example:  What's the purpose of this meeting?

→  ¿Cuál es el propósito de esta reunión?

Common:  on purpose

PURSUE   /pərˈsuː/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  perseguir; continuar con

Meaning:  to continue trying to achieve a goal over a period of time

Example:  She decided to pursue a career in medicine.

→  Ella decidió continuar con una carrera en medicina.

QUALIFY   /ˈkwɒlɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  calificar; cualificarse; clasificarse

Meaning:  to have the right skills or experience for something; to reach a required standard

Example:  She qualified as a nurse two years ago.

→  Ella se cualificó como enfermera hace dos años.

QUOTE   /kwoʊt/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  citar; la cita; el presupuesto

Meaning:  to repeat words said by someone else; a price given for a job

Example:  Can you give me a quote for the repairs?

→  ¿Puedes darme un presupuesto para las reparaciones?

RARE   /rɛr/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  raro/a; poco común

Meaning:  not common or frequent

Example:  It's rare to see snow here in April.

→  Es poco común ver nieve aquí en abril.

RARELY   /ˈrɛrli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  raramente; rara vez

Meaning:  not often

Example:  He rarely eats meat these days.

→  Él come carne rara vez estos días.

RATIONAL   /ˈræʃənəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  racional

Meaning:  based on clear thinking and reason, not emotion

Example:  Try to make a rational decision, not an emotional one.

→  Intenta tomar una decisión racional, no emocional.

REACT   /riˈækt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  reaccionar

Meaning:  to behave or respond in a certain way because of something

Example:  She reacted calmly to the bad news.

→  Ella reaccionó con calma ante la mala noticia.

Common:  react to

REACTION   /riˈækʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la reacción

Meaning:  a response to something that happens

Example:  His reaction to the news surprised everyone.

→  Su reacción ante la noticia sorprendió a todos.

REALISTIC   /ˌriːəˈlɪstɪk/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  realista

Meaning:  seeing things as they really are; likely to happen

Example:  We need a realistic plan to finish this on time.

→  Necesitamos un plan realista para terminar esto a tiempo.

REASONABLE   /ˈriːzənəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  razonable

Meaning:  fair and sensible; acceptable

Example:  The price seems reasonable to me.

→  El precio me parece razonable.

RECOGNIZE   /ˈrɛkəɡnaɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  reconocer

Meaning:  to know someone or something because you've seen it before; to accept as true

Example:  I almost didn't recognize you with short hair.

→  Casi no te reconozco con el pelo corto.

Note:  British spelling: "recognise".

RECOMMEND   /ˌrɛkəˈmɛnd/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  recomendar

Meaning:  to suggest that someone or something is good or suitable

Example:  Can you recommend a good restaurant nearby?

→  ¿Puedes recomendarme un buen restaurante cerca?

RECOVER   /rɪˈkʌvər/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  recuperarse; recuperar

Meaning:  to return to a normal state after an illness or difficulty

Example:  She's still recovering from surgery.

→  Ella todavía se está recuperando de la cirugía.

REFER   /rɪˈfɜːr/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  referirse a

Meaning:  to mention or talk about someone or something

Example:  When I say "the team," I'm referring to the whole department.

→  Cuando digo "el equipo", me refiero a todo el departamento.

Common:  refer to

REFERENCE   /ˈrɛfərəns/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la referencia

Meaning:  a mention of something; a source of information

Example:  She made a reference to last year's event.

→  Ella hizo una referencia al evento del año pasado.

REFLECT   /rɪˈflɛkt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  reflejar; reflexionar

Meaning:  to show or represent something; to think carefully about something

Example:  The report reflects the views of the whole team.

→  El informe refleja las opiniones de todo el equipo.

REGARD   /rɪˈɡɑːrd/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  considerar; el respeto

Meaning:  to think of someone or something in a particular way

Example:  She's regarded as one of the best in her field.

→  Se la considera una de las mejores en su campo.

Common:  regard as

REGION   /ˈriːdʒən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la región

Meaning:  a large area of land, or a part of a country

Example:  This region is famous for its wine.

→  Esta región es famosa por su vino.

REGISTER   /ˈrɛdʒɪstər/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  registrar; inscribirse; el registro

Meaning:  to officially record a name or fact on a list

Example:  You need to register before the course starts.

→  Necesitas inscribirte antes de que empiece el curso.

REGRET   /rɪˈɡrɛt/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  lamentar; arrepentirse de; el arrepentimiento

Meaning:  to feel sorry about something you did or didn't do

Example:  I regret not traveling more when I was younger.

→  Lamento no haber viajado más cuando era joven.

REGULATION   /ˌrɛɡjəˈleɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la regulación; la norma

Meaning:  an official rule made by an authority

Example:  New safety regulations were introduced last year.

→  Se introdujeron nuevas normas de seguridad el año pasado.

REINFORCE   /ˌriːɪnˈfɔːrs/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  reforzar

Meaning:  to make something stronger or more effective

Example:  Practice reinforces what you learn in class.

→  La práctica refuerza lo que aprendes en clase.

REJECT   /rɪˈdʒɛkt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  rechazar

Meaning:  to refuse to accept, believe, or agree with something

Example:  They rejected our proposal without much explanation.

→  Rechazaron nuestra propuesta sin mucha explicación.

RELATE   /rɪˈleɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  relacionar; relacionarse

Meaning:  to show a connection between things; to understand someone's feelings

Example:  I really relate to what she said about starting over.

→  Me identifico mucho con lo que dijo sobre empezar de nuevo.

Common:  relate to

RELATIONSHIP   /rɪˈleɪʃənʃɪp/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la relación

Meaning:  the way two people or things are connected

Example:  They have a strong relationship built on trust.

→  Tienen una relación sólida basada en la confianza.

RELATIVELY   /ˈrɛlətɪvli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  relativamente

Meaning:  to a fairly large or small degree, compared to something else

Example:  The test was relatively easy this time.

→  El examen fue relativamente fácil esta vez.

RELEASE   /rɪˈliːs/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  lanzar; liberar; publicar; el lanzamiento

Meaning:  to make something available to the public; to set someone free

Example:  The band released a new album last week.

→  La banda lanzó un nuevo álbum la semana pasada.

RELEVANCE   /ˈrɛləvəns/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la relevancia

Meaning:  the quality of being important or related to the matter at hand

Example:  I don't see the relevance of this to our discussion.

→  No veo la relevancia de esto para nuestra discusión.

RELEVANT   /ˈrɛləvənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  relevante; pertinente

Meaning:  closely connected to the subject being discussed

Example:  That's not really relevant to what we're talking about.

→  Eso no es realmente relevante para lo que estamos hablando.

RELIABLE   /rɪˈlaɪəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  fiable; confiable

Meaning:  able to be trusted to do something well

Example:  This car has always been very reliable.

→  Este coche siempre ha sido muy fiable.

RELY   /rɪˈlaɪ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  depender de; confiar en

Meaning:  to depend on someone or something with trust

Example:  We rely on public transport to get to work.

→  Dependemos del transporte público para ir al trabajo.

Common:  rely on

REMAIN   /rɪˈmeɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  permanecer; quedar

Meaning:  to continue to be in the same state or place

Example:  The cause of the fire remains unknown.

→  La causa del incendio permanece desconocida.

REMARKABLE   /rɪˈmɑːrkəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  notable; extraordinario/a

Meaning:  unusual or impressive enough to be noticed

→  Hizo una recuperación notable después de la cirugía.

REMIND   /rɪˈmaɪnd/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  recordar (a alguien algo)

→  ¿Puedes recordarme que llame al banco mañana?

RENEW   /rɪˈnuː/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  renovar

REPRESENTATIVE   /ˌrɛprɪˈzɛntətɪv/  •  noun; adjective  •  B1

Example:  A company representative answered all our questions.

REPUTATION   /ˌrɛpjəˈteɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la reputación

REQUIREMENT   /rɪˈkwaɪərmənt/  •  noun  •  B1

RESEMBLE   /rɪˈzɛmbəl/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  parecerse a

RESIST   /rɪˈzɪst/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  resistir; resistirse a

RESOLVE   /rɪˈzɒlv/  •  verb  •  B1

Meaning:  to find a solution to a problem or disagreement

Example:  They managed to resolve the issue quickly.

→  Lograron resolver el problema rápidamente.

RESOURCE   /ˈriːsɔːrs/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el recurso

→  La biblioteca tiene grandes recursos para los estudiantes.

RESPOND   /rɪˈspɒnd/  •  verb  •  B1

RESPONSE   /rɪˈspɒns/  •  noun  •  B1

RESPONSIBILITY   /rɪˌspɒnsəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B1

RESTORE   /rɪˈstɔːr/  •  verb  •  B1

RESTRICT   /rɪˈstrɪkt/  •  verb  •  B1

RESULT   /rɪˈzʌlt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

RETAIN   /rɪˈteɪn/  •  verb  •  B1

RETIRE   /rɪˈtaɪər/  •  verb  •  B1

REWARD   /rɪˈwɔːrd/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

RIGID   /ˈrɪdʒɪd/  •  adjective  •  B1

ROLE   /roʊl/  •  noun  •  B1

ROUGH   /rʌf/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  áspero/a; difícil

ROUTINE   /ruːˈtiːn/  •  noun  •  B1

RUSH   /rʌʃ/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

SATISFY   /ˈsætɪsfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B1

SCHEDULE   /ˈskɛdʒuːl/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Meaning:  a plan of times when things will happen; to arrange a time for something

SECURE   /sɪˈkjʊr/  •  adjective; verb  •  B1

Meaning:  safe from danger or risk; to obtain or make something safe

SEEK   /siːk/  •  verb  •  B1

SELECT   /sɪˈlɛkt/  •  verb  •  B1

SEQUENCE   /ˈsiːkwəns/  •  noun  •  B1

SEVERE   /sɪˈvɪr/  •  adjective  •  B1

SLIGHTLY   /ˈslaɪtli/  •  adverb  •  B1

SOLUTION   /səˈluːʃən/  •  noun  •  B1

SOLVE   /sɒlv/  •  verb  •  B1

SOPHISTICATED   /səˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/  •  adjective  •  B1

SORT   /sɔːrt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el tipo; la clase; clasificar; ordenar

Meaning:  a type or kind of something; to arrange things into groups or order

SOURCE   /sɔːrs/  •  noun  •  B1

SPECIALIZE   /ˈspɛʃəlaɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

Example:  She specializes in family law.

SPECIFICALLY   /spəˈsɪfɪkli/  •  adverb  •  B1

SPREAD   /sprɛd/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

STABILITY   /stəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la estabilidad

Example:  The country has enjoyed political stability for years.

STABLE   /ˈsteɪbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

STANDARD   /ˈstændərd/  •  noun; adjective  •  B1

STATE   /steɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

STRICT   /strɪkt/  •  adjective  •  B1

STRUCTURE   /ˈstrʌktʃər/  •  noun  •  B1

SUBMIT   /səbˈmɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

SUBSTANTIAL   /səbˈstænʃəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

SUCCEED   /səkˈsiːd/  •  verb  •  B1

SUFFER   /ˈsʌfər/  •  verb  •  B1

SUFFICIENT   /səˈfɪʃənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

SUMMARIZE   /ˈsʌməraɪz/  •  verb  •  B1

SUPPLY   /səˈplaɪ/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

SUPPORT   /səˈpɔːrt/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Meaning:  to help or encourage someone; to hold something up

→  Mi familia siempre ha apoyado mis decisiones.

SUPPOSE   /səˈpoʊz/  •  verb  •  B1

SURPRISINGLY   /sərˈpraɪzɪŋli/  •  adverb  •  B1

SURROUND   /səˈraʊnd/  •  verb  •  B1

SURVIVE   /sərˈvaɪv/  •  verb  •  B1

SUSPECT   /səˈspɛkt/ (verb); /ˈsʌspɛkt/ (noun)  •  verb; noun  •  B1

SUSPEND   /səˈspɛnd/  •  verb  •  B1

SYMBOL   /ˈsɪmbəl/  •  noun  •  B1

TARGET   /ˈtɑːrɡɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

TEND   /tɛnd/  •  verb  •  B1

TENDENCY   /ˈtɛndənsi/  •  noun  •  B1

TENSE   /tɛns/  •  adjective  •  B1

THEORY   /ˈθɪri/  •  noun  •  B1

THOROUGHLY   /ˈθɜːroʊli/  •  adverb  •  B1

THREATEN   /ˈθrɛtən/  •  verb  •  B1

TOLERATE   /ˈtɒləreɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

TOTALLY   /ˈtoʊtəli/  •  adverb  •  B1

Spanish:  totalmente

→  Estoy totalmente de acuerdo contigo.

TOUGH   /tʌf/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  duro/a; difícil

Meaning:  difficult; strong and able to deal with problems

Example:  It's been a tough week at work.

→  Ha sido una semana dura en el trabajo.

TRACE   /treɪs/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  rastrear; el rastro; la huella

Meaning:  to find where something came from by following evidence

Example:  Police traced the call to a house nearby.

→  La policía rastreó la llamada hasta una casa cercana.

TRADE   /treɪd/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el comercio; comerciar; intercambiar

Meaning:  the buying and selling of goods; to exchange goods or services

Example:  Trade between the two countries has increased.

→  El comercio entre los dos países ha aumentado.

TRANSFER   /trænsˈfɜːr/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  transferir; trasladar; la transferencia

Meaning:  to move something or someone from one place to another

Example:  He was transferred to the Madrid office.

→  Lo trasladaron a la oficina de Madrid.

TRANSFORM   /trænsˈfɔːrm/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  transformar

Meaning:  to change completely in form or appearance

Example:  The new paint completely transformed the room.

→  La pintura nueva transformó completamente la habitación.

TREAT   /triːt/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  tratar; el capricho

Meaning:  to behave toward someone in a particular way; to give medical care

Example:  She always treats her staff with respect.

→  Ella siempre trata a su personal con respeto.

TREND   /trɛnd/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la tendencia

Meaning:  a general direction in which something is changing

Example:  There's a growing trend toward remote work.

→  Hay una tendencia creciente hacia el trabajo remoto.

TYPICAL   /ˈtɪpɪkəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  típico/a

Meaning:  having the usual features of a particular type of thing

Example:  This is a typical example of his work.

→  Este es un ejemplo típico de su trabajo.

ULTIMATE   /ˈʌltɪmət/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  último/a; definitivo/a

Meaning:  final, or the best or most extreme of its kind

Example:  Winning the championship is our ultimate goal.

→  Ganar el campeonato es nuestro objetivo final.

UNDERGO   /ˌʌndərˈɡoʊ/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  someterse a; experimentar

Meaning:  to experience a process, especially one that involves change

Example:  She underwent surgery last month.

→  Ella se sometió a una cirugía el mes pasado.

Note:  Irregular: undergo → underwent → undergone.

UNDERTAKE   /ˌʌndərˈteɪk/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  emprender

Meaning:  to start a task or project, especially a formal or difficult one

Example:  The city undertook a major renovation of the park.

→  La ciudad emprendió una gran renovación del parque.

Note:  Irregular: undertake → undertook → undertaken.

UNIQUE   /juˈniːk/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  único/a

Meaning:  being the only one of its kind; very special or unusual

Example:  Every snowflake is unique.

→  Cada copo de nieve es único.

UNUSUAL   /ʌnˈjuːʒuəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  inusual; poco común

Meaning:  not common or normal

Example:  It's unusual for her to be late.

→  Es inusual que ella llegue tarde.

UPDATE   /ˈʌpdeɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  la actualización; actualizar

Meaning:  the latest information about something; to add the most recent information

Example:  Can you give me an update on the project?

→  ¿Puedes darme una actualización sobre el proyecto?

URGE   /ɜːrdʒ/  •  verb; noun  •  B1

Spanish:  instar; animar con insistencia; el impulso

Meaning:  to strongly encourage someone to do something; a strong desire

Example:  Doctors urge people to get vaccinated.

→  Los médicos instan a la gente a vacunarse.

URGENT   /ˈɜːrdʒənt/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  urgente

Meaning:  needing immediate attention or action

Example:  This is an urgent matter that needs your attention.

→  Este es un asunto urgente que necesita tu atención.

VALID   /ˈvælɪd/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  válido/a

Meaning:  legally or officially acceptable; based on sound reasoning

Example:  Your passport is valid for another five years.

→  Tu pasaporte es válido por cinco años más.

VALUE   /ˈvæljuː/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el valor; valorar

Meaning:  how much something is worth; to think highly of something

Example:  The house has increased in value.

→  La casa ha aumentado de valor.

VARY   /ˈvɛri/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  variar

Meaning:  to be different or to change

Example:  Prices vary depending on the season.

→  Los precios varían según la temporada.

VERSION   /ˈvɜːrʒən/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  la versión

Meaning:  a particular form of something that differs from other forms

Example:  There's a new version of the app available.

→  Hay una nueva versión de la aplicación disponible.

VIEWPOINT   /ˈvjuːpɔɪnt/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el punto de vista

Meaning:  a particular way of thinking about a situation

Example:  I'd like to hear your viewpoint on this.

→  Me gustaría escuchar tu punto de vista sobre esto.

VIOLATE   /ˈvaɪəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  violar; infringir

Meaning:  to break a rule, law, or agreement

Example:  The company violated several safety regulations.

→  La empresa infringió varias normas de seguridad.

VITAL   /ˈvaɪtəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  vital; esencial

Meaning:  absolutely necessary for something to succeed or survive

Example:  Clean water is vital for good health.

→  El agua limpia es vital para la buena salud.

VOLUNTEER   /ˌvɒlənˈtɪr/  •  noun; verb  •  B1

Spanish:  el voluntario; la voluntaria; ofrecerse (como voluntario)

Meaning:  a person who does work without being paid; to offer to do something

Example:  She volunteers at the animal shelter every weekend.

→  Ella hace de voluntaria en el refugio de animales cada fin de semana.

VULNERABLE   /ˈvʌlnərəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  vulnerable

Meaning:  easily hurt or affected by something harmful

Example:  Older people are more vulnerable to the cold.

→  Las personas mayores son más vulnerables al frío.

WELFARE   /ˈwɛlfɛr/  •  noun  •  B1

Spanish:  el bienestar

Meaning:  the general health, happiness, and safety of a person or group

Example:  The organization works to improve child welfare.

→  La organización trabaja para mejorar el bienestar infantil.

WIDESPREAD   /ˈwaɪdsprɛd/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  generalizado/a; extendido/a

Meaning:  existing or happening over a large area, or affecting many people

Example:  There's widespread support for the new policy.

→  Hay un apoyo generalizado a la nueva política.

WILLING   /ˈwɪlɪŋ/  •  adjective  •  B1

Spanish:  dispuesto/a

Meaning:  ready or happy to do something

Example:  She's willing to help if you need it.

→  Ella está dispuesta a ayudar si lo necesitas.

Common:  willing to

WITHDRAW   /wɪðˈdrɔː/  •  verb  •  B1

Spanish:  retirar; retirarse

Meaning:  to take money out of an account; to remove or leave a situation

Example:  I need to withdraw some cash from the ATM.

→  Necesito retirar algo de efectivo del cajero.

Note:  Irregular: withdraw → withdrew → withdrawn.

# B2 Vocabulary

Upper-intermediate vocabulary — 420 words, alphabetical.

ABOLISH   /əˈbɒlɪʃ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  abolir

Meaning:  to officially end a law, system, or practice

Example:  The country abolished the tax on small businesses.

→  El país abolió el impuesto a las pequeñas empresas.

ABUNDANCE   /əˈbʌndəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la abundancia

Meaning:  a very large quantity of something

Example:  There's an abundance of fresh fruit at the market.

→  Hay abundancia de fruta fresca en el mercado.

ACCELERATE   /əkˈsɛləreɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  acelerar

Meaning:  to happen faster, or to make something happen faster

Example:  The car accelerated quickly onto the highway.

→  El coche aceleró rápidamente hacia la autopista.

ACCOMMODATE   /əˈkɒmədeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  acomodar; alojar; adaptarse a

Meaning:  to provide space or lodging for someone; to adapt to someone's needs

Example:  The hotel can accommodate up to two hundred guests.

→  El hotel puede alojar hasta doscientos huéspedes.

ACCOUNTABILITY   /əˌkaʊntəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la responsabilidad; la rendición de cuentas

Meaning:  the obligation to explain and take responsibility for your actions

Example:  There's a lack of accountability in how the funds were spent.

→  Hay una falta de rendición de cuentas en cómo se gastaron los fondos.

ACCUMULATE   /əˈkjuːmjəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  acumular; acumularse

Meaning:  to gradually gather or increase in quantity over time

Example:  Dust had accumulated on the shelves for months.

→  El polvo se había acumulado en los estantes durante meses.

ACKNOWLEDGE   /əkˈnɒlɪdʒ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  reconocer; admitir

Meaning:  to accept or admit that something is true

Example:  He acknowledged that the plan had some flaws.

→  Él reconoció que el plan tenía algunos fallos.

ADVERSITY   /ədˈvɜːrsəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la adversidad

Meaning:  a difficult or unfavorable situation

Example:  She showed great strength in the face of adversity.

→  Ella mostró gran fortaleza frente a la adversidad.

ADVOCATE   /ˈædvəkeɪt/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  defender; abogar por; el defensor; la defensora

Meaning:  to publicly support an idea or course of action; a person who does this

Example:  She advocates for better public transport in the city.

→  Ella aboga por un mejor transporte público en la ciudad.

Common:  advocate for

AFFILIATION   /əˌfɪliˈeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la afiliación

Meaning:  an official connection with a group or organization

Example:  The club has no political affiliation.

→  El club no tiene afiliación política.

AGGRAVATE   /ˈæɡrəveɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  agravar

Meaning:  to make a problem, injury, or situation worse

Example:  Running on that leg will only aggravate the injury.

→  Correr con esa pierna solo agravará la lesión.

AGGRESSION   /əˈɡrɛʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la agresión

Meaning:  angry or violent behavior toward others

Example:  The dog showed signs of aggression toward strangers.

→  El perro mostró señales de agresión hacia los desconocidos.

ALLEGATION   /ˌæləˈɡeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la acusación; la alegación

Meaning:  a claim that someone has done something wrong, without proof yet

Example:  The company denied the allegations of fraud.

→  La empresa negó las acusaciones de fraude.

ALLIANCE   /əˈlaɪəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la alianza

Meaning:  a formal agreement between countries, groups, or people to work together

Example:  The two companies formed an alliance to develop new technology.

→  Las dos empresas formaron una alianza para desarrollar nueva tecnología.

ALLOCATE   /ˈæləkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  asignar

Meaning:  to give a share of money, time, or resources for a particular purpose

Example:  The city allocated more funds to public schools this year.

→  La ciudad asignó más fondos a las escuelas públicas este año.

ALTER   /ˈɔːltər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  alterar; modificar

Meaning:  to change something, usually slightly

Example:  They altered the design after the first tests.

→  Modificaron el diseño tras las primeras pruebas.

AMBIGUITY   /ˌæmbɪˈɡjuːəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la ambigüedad

Meaning:  the quality of having more than one possible meaning

Example:  There's some ambiguity in how the contract is worded.

→  Hay cierta ambigüedad en cómo está redactado el contrato.

AMBIGUOUS   /æmˈbɪɡjuəs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  ambiguo/a

Meaning:  having more than one possible meaning; unclear

Example:  His answer was deliberately ambiguous.

→  Su respuesta fue deliberadamente ambigua.

AMEND   /əˈmɛnd/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  enmendar; modificar

Meaning:  to make small changes to a law, document, or agreement

Example:  The committee voted to amend the proposal.

→  El comité votó para enmendar la propuesta.

AMENDMENT   /əˈmɛndmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la enmienda

Meaning:  a small change made to a law or document

Example:  Several amendments were made to the constitution.

→  Se hicieron varias enmiendas a la constitución.

ANALOGY   /əˈnælədʒi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la analogía

Meaning:  a comparison between two things to explain or clarify something

Example:  The teacher used a simple analogy to explain the concept.

→  El profesor usó una analogía sencilla para explicar el concepto.

ANOMALY   /əˈnɒməli/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la anomalía

Meaning:  something that is unusual or different from what is expected

Example:  The results showed an anomaly that scientists couldn't explain.

→  Los resultados mostraron una anomalía que los científicos no pudieron explicar.

ANONYMITY   /ˌænəˈnɪməti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el anonimato

Meaning:  the state of not having your name known or identified

Example:  She valued her anonymity and avoided social media.

→  Ella valoraba su anonimato y evitaba las redes sociales.

ANTAGONIST   /ænˈtæɡənɪst/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el antagonista; la antagonista

Meaning:  a person who opposes or fights against the main character or another person

Example:  The film's antagonist is a corrupt businessman.

→  El antagonista de la película es un empresario corrupto.

ANTHROPOLOGY   /ˌænθrəˈpɒlədʒi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la antropología

Meaning:  the study of human societies, cultures, and their development

Example:  She's studying anthropology at university.

→  Ella estudia antropología en la universidad.

APATHY   /ˈæpəθi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la apatía

Meaning:  a lack of interest, enthusiasm, or concern

Example:  Voter apathy was a big issue in the last election.

→  La apatía de los votantes fue un gran problema en las últimas elecciones.

APTITUDE   /ˈæptɪtuːd/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la aptitud

Meaning:  a natural ability to learn or do something well

Example:  She has a real aptitude for languages.

→  Ella tiene una verdadera aptitud para los idiomas.

ARBITRARY   /ˈɑːrbɪtrɛri/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  arbitrario/a

Meaning:  based on random choice rather than reason or a system

Example:  The rules seemed completely arbitrary to the new employees.

→  Las normas parecían completamente arbitrarias para los nuevos empleados.

ARBITRATION   /ˌɑːrbɪˈtreɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el arbitraje

Meaning:  a process where an independent person helps settle a dispute

Example:  The dispute was resolved through arbitration.

→  El conflicto se resolvió mediante arbitraje.

ARCHIVE   /ˈɑːrkaɪv/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  el archivo; archivar

Meaning:  a collection of historical documents or records; to store records for the future

Example:  The old letters are kept in the city archive.

→  Las cartas antiguas se guardan en el archivo de la ciudad.

AROUSE   /əˈraʊz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  despertar; provocar (una reacción)

Meaning:  to cause a feeling or reaction in someone

Example:  Her comment aroused a lot of curiosity.

→  Su comentario despertó mucha curiosidad.

ASPIRATION   /ˌæspəˈreɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la aspiración

Meaning:  a strong desire to achieve something

Example:  Her aspiration is to become a surgeon.

→  Su aspiración es llegar a ser cirujana.

ASSEMBLE   /əˈsɛmbəl/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  ensamblar; reunir

Meaning:  to put parts together to make a whole; to gather people in one place

Example:  It took an hour to assemble the new bookshelf.

→  Tardamos una hora en ensamblar la estantería nueva.

ASSERT   /əˈsɜːrt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  afirmar; hacer valer

Meaning:  to state something firmly and confidently

Example:  She asserted her right to a fair hearing.

→  Ella hizo valer su derecho a una audiencia justa.

ASSESS   /əˈsɛs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  evaluar

Meaning:  to carefully judge the quality, value, or extent of something

Example:  The teacher assessed each student's progress individually.

→  El profesor evaluó el progreso de cada estudiante de forma individual.

ASSUMPTION   /əˈsʌmpʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la suposición; el supuesto

Meaning:  something believed to be true without proof

Example:  We made the assumption that prices would stay the same.

→  Dimos por sentado que los precios se mantendrían igual.

ASYLUM   /əˈsaɪləm/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el asilo

Meaning:  protection given by a country to people fleeing danger in their own country

Example:  The family was granted asylum after fleeing the conflict.

→  A la familia se le concedió asilo tras huir del conflicto.

ATTAIN   /əˈteɪn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  alcanzar; lograr

Meaning:  to succeed in achieving something, often after effort

Example:  She attained the highest score in her class.

→  Ella alcanzó la puntuación más alta de su clase.

ATTRIBUTE   /ˈætrɪbjuːt/ (noun); /əˈtrɪbjuːt/ (verb)  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  el atributo; la cualidad; atribuir

Meaning:  a quality or feature of someone or something; to say something is caused by something else

Example:  Patience is one of her best attributes.

→  La paciencia es una de sus mejores cualidades.

Common:  attribute to

AUGMENT   /ɔːɡˈmɛnt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  aumentar; incrementar

Meaning:  to make something greater in size, value, or amount

Example:  She augmented her income with freelance work.

→  Ella aumentó sus ingresos con trabajo autónomo.

AUTHORIZE   /ˈɔːθəraɪz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  autorizar

Meaning:  to give official permission for something

Example:  The manager authorized the extra spending.

→  El gerente autorizó el gasto adicional.

Note:  British spelling: "authorise".

AUTONOMOUS   /ɔːˈtɒnəməs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  autónomo/a

Meaning:  able to govern or act independently

Example:  The region became autonomous in 1980.

→  La región se volvió autónoma en 1980.

AUTONOMY   /ɔːˈtɒnəmi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la autonomía

Meaning:  the right or ability to govern or act independently

Example:  The region has a lot of autonomy over its own budget.

→  La región tiene mucha autonomía sobre su propio presupuesto.

BACKLASH   /ˈbæklæʃ/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la reacción negativa

Meaning:  a strong negative reaction by a group of people

Example:  The company faced a backlash over its pricing changes.

→  La empresa enfrentó una fuerte reacción negativa por sus cambios de precios.

BANKRUPTCY   /ˈbæŋkrʌptsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la bancarrota; la quiebra

Meaning:  the legal state of being unable to pay your debts

Example:  The company filed for bankruptcy last year.

→  La empresa se declaró en quiebra el año pasado.

BIAS   /ˈbaɪəs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el sesgo; el prejuicio

Meaning:  an unfair preference for or against something, based on personal opinion

Example:  The report was criticized for showing bias toward one side.

→  El informe fue criticado por mostrar sesgo hacia un lado.

BUREAUCRACY   /bjʊˈrɒkrəsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la burocracia

Meaning:  a complex system of official rules and procedures, often slow and frustrating

Example:  Getting the permit involved a lot of bureaucracy.

→  Conseguir el permiso implicó mucha burocracia.

CAPITALIZE   /ˈkæpɪtəlaɪz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  aprovechar; capitalizar

Meaning:  to take advantage of a situation or opportunity

Example:  The company capitalized on the growing demand for electric cars.

→  La empresa aprovechó la creciente demanda de coches eléctricos.

Common:  capitalize on

Note:  British spelling: "capitalise".

CATALYST   /ˈkætəlɪst/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el catalizador

Meaning:  something that causes an important change or event to happen

Example:  The protest acted as a catalyst for real political change.

→  La protesta actuó como catalizador de un verdadero cambio político.

CENSORSHIP   /ˈsɛnsərʃɪp/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la censura

Meaning:  the practice of officially controlling what can be published or shown

Example:  The film was banned due to government censorship.

→  La película fue prohibida debido a la censura del gobierno.

CIRCULATE   /ˈsɜːrkjəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  circular; hacer circular

Meaning:  to move around and be shared among a group of people

Example:  Rumors began to circulate about the merger.

→  Empezaron a circular rumores sobre la fusión.

COALITION   /ˌkoʊəˈlɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la coalición

Meaning:  a temporary alliance between different groups, especially political parties

Example:  The two parties formed a coalition to win the election.

→  Los dos partidos formaron una coalición para ganar las elecciones.

COHERENCE   /koʊˈhɪrəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la coherencia

Meaning:  the quality of being logical and consistent

Example:  The essay lacks coherence in its second half.

→  El ensayo carece de coherencia en su segunda mitad.

COHERENT   /koʊˈhɪrənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  coherente

Meaning:  logical and easy to understand; consistent

Example:  He gave a clear and coherent explanation.

→  Él dio una explicación clara y coherente.

COINCIDE   /ˌkoʊɪnˈsaɪd/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  coincidir

Meaning:  to happen at the same time as something else; to be the same or similar

Example:  Her birthday coincides with a national holiday.

→  Su cumpleaños coincide con un día festivo nacional.

COLLAPSE   /kəˈlæps/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  colapsar; derrumbarse; el colapso

Meaning:  to suddenly fall down or fail completely

Example:  The old bridge collapsed during the storm.

→  El puente viejo se derrumbó durante la tormenta.

COMMODITY   /kəˈmɒdəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la materia prima; el producto básico

Meaning:  a raw material or basic product that can be bought and sold

Example:  Coffee is one of the world's most traded commodities.

→  El café es una de las materias primas más comercializadas del mundo.

COMPASSION   /kəmˈpæʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la compasión

Meaning:  a strong feeling of sympathy for someone who is suffering

Example:  She treated every patient with compassion.

→  Ella trataba a cada paciente con compasión.

COMPATIBLE   /kəmˈpætəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  compatible

Meaning:  able to exist or work together without problems

Example:  This charger isn't compatible with my old phone.

→  Este cargador no es compatible con mi móvil viejo.

COMPELLING   /kəmˈpɛlɪŋ/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  convincente; cautivador/a

Meaning:  very interesting or convincing, in a way that holds your attention

Example:  She made a compelling argument for the new policy.

→  Ella presentó un argumento convincente a favor de la nueva política.

COMPENSATE   /ˈkɒmpənseɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  compensar

Meaning:  to give someone money or something else to make up for a loss or harm

Example:  The airline compensated passengers for the delay.

→  La aerolínea compensó a los pasajeros por el retraso.

COMPILE   /kəmˈpaɪl/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  recopilar; compilar

Meaning:  to collect information from different sources into one document or list

Example:  She compiled a list of useful resources for new students.

→  Ella recopiló una lista de recursos útiles para los estudiantes nuevos.

COMPLEMENT   /ˈkɒmplɪmɛnt/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  complementar; el complemento

Meaning:  to go well with something and make it better

Example:  The wine complements the meal perfectly.

→  El vino complementa la comida a la perfección.

Note:  Don't confuse with "compliment" (a flattering remark).

COMPLIANCE   /kəmˈplaɪəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el cumplimiento

Meaning:  the act of obeying a rule, law, or request

Example:  The factory was checked for compliance with safety rules.

→  Se revisó la fábrica para verificar el cumplimiento de las normas de seguridad.

COMPLY   /kəmˈplaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  cumplir

Meaning:  to act according to a rule, law, or request

Example:  All businesses must comply with the new regulations.

→  Todas las empresas deben cumplir con las nuevas normativas.

Common:  comply with

COMPOUND   /ˈkɒmpaʊnd/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  el compuesto; agravar; empeorar

Meaning:  a substance made of two or more elements; to make a problem worse

Example:  The delay was compounded by bad weather.

→  El retraso se agravó por el mal tiempo.

COMPREHEND   /ˌkɒmprɪˈhɛnd/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  comprender

Meaning:  to fully understand something

Example:  It's hard to comprehend the scale of the disaster.

→  Es difícil comprender la magnitud del desastre.

COMPREHENSION   /ˌkɒmprɪˈhɛnʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la comprensión

Meaning:  the ability to understand something

Example:  The exam includes a listening comprehension section.

→  El examen incluye una sección de comprensión auditiva.

COMPREHENSIVE   /ˌkɒmprɪˈhɛnsɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  integral; completo/a

Meaning:  including everything that is necessary or relevant

Example:  The report offers a comprehensive review of the market.

→  El informe ofrece una revisión integral del mercado.

COMPRISE   /kəmˈpraɪz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  comprender; estar compuesto por

Meaning:  to consist of, or to be made up of particular parts

Example:  The committee comprises ten members.

→  El comité está compuesto por diez miembros.

CONCEAL   /kənˈsiːl/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  ocultar

Meaning:  to hide something so it cannot be seen or discovered

Example:  She tried to conceal her disappointment.

→  Ella intentó ocultar su decepción.

CONCEDE   /kənˈsiːd/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  admitir; ceder

Meaning:  to admit that something is true, often reluctantly; to give up in a competition

Example:  He conceded that the plan needed changes.

→  Él admitió que el plan necesitaba cambios.

CONCEIVE   /kənˈsiːv/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  concebir; imaginar

Meaning:  to form an idea or plan in your mind

Example:  It's hard to conceive of a world without the internet.

→  Es difícil concebir un mundo sin internet.

CONDEMN   /kənˈdɛm/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  condenar

Meaning:  to say strongly that something is wrong or unacceptable

Example:  The government condemned the attack.

→  El gobierno condenó el ataque.

Note:  The "n" is silent.

CONDUCIVE   /kənˈduːsɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  propicio/a; favorable

Meaning:  making a particular situation or outcome more likely

Example:  A quiet room is conducive to good studying.

→  Una habitación tranquila es propicia para estudiar bien.

Common:  conducive to

CONFER   /kənˈfɜːr/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  conferir; conferenciar; consultar

Meaning:  to discuss something with others before deciding; to give someone a title or right

Example:  The doctors conferred before deciding on the treatment.

→  Los médicos consultaron antes de decidir el tratamiento.

CONFIGURE   /kənˈfɪɡjər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  configurar

Meaning:  to set up a system or device to work in a particular way

Example:  You'll need to configure the router before using it.

→  Necesitarás configurar el router antes de usarlo.

CONFORM   /kənˈfɔːrm/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  cumplir; ajustarse a; conformarse

Meaning:  to behave according to accepted rules or standards

Example:  The building doesn't conform to safety regulations.

→  El edificio no cumple con las normas de seguridad.

Common:  conform to

CONFORMITY   /kənˈfɔːrməti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la conformidad

Meaning:  behavior that follows accepted rules or standards

Example:  The school expects a certain level of conformity in dress.

→  La escuela espera cierto nivel de conformidad en la vestimenta.

CONNOTATION   /ˌkɒnəˈteɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la connotación

Meaning:  a feeling or idea suggested by a word beyond its literal meaning

Example:  That word has a negative connotation in this context.

→  Esa palabra tiene una connotación negativa en este contexto.

CONSENSUS   /kənˈsɛnsəs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el consenso

Meaning:  general agreement among a group of people

Example:  The team reached a consensus after a long discussion.

→  El equipo llegó a un consenso tras una larga discusión.

CONSOLIDATE   /kənˈsɒlɪdeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  consolidar

Meaning:  to make something stronger or more secure; to combine things into one

Example:  The company consolidated its offices into a single building.

→  La empresa consolidó sus oficinas en un solo edificio.

CONSTITUENCY   /kənˈstɪtʃuənsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la circunscripción electoral

Meaning:  a district that elects a representative to a governing body

Example:  She has represented this constituency for ten years.

→  Ella ha representado esta circunscripción durante diez años.

CONSTITUTE   /ˈkɒnstɪtuːt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  constituir

Meaning:  to be considered as, or to form part of a whole

Example:  Women constitute half of the workforce.

→  Las mujeres constituyen la mitad de la fuerza laboral.

CONTEMPLATE   /ˈkɒntəmpleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  contemplar; considerar

Meaning:  to think carefully about something for a long time

Example:  She's contemplating a career change.

→  Ella está considerando un cambio de carrera.

CONTENTIOUS   /kənˈtɛnʃəs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  controvertido/a; polémico/a

Meaning:  likely to cause disagreement

Example:  Immigration remains a contentious issue in politics.

→  La inmigración sigue siendo un tema controvertido en política.

CONTINGENCY   /kənˈtɪndʒənsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la contingencia; el imprevisto

Meaning:  a possible future event that must be planned for

Example:  We need a contingency plan in case the flight is cancelled.

→  Necesitamos un plan de contingencia por si se cancela el vuelo.

CONTROVERSIAL   /ˌkɒntrəˈvɜːrʃəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  controvertido/a; polémico/a

Meaning:  causing strong disagreement or public discussion

Example:  The new law has proven very controversial.

→  La nueva ley ha resultado muy controvertida.

CONVERGENCE   /kənˈvɜːrdʒəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la convergencia

Meaning:  the process of different things coming together toward a similar point

Example:  There's a growing convergence between the two technologies.

→  Hay una creciente convergencia entre las dos tecnologías.

CONVEY   /kənˈveɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  transmitir; expresar

Meaning:  to communicate an idea, feeling, or message

Example:  Her letter conveyed a real sense of gratitude.

→  Su carta transmitía un verdadero sentido de gratitud.

CONVICTION   /kənˈvɪkʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la convicción; la condena

Meaning:  a firm belief; a formal decision that someone is guilty of a crime

Example:  She spoke with real conviction about the project.

→  Ella habló con verdadera convicción sobre el proyecto.

CORRELATE   /ˈkɔːrəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  correlacionar; correlacionarse

Meaning:  to have a mutual relationship where one thing affects or connects to another

Example:  Higher education tends to correlate with higher income.

→  La educación superior tiende a correlacionarse con mayores ingresos.

CORRUPTION   /kəˈrʌpʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la corrupción

Meaning:  dishonest or illegal behavior, especially by people in power

Example:  The scandal exposed corruption within the department.

→  El escándalo expuso la corrupción dentro del departamento.

COUNTER   /ˈkaʊntər/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  contrarrestar; el mostrador

Meaning:  to oppose or respond to something with an opposite action; a long flat surface in a shop or kitchen

Example:  She countered his argument with solid evidence.

→  Ella contrarrestó su argumento con pruebas sólidas.

CREDIBILITY   /ˌkrɛdəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la credibilidad

Meaning:  the quality of being believable or trustworthy

Example:  The scandal damaged his credibility as a leader.

→  El escándalo dañó su credibilidad como líder.

CREDIBLE   /ˈkrɛdəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  creíble

Meaning:  able to be believed or trusted

Example:  They need a credible witness to support their case.

→  Necesitan un testigo creíble para respaldar su caso.

CUMULATIVE   /ˈkjuːmjələtɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  acumulativo/a

Meaning:  increasing gradually as more things of the same kind are added

Example:  The cumulative effect of small changes can be significant.

→  El efecto acumulativo de pequeños cambios puede ser significativo.

CURB   /kɜːrb/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  frenar; controlar; el bordillo (Am.)

Meaning:  to control or limit something, especially something harmful

Example:  The city introduced new rules to curb air pollution.

→  La ciudad introdujo nuevas normas para frenar la contaminación del aire.

CYNICISM   /ˈsɪnəsɪzəm/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el cinismo

Meaning:  a general distrust of people's motives or sincerity

Example:  There's a lot of cynicism about politicians these days.

→  Hoy en día hay mucho cinismo respecto a los políticos.

DECADENCE   /ˈdɛkədəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la decadencia

Meaning:  a state of moral or cultural decline, often linked to excessive luxury

Example:  The novel describes the decadence of the aristocracy.

→  La novela describe la decadencia de la aristocracia.

DECLINE   /dɪˈklaɪn/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  declinar; rechazar; disminuir; el declive

Meaning:  to become smaller, weaker, or worse; to politely refuse

Example:  Sales have declined steadily this year.

→  Las ventas han disminuido de forma constante este año.

DECOMPOSE   /ˌdiːkəmˈpoʊz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  descomponerse; descomponer

Meaning:  to slowly break down and rot, especially organic matter

Example:  Fallen leaves decompose and enrich the soil.

→  Las hojas caídas se descomponen y enriquecen el suelo.

DEDUCE   /dɪˈduːs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  deducir

Meaning:  to reach a conclusion using logic and available information

Example:  From the evidence, we can deduce what really happened.

→  A partir de las pruebas, podemos deducir lo que realmente pasó.

DEFICIT   /ˈdɛfɪsɪt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el déficit

Meaning:  the amount by which spending is more than income

Example:  The country is trying to reduce its budget deficit.

→  El país está intentando reducir su déficit presupuestario.

DEFY   /dɪˈfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  desafiar

Meaning:  to openly refuse to obey a rule, person, or expectation

Example:  The teenager defied his parents' wishes.

→  El adolescente desafió los deseos de sus padres.

DELIBERATE   /dɪˈlɪbərət/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  deliberado/a; intencionado/a

Meaning:  done on purpose, not by accident

Example:  It was a deliberate decision to keep the price low.

→  Fue una decisión deliberada mantener el precio bajo.

DEPLOY   /dɪˈplɔɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  desplegar

Meaning:  to move troops, resources, or equipment into position for use

Example:  Extra staff were deployed to handle the holiday rush.

→  Se desplegó personal adicional para gestionar el ajetreo de las fiestas.

DEPLOYMENT   /dɪˈplɔɪmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el despliegue

Meaning:  the act of moving troops, resources, or equipment into position

Example:  The deployment of new software caused some delays.

→  El despliegue del nuevo software causó algunos retrasos.

DEPRIVATION   /ˌdɛprɪˈveɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la privación

Meaning:  the state of not having something considered necessary, such as food or sleep

Example:  Sleep deprivation affects concentration and mood.

→  La privación del sueño afecta la concentración y el estado de ánimo.

DERIVE   /dɪˈraɪv/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  derivar; obtener

Meaning:  to obtain or trace something from a particular source

Example:  She derives a lot of satisfaction from her work.

→  Ella obtiene mucha satisfacción de su trabajo.

Common:  derive from

DESIGNATE   /ˈdɛzɪɡneɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  designar

Meaning:  to officially choose or name someone or something for a particular purpose

Example:  This area has been designated a wildlife reserve.

→  Esta zona ha sido designada como reserva natural.

DETERIORATE   /dɪˈtɪriəreɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  deteriorarse; empeorar

Meaning:  to become progressively worse

Example:  His health deteriorated quickly after the accident.

→  Su salud se deterioró rápidamente después del accidente.

DETRIMENT   /ˈdɛtrɪmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el perjuicio; el detrimento

Meaning:  harm or damage caused to someone or something

Example:  He worked long hours, to the detriment of his health.

→  Trabajaba muchas horas, en detrimento de su salud.

DEVIATE   /ˈdiːvieɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  desviarse

Meaning:  to move away from an expected or usual course or standard

Example:  The plan didn't deviate from the original schedule.

→  El plan no se desvió del calendario original.

Common:  deviate from

DEVIATION   /ˌdiːviˈeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la desviación

Meaning:  a difference from what is expected or normal

Example:  There was a small deviation from the usual procedure.

→  Hubo una pequeña desviación del procedimiento habitual.

DILEMMA   /dɪˈlɛmə/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el dilema

Meaning:  a difficult situation requiring a choice between equally unwelcome options

Example:  She faced a real dilemma about which job offer to accept.

→  Ella se enfrentaba a un verdadero dilema sobre qué oferta de trabajo aceptar.

DIMINISH   /dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  disminuir

Meaning:  to make or become smaller, weaker, or less important

Example:  His influence in the company has diminished over time.

→  Su influencia en la empresa ha disminuido con el tiempo.

DISCARD   /dɪˈskɑːrd/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  desechar; descartar

Meaning:  to get rid of something you no longer need

Example:  He discarded the old documents after scanning them.

→  Desechó los documentos viejos después de escanearlos.

DISCERN   /dɪˈsɜːrn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  discernir; percibir

Meaning:  to notice or understand something that is not obvious

Example:  It was hard to discern any real pattern in the data.

→  Era difícil discernir algún patrón real en los datos.

DISCLOSE   /dɪsˈkloʊz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  revelar; divulgar

Meaning:  to make secret or private information known

Example:  The company refused to disclose its profits.

→  La empresa se negó a revelar sus ganancias.

DISCOURAGE   /dɪsˈkɜːrɪdʒ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  desanimar; disuadir

Meaning:  to make someone less confident or enthusiastic about doing something

Example:  Don't let one failure discourage you.

→  No dejes que un fracaso te desanime.

DISCREET   /dɪˈskriːt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  discreto/a

Meaning:  careful not to attract attention or reveal private information

Example:  Please be discreet about the surprise party.

→  Por favor, sé discreto con lo de la fiesta sorpresa.

Note:  Don't confuse with "discrete" (separate).

DISCREPANCY   /dɪˈskrɛpənsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la discrepancia

Meaning:  a difference between things that should be the same

Example:  There's a discrepancy between the two reports.

→  Hay una discrepancia entre los dos informes.

DISCRETE   /dɪˈskriːt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  discreto/a (separado)

Meaning:  individually separate and distinct

Example:  The course is divided into three discrete units.

→  El curso está dividido en tres unidades separadas.

Note:  Don't confuse with "discreet" (careful, tactful).

DISPARITY   /dɪˈspærəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la disparidad

Meaning:  a great difference between two things that should be similar

Example:  There's a huge disparity in wages between the two regions.

→  Hay una gran disparidad salarial entre las dos regiones.

DISPERSE   /dɪˈspɜːrs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  dispersar; dispersarse

Meaning:  to spread out or move away in different directions

Example:  The crowd dispersed after the concert ended.

→  La multitud se dispersó después de que terminara el concierto.

DISPUTE   /dɪˈspjuːt/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  la disputa; el conflicto; disputar; cuestionar

Meaning:  a disagreement or argument; to question whether something is true

Example:  There's a long-running dispute between the two neighbors.

→  Hay una disputa de larga data entre los dos vecinos.

DISRUPT   /dɪsˈrʌpt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  interrumpir; alterar

Meaning:  to interrupt something by causing disorder or a problem

Example:  The strike disrupted train services across the country.

→  La huelga alteró los servicios de tren en todo el país.

DISSENT   /dɪˈsɛnt/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  la disidencia; disentir

Meaning:  disagreement with an official opinion or decision

Example:  There was some dissent within the party over the new policy.

→  Hubo cierta disidencia dentro del partido sobre la nueva política.

DISTORT   /dɪˈstɔːrt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  distorsionar

Meaning:  to change the shape, sound, or meaning of something in a misleading way

Example:  The article distorted what she actually said.

→  El artículo distorsionó lo que ella realmente dijo.

DIVERGE   /daɪˈvɜːrdʒ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  divergir

Meaning:  to develop in different directions from a common point

Example:  Their opinions began to diverge on this issue.

→  Sus opiniones empezaron a divergir sobre este tema.

DIVERSE   /daɪˈvɜːrs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  diverso/a

Meaning:  showing a wide variety; very different from each other

Example:  The city has a very diverse population.

→  La ciudad tiene una población muy diversa.

DIVERSIFY   /daɪˈvɜːrsɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  diversificar

Meaning:  to make something more varied, especially to reduce risk

Example:  The company decided to diversify its product range.

→  La empresa decidió diversificar su gama de productos.

DIVERSITY   /daɪˈvɜːrsəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la diversidad

Meaning:  the state of including a wide range of different types of people or things

Example:  The festival celebrates the city's cultural diversity.

→  El festival celebra la diversidad cultural de la ciudad.

DOCTRINE   /ˈdɒktrɪn/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la doctrina

Meaning:  a set of beliefs held and taught by a group, especially religious or political

Example:  The party's economic doctrine has changed over the years.

→  La doctrina económica del partido ha cambiado con los años.

DOMINANCE   /ˈdɒmɪnəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el dominio; la dominancia

Meaning:  the state of having the most power or influence

Example:  The company maintains its dominance in the market.

→  La empresa mantiene su dominio en el mercado.

DOMINANT   /ˈdɒmɪnənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  dominante

Meaning:  more powerful, important, or influential than others

Example:  English is the dominant language in international business.

→  El inglés es la lengua dominante en los negocios internacionales.

DUBIOUS   /ˈduːbiəs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  dudoso/a

Meaning:  not sure or certain; possibly not honest or true

Example:  I'm a bit dubious about his real intentions.

→  Tengo mis dudas sobre sus verdaderas intenciones.

DURATION   /dʊˈreɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la duración

Meaning:  the length of time something lasts

Example:  The duration of the flight is about ten hours.

→  La duración del vuelo es de unas diez horas.

DWELL   /dwɛl/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  habitar; morar; darle vueltas a

Meaning:  to live in a place; to think or talk about something for too long

Example:  Try not to dwell on your mistakes.

→  Intenta no darle demasiadas vueltas a tus errores.

Common:  dwell on

ELOQUENCE   /ˈɛləkwəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la elocuencia

Meaning:  the ability to speak or write in a persuasive and expressive way

Example:  She spoke with great eloquence at the ceremony.

→  Ella habló con gran elocuencia en la ceremonia.

ELOQUENT   /ˈɛləkwənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  elocuente

Meaning:  able to speak or write clearly and persuasively

Example:  He gave an eloquent speech about equality.

→  Él dio un discurso elocuente sobre la igualdad.

EMPATHY   /ˈɛmpəθi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la empatía

Meaning:  the ability to understand and share the feelings of another person

Example:  She showed a lot of empathy toward her patients.

→  Ella mostró mucha empatía hacia sus pacientes.

EMPIRICAL   /ɪmˈpɪrɪkəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  empírico/a

Meaning:  based on observation or experience rather than theory

Example:  There's no empirical evidence to support that claim.

→  No hay evidencia empírica que respalde esa afirmación.

ENDEAVOR   /ɪnˈdɛvər/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  el esfuerzo; el empeño; esforzarse por

Meaning:  a serious attempt to do something; to try hard to do something

Example:  The business was a joint endeavor between two friends.

→  El negocio fue un esfuerzo conjunto entre dos amigos.

Note:  British spelling: "endeavour".

ENTITY   /ˈɛntəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la entidad

Meaning:  something that exists as a single, separate unit

Example:  The new department will operate as a separate entity.

→  El nuevo departamento funcionará como una entidad separada.

EPIDEMIC   /ˌɛpɪˈdɛmɪk/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la epidemia

Meaning:  a widespread occurrence of a disease in a community

Example:  The city acted quickly to stop the epidemic from spreading.

→  La ciudad actuó rápido para evitar que se propagara la epidemia.

EQUITY   /ˈɛkwəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la equidad; el capital (finanzas)

Meaning:  fairness and justice; the value of shares in a company

Example:  The policy aims to promote equity in education.

→  La política busca promover la equidad en la educación.

EQUIVALENT   /ɪˈkwɪvələnt/  •  adjective; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  equivalente

Meaning:  equal in value, meaning, or importance

Example:  One kilometer is equivalent to about 0.6 miles.

→  Un kilómetro equivale a unas 0.6 millas.

ERADICATE   /ɪˈrædɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  erradicar

Meaning:  to destroy or eliminate something completely

Example:  The vaccine helped eradicate the disease worldwide.

→  La vacuna ayudó a erradicar la enfermedad en todo el mundo.

ERODE   /ɪˈroʊd/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  erosionar; erosionarse

Meaning:  to gradually wear away or weaken over time

Example:  The coastline has eroded significantly over the decades.

→  La costa se ha erosionado significativamente a lo largo de las décadas.

ESCALATE   /ˈɛskəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  intensificarse; escalar

Meaning:  to become more serious or intense

Example:  The conflict escalated quickly after the meeting.

→  El conflicto se intensificó rápidamente tras la reunión.

ETHICS   /ˈɛθɪks/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la ética

Meaning:  moral principles that govern behavior

Example:  The company was praised for its business ethics.

→  La empresa fue elogiada por su ética empresarial.

EXEMPT   /ɪɡˈzɛmpt/  •  adjective; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  exento/a; eximir

Meaning:  not required to do something or pay something that others must; to free someone from an obligation

Example:  Charities are exempt from this tax.

→  Las organizaciones benéficas están exentas de este impuesto.

EXEMPTION   /ɪɡˈzɛmpʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la exención

Meaning:  freedom from a duty, tax, or requirement that applies to others

Example:  Students can apply for a tax exemption.

→  Los estudiantes pueden solicitar una exención fiscal.

EXERT   /ɪɡˈzɜːrt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  ejercer

Meaning:  to use power, influence, or effort to produce an effect

Example:  Parents exert a strong influence on their children.

→  Los padres ejercen una fuerte influencia sobre sus hijos.

EXPLICIT   /ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  explícito/a

Meaning:  stated clearly and directly, leaving nothing implied

Example:  She gave explicit instructions on how to proceed.

→  Ella dio instrucciones explícitas sobre cómo proceder.

EXPLOIT   /ɪkˈsplɔɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  explotar; aprovechar

Meaning:  to use something to your advantage, sometimes unfairly

Example:  The company was accused of exploiting its workers.

→  Se acusó a la empresa de explotar a sus trabajadores.

EXPLOITATION   /ˌɛksplɔɪˈteɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la explotación

Meaning:  the act of using someone or something unfairly for your own benefit

Example:  The report exposed the exploitation of child labor.

→  El informe expuso la explotación del trabajo infantil.

EXTREMITY   /ɪkˈstrɛməti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el extremo; la extremidad

Meaning:  the furthest point or edge of something; an extreme degree of something

Example:  They live at the extremity of the island.

→  Viven en el extremo de la isla.

FACILITATE   /fəˈsɪlɪteɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  facilitar

Meaning:  to make an action or process easier

Example:  The new software facilitates communication between teams.

→  El nuevo software facilita la comunicación entre equipos.

FACILITATION   /fəˌsɪlɪˈteɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la facilitación

Meaning:  the act of making something easier or possible

Example:  Her role involves the facilitation of team workshops.

→  Su rol implica la facilitación de talleres de equipo.

FALTER   /ˈfɔːltər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  flaquear; vacilar; titubear

Meaning:  to become weaker or less confident; to hesitate while speaking

Example:  His voice faltered as he spoke about the accident.

→  Su voz titubeó al hablar del accidente.

FEASIBILITY   /ˌfiːzəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la viabilidad; la factibilidad

Meaning:  whether something is possible to do successfully

Example:  They're studying the feasibility of the new project.

→  Están estudiando la viabilidad del nuevo proyecto.

FEASIBLE   /ˈfiːzəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  viable; factible

Meaning:  possible to do easily or realistically

Example:  Is it feasible to finish this by Friday?

→  ¿Es viable terminar esto para el viernes?

FLUCTUATION   /ˌflʌktʃuˈeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la fluctuación

Meaning:  a change in level or amount, especially one that happens repeatedly

Example:  There have been big fluctuations in oil prices.

→  Ha habido grandes fluctuaciones en el precio del petróleo.

FORMIDABLE   /ˈfɔːrmɪdəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  formidable; imponente

Meaning:  inspiring fear or respect through being very impressive or powerful

Example:  She's a formidable opponent in any debate.

→  Ella es una oponente formidable en cualquier debate.

FORMULATE   /ˈfɔːrmjəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  formular

Meaning:  to create or develop a plan, idea, or theory carefully

Example:  The team is formulating a new strategy for next year.

→  El equipo está formulando una nueva estrategia para el próximo año.

FORTIFY   /ˈfɔːrtɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  fortalecer; fortificar

Meaning:  to make something stronger, physically or emotionally

Example:  The walls were built to fortify the old city.

→  Las murallas se construyeron para fortificar la ciudad antigua.

FOSTER   /ˈfɔːstər/  •  verb; adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  fomentar; de acogida

Meaning:  to encourage the development of something; relating to temporary family care

Example:  The program aims to foster creativity in young children.

→  El programa busca fomentar la creatividad en los niños pequeños.

FRACTION   /ˈfrækʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la fracción; una parte pequeña

Meaning:  a small part or amount of something; a number expressed as a ratio

Example:  Only a fraction of applicants get the job.

→  Solo una fracción de los candidatos consigue el trabajo.

FRAGILE   /ˈfrædʒəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  frágil

Meaning:  easily broken or damaged; delicate

Example:  Please handle the glasses carefully, they're fragile.

→  Maneja los vasos con cuidado, son frágiles.

FRAGMENT   /ˈfræɡmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el fragmento

Meaning:  a small piece broken off from something larger

Example:  Archaeologists found fragments of ancient pottery.

→  Los arqueólogos encontraron fragmentos de cerámica antigua.

FRAUD   /frɔːd/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el fraude

Meaning:  the crime of deceiving someone to gain money or benefits illegally

Example:  He was arrested for tax fraud.

→  Fue arrestado por fraude fiscal.

FULFILL   /fʊlˈfɪl/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  cumplir; realizar

Meaning:  to achieve or complete something, such as a goal or duty

Example:  She finally fulfilled her dream of traveling the world.
