→  Ella finalmente cumplió su sueño de viajar por el mundo.

Note:  British spelling: "fulfil".

GRASP   /ɡræsp/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  comprender; agarrar; el entendimiento

Meaning:  to understand something fully; to take firm hold of something

Example:  It took me a while to grasp the new system.

→  Me costó un poco comprender el nuevo sistema.

GRIEVANCE   /ˈɡriːvəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la queja; el agravio

Meaning:  a formal complaint about being treated unfairly

Example:  The employees filed a grievance about their working conditions.

→  Los empleados presentaron una queja sobre sus condiciones laborales.

GUIDELINE   /ˈɡaɪdlaɪn/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la directriz; la pauta

Meaning:  a piece of advice or a rule about how to do something

Example:  Please follow the safety guidelines carefully.

→  Sigue las directrices de seguridad con cuidado.

HARASS   /həˈræs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  acosar

Meaning:  to repeatedly bother or trouble someone in an aggressive way

Example:  She was harassed online for weeks.

→  Fue acosada en línea durante semanas.

HIERARCHICAL   /ˌhaɪəˈrɑːrkɪkəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  jerárquico/a

Meaning:  organized in order of rank or level of importance

Example:  The company has a very hierarchical structure.

→  La empresa tiene una estructura muy jerárquica.

HIERARCHY   /ˈhaɪərɑːrki/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la jerarquía

Meaning:  a system in which people or things are ranked according to importance

Example:  There's a clear hierarchy in this organization.

→  Hay una jerarquía clara en esta organización.

HINDER   /ˈhɪndər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  obstaculizar; dificultar

Meaning:  to make something more difficult to achieve

Example:  Bad weather hindered the rescue operation.

→  El mal tiempo obstaculizó la operación de rescate.

HINDSIGHT   /ˈhaɪndsaɪt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la retrospectiva

Meaning:  understanding of a situation only after it has happened

Example:  In hindsight, we should have booked earlier.

→  En retrospectiva, deberíamos haber reservado antes.

HYPOTHESIS   /haɪˈpɒθəsɪs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la hipótesis

Meaning:  an idea suggested as a possible explanation, to be tested

Example:  The scientists tested their hypothesis in the lab.

→  Los científicos pusieron a prueba su hipótesis en el laboratorio.

Note:  Irregular plural: hypothesis → hypotheses.

HYPOTHETICAL   /ˌhaɪpəˈθɛtɪkəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  hipotético/a

Meaning:  imagined or suggested, but not necessarily real

Example:  Let's consider a hypothetical situation.

→  Consideremos una situación hipotética.

IDEOLOGY   /ˌaɪdiˈɒlədʒi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la ideología

Meaning:  a set of beliefs and ideas, especially political ones

Example:  The two parties have very different ideologies.

→  Los dos partidos tienen ideologías muy diferentes.

IMPLICATE   /ˈɪmplɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  implicar; involucrar

Meaning:  to show that someone is involved in a crime or wrongdoing

Example:  New evidence implicated him in the theft.

→  Nuevas pruebas lo implicaron en el robo.

IMPLICATION   /ˌɪmplɪˈkeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la implicación; la consecuencia

Meaning:  a possible effect or result of an action; something suggested but not stated directly

Example:  This decision has serious implications for the whole team.

→  Esta decisión tiene serias implicaciones para todo el equipo.

IMPOSE   /ɪmˈpoʊz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  imponer

Meaning:  to force a rule, tax, or opinion on someone

Example:  The government imposed new restrictions on travel.

→  El gobierno impuso nuevas restricciones de viaje.

INADEQUATE   /ɪnˈædɪkwət/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  inadecuado/a; insuficiente

Meaning:  not good enough or not sufficient for a particular purpose

Example:  The funding was inadequate to complete the project.

→  La financiación fue insuficiente para completar el proyecto.

INCENTIVE   /ɪnˈsɛntɪv/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el incentivo

Meaning:  something that encourages a person to do something

Example:  The company offers incentives for meeting sales targets.

→  La empresa ofrece incentivos por cumplir los objetivos de ventas.

INCIDENCE   /ˈɪnsɪdəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la incidencia

Meaning:  the rate at which something happens

Example:  There's been a rise in the incidence of the disease.

→  Ha habido un aumento en la incidencia de la enfermedad.

INCLINATION   /ˌɪnklɪˈneɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la inclinación; la tendencia

Meaning:  a tendency to act or feel in a particular way

Example:  She has a natural inclination toward helping others.

→  Ella tiene una inclinación natural a ayudar a los demás.

INCLUSIVE   /ɪnˈkluːsɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  inclusivo/a

Meaning:  including everything or everyone, especially avoiding exclusion

Example:  The school promotes an inclusive environment for all students.

→  La escuela promueve un entorno inclusivo para todos los estudiantes.

INCONSISTENCY   /ˌɪnkənˈsɪstənsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la inconsistencia; la incoherencia

Meaning:  a lack of agreement between different parts or statements

Example:  There were several inconsistencies in his story.

→  Había varias inconsistencias en su historia.

INCONSISTENT   /ˌɪnkənˈsɪstənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  inconsistente; incoherente

Meaning:  not staying the same, or not in agreement with something else

Example:  His statements were inconsistent with the evidence.

→  Sus declaraciones eran incoherentes con las pruebas.

INCORPORATE   /ɪnˈkɔːrpəreɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  incorporar

Meaning:  to include something as part of a whole

Example:  The new design incorporates several safety features.

→  El nuevo diseño incorpora varias características de seguridad.

INCUR   /ɪnˈkɜːr/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  incurrir en

Meaning:  to experience something unpleasant as a result of your own actions, such as a cost

Example:  You may incur extra charges for late payment.

→  Puedes incurrir en cargos adicionales por pago tardío.

INDIFFERENCE   /ɪnˈdɪfərəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la indiferencia

Meaning:  a lack of interest or concern

Example:  His indifference to the problem frustrated everyone.

→  Su indiferencia ante el problema frustró a todos.

INDISPENSABLE   /ˌɪndɪˈspɛnsəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  indispensable; imprescindible

Meaning:  absolutely necessary; too important to be without

Example:  She has become an indispensable member of the team.

→  Ella se ha convertido en un miembro imprescindible del equipo.

INEQUALITY   /ˌɪnɪˈkwɒləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la desigualdad

Meaning:  unfair differences between groups in society, wealth, or opportunity

Example:  The report focuses on income inequality.

→  El informe se centra en la desigualdad de ingresos.

INFER   /ɪnˈfɜːr/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  inferir; deducir

Meaning:  to reach a conclusion based on evidence and reasoning

Example:  From her tone, I inferred that she was upset.

→  Por su tono, inferí que estaba molesta.

INFERENCE   /ˈɪnfərəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la inferencia; la deducción

Meaning:  a conclusion reached based on evidence and reasoning

Example:  That's a reasonable inference given the facts.

→  Esa es una deducción razonable dados los hechos.

INFLICT   /ɪnˈflɪkt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  infligir

Meaning:  to cause pain, suffering, or damage to someone

Example:  The storm inflicted serious damage on the coast.

→  La tormenta infligió graves daños en la costa.

INFRASTRUCTURE   /ˈɪnfrəstrʌktʃər/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la infraestructura

Meaning:  the basic systems and structures a country or organization needs, such as roads and power

Example:  The government is investing in new transport infrastructure.

→  El gobierno está invirtiendo en nueva infraestructura de transporte.

INGENUITY   /ˌɪndʒəˈnuːəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el ingenio

Meaning:  the quality of being clever and inventive, especially in solving problems

Example:  They solved the problem with real ingenuity.

→  Resolvieron el problema con verdadero ingenio.

INHERENT   /ɪnˈhɪrənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  inherente

Meaning:  existing as a natural or basic part of something

Example:  There are inherent risks in any investment.

→  Hay riesgos inherentes en cualquier inversión.

INITIATE   /ɪˈnɪʃieɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  iniciar

Meaning:  to start a process or action

Example:  They initiated talks to resolve the dispute.

→  Iniciaron conversaciones para resolver el conflicto.

INNOVATION   /ˌɪnəˈveɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la innovación

Meaning:  a new idea, method, or invention

Example:  The company is known for its innovation in design.

→  La empresa es conocida por su innovación en diseño.

INNOVATIVE   /ˈɪnəveɪtɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  innovador/a

Meaning:  using new methods or ideas

Example:  They came up with an innovative solution to the problem.

→  Se les ocurrió una solución innovadora al problema.

INSIGHT   /ˈɪnsaɪt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la perspicacia; el entendimiento profundo

Meaning:  a clear understanding of a complex problem or situation

Example:  The book offers great insight into the author's childhood.

→  El libro ofrece un gran entendimiento sobre la infancia del autor.

INSTINCT   /ˈɪnstɪŋkt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el instinto

Meaning:  a natural tendency to behave in a certain way, without thinking

Example:  Her first instinct was to call for help.

→  Su primer instinto fue pedir ayuda.

INTEGRATE   /ˈɪntɪɡreɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  integrar; integrarse

Meaning:  to combine one thing with another so they work as a whole

Example:  The new software integrates well with our existing system.

→  El nuevo software se integra bien con nuestro sistema actual.

INTEGRITY   /ɪnˈtɛɡrəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la integridad

Meaning:  the quality of being honest and having strong moral principles

Example:  She is known for her integrity and honesty.

→  Ella es conocida por su integridad y honestidad.

INTENSIFY   /ɪnˈtɛnsɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  intensificar; intensificarse

Meaning:  to become or make something more intense or extreme

Example:  The rain intensified as the storm approached.

→  La lluvia se intensificó a medida que se acercaba la tormenta.

INTERVENE   /ˌɪntərˈviːn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  intervenir

Meaning:  to become involved in a situation in order to change or influence it

Example:  The teacher intervened to stop the argument.

→  La profesora intervino para detener la discusión.

INTRICATE   /ˈɪntrɪkət/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  intrincado/a; complejo/a

Meaning:  very detailed and complicated

Example:  The dress has an intricate lace pattern.

→  El vestido tiene un patrón de encaje intrincado.

INTRIGUE   /ɪnˈtriːɡ/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  intrigar; fascinar; la intriga

Meaning:  to arouse curiosity or interest; secret plotting or scheming

Example:  The mystery of the old house intrigued the whole town.

→  El misterio de la casa vieja intrigó a todo el pueblo.

INTRINSIC   /ɪnˈtrɪnzɪk/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  intrínseco/a

Meaning:  belonging naturally to something, as a basic part of it

Example:  The painting has intrinsic artistic value.

→  El cuadro tiene un valor artístico intrínseco.

INTUITION   /ˌɪntuˈɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la intuición

Meaning:  the ability to understand something instinctively, without reasoning

Example:  Trust your intuition when making this decision.

→  Confía en tu intuición al tomar esta decisión.

INVARIABLE   /ɪnˈvɛriəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  invariable

Meaning:  never changing

Example:  His response was invariable no matter what she asked.

→  Su respuesta era invariable sin importar lo que ella preguntara.

INVENTORY   /ˈɪnvəntɔːri/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el inventario

Meaning:  a complete list of items, such as goods in a store or warehouse

Example:  We need to update the store's inventory.

→  Necesitamos actualizar el inventario de la tienda.

INVOKE   /ɪnˈvoʊk/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  invocar

Meaning:  to refer to something, such as a law or idea, to support your argument

Example:  The lawyer invoked a previous court ruling.

→  El abogado invocó un fallo judicial anterior.

ISOLATE   /ˈaɪsəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  aislar

Meaning:  to separate someone or something from others

Example:  They isolated the patient to prevent the infection from spreading.

→  Aislaron al paciente para evitar que la infección se propagara.

JURISDICTION   /ˌdʒʊrɪsˈdɪkʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la jurisdicción

Meaning:  the official power to make legal decisions in a particular area

Example:  This case falls outside the court's jurisdiction.

→  Este caso está fuera de la jurisdicción del tribunal.

LEGACY   /ˈlɛɡəsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el legado

Meaning:  something left behind by a person or event, especially their long-term impact

Example:  His legacy as a teacher will live on for generations.

→  Su legado como profesor perdurará por generaciones.

LEGISLATURE   /ˈlɛdʒɪsleɪtʃər/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la legislatura; el poder legislativo

Meaning:  a group of people with the power to make laws for a country or region

Example:  The bill was approved by the state legislature.

→  El proyecto de ley fue aprobado por la legislatura estatal.

LEGITIMACY   /lɪˈdʒɪtɪməsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la legitimidad

Meaning:  the quality of being lawful, valid, or generally accepted as correct

Example:  The election's legitimacy was questioned by opposition parties.

→  La legitimidad de las elecciones fue cuestionada por los partidos de oposición.

LEGITIMATE   /lɪˈdʒɪtɪmət/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  legítimo/a

Meaning:  lawful or valid; reasonable and justified

Example:  She has a legitimate reason to be upset.

→  Ella tiene una razón legítima para estar molesta.

LEVERAGE   /ˈlɛvərɪdʒ/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  la influencia; el apalancamiento; aprovechar

Meaning:  power or influence used to achieve something; to use something to maximum advantage

Example:  The union used its leverage to negotiate better pay.

→  El sindicato usó su influencia para negociar un mejor salario.

LIABILITY   /ˌlaɪəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la responsabilidad legal; el pasivo (finanzas)

Meaning:  legal responsibility for something, especially damage or debt

Example:  The company accepted liability for the accident.

→  La empresa aceptó la responsabilidad legal por el accidente.

LUCRATIVE   /ˈluːkrətɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  lucrativo/a; rentable

Meaning:  producing a lot of money or profit

Example:  She turned her hobby into a lucrative business.

→  Ella convirtió su pasatiempo en un negocio lucrativo.

MAGNITUDE   /ˈmæɡnɪtuːd/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la magnitud

Meaning:  the great size or importance of something

Example:  We didn't understand the magnitude of the problem at first.

→  Al principio no entendimos la magnitud del problema.

MANDATE   /ˈmændeɪt/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  el mandato; ordenar; exigir

Meaning:  official authority to do something, especially given by voters; to officially require something

Example:  The new mayor has a clear mandate for reform.

→  El nuevo alcalde tiene un claro mandato para la reforma.

MANIFESTATION   /ˌmænɪfəˈsteɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la manifestación (muestra)

Meaning:  a visible sign or expression of something

Example:  His anger was a manifestation of his frustration at work.

→  Su enfado fue una manifestación de su frustración en el trabajo.

MANIPULATE   /məˈnɪpjəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  manipular

Meaning:  to control or influence someone or something unfairly or skillfully

Example:  She felt manipulated into agreeing to the deal.

→  Ella se sintió manipulada para aceptar el trato.

MARGINAL   /ˈmɑːrdʒɪnəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  marginal

Meaning:  small in size or importance; barely significant

Example:  There was only a marginal improvement in sales.

→  Solo hubo una mejora marginal en las ventas.

MARGINALIZATION   /ˌmɑːrdʒɪnələˈzeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la marginación

Meaning:  the process of pushing a group of people to the edges of society

Example:  The report addresses the marginalization of rural communities.

→  El informe aborda la marginación de las comunidades rurales.

Note:  British spelling: "marginalisation".

MECHANISM   /ˈmɛkənɪzəm/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el mecanismo

Meaning:  a system or process used to achieve a result; a part of a machine

Example:  The government needs a better mechanism for handling complaints.

→  El gobierno necesita un mejor mecanismo para gestionar las quejas.

MEDIATE   /ˈmiːdieɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  mediar

Meaning:  to try to help two sides settle a disagreement

Example:  A neutral party mediated the negotiations.

→  Una parte neutral medió en las negociaciones.

MEDIATOR   /ˈmiːdieɪtər/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el mediador; la mediadora

Meaning:  a person who helps two sides reach an agreement

Example:  They hired a mediator to resolve the dispute.

→  Contrataron a un mediador para resolver el conflicto.

METHODOLOGY   /ˌmɛθəˈdɒlədʒi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la metodología

Meaning:  a system of methods used in a particular activity or field of study

Example:  The study used a rigorous research methodology.

→  El estudio utilizó una metodología de investigación rigurosa.

METICULOUS   /məˈtɪkjələs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  meticuloso/a

Meaning:  very careful and precise about details

Example:  She's meticulous about checking her work.

→  Ella es meticulosa a la hora de revisar su trabajo.

MIGRATE   /ˈmaɪɡreɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  migrar; emigrar

Meaning:  to move from one place to another, especially to find work or better conditions

Example:  Many birds migrate south for the winter.

→  Muchas aves migran hacia el sur en invierno.

MINIMIZE   /ˈmɪnɪmaɪz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  minimizar

Meaning:  to reduce something to the smallest possible amount

Example:  We took steps to minimize the risk of injury.

→  Tomamos medidas para minimizar el riesgo de lesiones.

Note:  British spelling: "minimise".

MITIGATE   /ˈmɪtɪɡeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  mitigar

Meaning:  to make something less severe or serious

Example:  The new policy aims to mitigate the effects of climate change.

→  La nueva política busca mitigar los efectos del cambio climático.

MODIFY   /ˈmɒdɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  modificar

Meaning:  to make small changes to something in order to improve it

Example:  They modified the design after customer feedback.

→  Modificaron el diseño tras los comentarios de los clientes.

MOMENTUM   /moʊˈmɛntəm/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el impulso; el ímpetu

Meaning:  the force that keeps something moving forward or developing

Example:  The campaign has gained a lot of momentum recently.

→  La campaña ha ganado mucho impulso últimamente.

MONOPOLY   /məˈnɒpəli/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el monopolio

Meaning:  complete control of a market by one company or group

Example:  The company holds a monopoly on local electricity supply.

→  La empresa tiene el monopolio del suministro eléctrico local.

MORALE   /məˈræl/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la moral (ánimo)

Meaning:  the level of confidence and enthusiasm felt by a person or group

Example:  Staff morale has improved since the new manager arrived.

→  La moral del personal ha mejorado desde que llegó el nuevo gerente.

MOTIVE   /ˈmoʊtɪv/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el motivo

Meaning:  a reason for doing something

Example:  Police are still investigating the motive for the crime.

→  La policía todavía está investigando el motivo del crimen.

MOUNT   /maʊnt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  aumentar; organizar; montar

Meaning:  to increase gradually; to organize a campaign or event

Example:  Pressure is mounting on the company to apologize.

→  La presión está aumentando sobre la empresa para que se disculpe.

NEGLECT   /nɪˈɡlɛkt/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  descuidar; el descuido

Meaning:  to fail to take care of someone or something properly

Example:  He neglected his health for years because of work.

→  Descuidó su salud durante años a causa del trabajo.

NOMINATE   /ˈnɒmɪneɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  nominar; nombrar; proponer

Meaning:  to formally suggest someone for a position, award, or role

Example:  She was nominated for an award for her research.

→  Ella fue nominada a un premio por su investigación.

NOSTALGIA   /nɒˈstældʒə/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la nostalgia

Meaning:  a sentimental longing for the past

Example:  Looking at old photos always fills me with nostalgia.

→  Mirar fotos antiguas siempre me llena de nostalgia.

NOTION   /ˈnoʊʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la noción; la idea

Meaning:  a belief or idea about something

Example:  He rejected the notion that money brings happiness.

→  Él rechazó la noción de que el dinero trae felicidad.

NUANCE   /ˈnuːɑːns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el matiz

Meaning:  a small, subtle difference in meaning, tone, or expression

Example:  A good translator captures the nuances of the original text.

→  Un buen traductor capta los matices del texto original.

NUANCED   /ˈnuːɑːnst/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  matizado/a

Meaning:  showing or having subtle differences or shades of meaning

Example:  She gave a nuanced answer instead of a simple yes or no.

→  Ella dio una respuesta matizada en vez de un simple sí o no.

NURTURE   /ˈnɜːrtʃər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  nutrir; fomentar; criar

Meaning:  to care for and help someone or something grow and develop

Example:  Good teachers nurture their students' curiosity.

→  Los buenos profesores fomentan la curiosidad de sus estudiantes.

OBLIGATION   /ˌɒblɪˈɡeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la obligación

Meaning:  a duty to do something because of a law, promise, or moral principle

Example:  You have no obligation to accept the offer.

→  No tienes ninguna obligación de aceptar la oferta.

OBSCURE   /əbˈskjʊr/  •  adjective; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  oscuro/a; poco conocido/a; oscurecer

Meaning:  not well known; not clear or easy to understand; to make unclear

Example:  He quoted an obscure poet I'd never heard of.

→  Citó a un poeta poco conocido del que nunca había oído hablar.

OBSESSION   /əbˈsɛʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la obsesión

Meaning:  a state where you think about something all the time

Example:  His obsession with football started as a child.

→  Su obsesión por el fútbol empezó de niño.

OBSOLETE   /ˌɒbsəˈliːt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  obsoleto/a

Meaning:  no longer used because something newer has replaced it

Example:  This technology will soon become obsolete.

→  Esta tecnología pronto se volverá obsoleta.

ORIENT   /ˈɔːrient/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  orientar; orientarse

Meaning:  to position or direct something toward a particular purpose; to find your bearings

Example:  The course is oriented toward beginners.

→  El curso está orientado a principiantes.

Note:  British spelling: "orientate".

OUTLOOK   /ˈaʊtlʊk/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la perspectiva; el panorama

Meaning:  a person's general attitude toward life; the future prospects of something

Example:  She has a very positive outlook on life.

→  Ella tiene una perspectiva muy positiva ante la vida.

OUTWEIGH   /ˌaʊtˈweɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  pesar más que; superar en importancia

Meaning:  to be more important or significant than something else

Example:  The benefits of the plan outweigh the risks.

→  Los beneficios del plan superan a los riesgos.

OVERHAUL   /ˈoʊvərhɔːl/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  la revisión general; revisar a fondo

Meaning:  a thorough examination and improvement of something; to make major changes to improve something

Example:  The company plans a major overhaul of its website.

→  La empresa planea una revisión a fondo de su página web.

OVERLAP   /ˌoʊvərˈlæp/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  superponerse; coincidir; la superposición

Meaning:  to partly cover or coincide with something else

Example:  Our work schedules overlap on Tuesdays.

→  Nuestros horarios de trabajo se superponen los martes.

PARADOX   /ˈpærədɒks/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la paradoja

Meaning:  a statement or situation that seems contradictory but may be true

Example:  It's a paradox that working less can make you more productive.

→  Es una paradoja que trabajar menos pueda hacerte más productivo.

PARAMETER   /pəˈræmɪtər/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el parámetro

Meaning:  a limit or boundary that defines what is possible; a measurable factor

Example:  We need to define the parameters of the project.

→  Necesitamos definir los parámetros del proyecto.

PERCEIVE   /pərˈsiːv/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  percibir

Meaning:  to become aware of something through the senses; to understand or interpret something

Example:  She perceived a change in his attitude right away.

→  Ella percibió un cambio en su actitud enseguida.

PERCEPTION   /pərˈsɛpʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la percepción

Meaning:  the way someone understands or interprets something

Example:  Public perception of the company has improved.

→  La percepción pública de la empresa ha mejorado.

PERPETUATE   /pərˈpɛtʃueɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  perpetuar

Meaning:  to make something continue for a long time

Example:  The policy risks perpetuating existing inequalities.

→  La política corre el riesgo de perpetuar las desigualdades existentes.

PERSIST   /pərˈsɪst/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  persistir

Meaning:  to continue doing something despite difficulty; to continue to exist

Example:  If the pain persists, see a doctor.

→  Si el dolor persiste, consulta a un médico.

PERVASIVE   /pərˈveɪsɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  omnipresente; generalizado/a

Meaning:  existing in every part of something; widespread

Example:  Social media has become pervasive in daily life.

→  Las redes sociales se han vuelto omnipresentes en la vida diaria.

PHENOMENON   /fəˈnɒmɪnən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el fenómeno

Meaning:  a fact, situation, or event that can be observed to exist or happen

Example:  This is a common phenomenon in large cities.

→  Este es un fenómeno común en las grandes ciudades.

Note:  Irregular plural: phenomenon → phenomena.

PINPOINT   /ˈpɪnpɔɪnt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  identificar con precisión; localizar

Meaning:  to find or explain the exact location or cause of something

Example:  Doctors couldn't pinpoint the cause of her symptoms.

→  Los médicos no pudieron identificar la causa exacta de sus síntomas.

PLAUSIBILITY   /ˌplɔːzəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la plausibilidad; la verosimilitud

Meaning:  the quality of seeming reasonable or probably true

Example:  Experts questioned the plausibility of his explanation.

→  Los expertos cuestionaron la verosimilitud de su explicación.

PLAUSIBLE   /ˈplɔːzəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  plausible; verosímil

Meaning:  seeming reasonable or probable

Example:  That's a plausible explanation for what happened.

→  Esa es una explicación plausible de lo que pasó.

POLARIZATION   /ˌpoʊlərəˈzeɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la polarización

Meaning:  the division of people into two strongly opposing groups

Example:  Political polarization has increased in recent years.

→  La polarización política ha aumentado en los últimos años.

Note:  British spelling: "polarisation".

POSTULATE   /ˈpɒstʃəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  postular; plantear

Meaning:  to suggest something as a basic principle from which conclusions can be drawn

Example:  The theory postulates that the universe is expanding.

→  La teoría postula que el universo se está expandiendo.

PRECEDE   /prɪˈsiːd/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  preceder

Meaning:  to come before something in time, order, or importance

Example:  A short introduction precedes the main article.

→  Una breve introducción precede al artículo principal.

PRECEDENT   /ˈprɛsɪdənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el precedente

Meaning:  an earlier example or decision used as a guide for future cases

Example:  The ruling sets an important legal precedent.

→  El fallo sienta un precedente legal importante.

PREDICAMENT   /prɪˈdɪkəmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el aprieto; la situación difícil

Meaning:  a difficult or unpleasant situation

Example:  She found herself in a difficult predicament after losing her keys.

→  Se encontró en un aprieto después de perder sus llaves.

PREDOMINANT   /prɪˈdɒmɪnənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  predominante

Meaning:  more common, powerful, or noticeable than others

Example:  Spanish is the predominant language in that region.

→  El español es el idioma predominante en esa región.

PREDOMINATE   /prɪˈdɒmɪneɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  predominar

Meaning:  to be the largest or most important part of something

Example:  Small farms predominate in this part of the country.

→  Las pequeñas granjas predominan en esta parte del país.

PREMISE   /ˈprɛmɪs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la premisa

Meaning:  an idea or theory on which a statement or action is based

Example:  The whole plan is based on a false premise.

→  Todo el plan se basa en una premisa falsa.

PREREQUISITE   /ˌpriːˈrɛkwəzɪt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el requisito previo

Meaning:  something required before something else can happen

Example:  Basic math is a prerequisite for this course.

→  Las matemáticas básicas son un requisito previo para este curso.

PRESTIGE   /prɛˈstiːʒ/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el prestigio

Meaning:  widespread respect and admiration based on achievements or reputation

Example:  The university has a lot of international prestige.

→  La universidad tiene mucho prestigio internacional.

PRESUME   /prɪˈzuːm/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  presumir; suponer

Meaning:  to believe something is true without proof

Example:  I presume you've already read the report.

→  Supongo que ya has leído el informe.

PRESUMPTION   /prɪˈzʌmpʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la presunción; la suposición

Meaning:  an idea assumed to be true without proof

Example:  There's a legal presumption of innocence.

→  Existe una presunción legal de inocencia.

PRESUPPOSE   /ˌpriːsəˈpoʊz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  presuponer

Meaning:  to assume something is true before it has been proven

Example:  The question presupposes that a solution already exists.

→  La pregunta presupone que ya existe una solución.

PREVAIL   /prɪˈveɪl/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  prevalecer

Meaning:  to win or succeed despite difficulty; to be the most common condition

Example:  Justice prevailed in the end.

→  La justicia prevaleció al final.

PREVALENCE   /ˈprɛvələns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la prevalencia

Meaning:  how common or widespread something is

Example:  The prevalence of the disease has decreased in recent years.

→  La prevalencia de la enfermedad ha disminuido en los últimos años.

PREVALENT   /ˈprɛvələnt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  prevalente; predominante

Meaning:  widespread or common at a particular time or place

Example:  This disease is more prevalent in older adults.

→  Esta enfermedad es más prevalente en adultos mayores.

PROCLAIM   /proʊˈkleɪm/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  proclamar

Meaning:  to announce something officially or publicly

Example:  The country proclaimed its independence in 1821.

→  El país proclamó su independencia en 1821.

PROFICIENCY   /prəˈfɪʃənsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la competencia; el dominio (de un idioma)

Meaning:  a high level of skill or competence in something

Example:  She has a high level of proficiency in French.

→  Ella tiene un alto nivel de dominio del francés.

PROFICIENT   /prəˈfɪʃənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  competente; hábil

Meaning:  skilled and competent at something

Example:  He's quite proficient in several programming languages.

→  Él es bastante competente en varios lenguajes de programación.

PROLIFERATION   /prəˌlɪfəˈreɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la proliferación

Meaning:  a rapid increase in the number or amount of something

Example:  There's been a proliferation of food delivery apps.

→  Ha habido una proliferación de aplicaciones de reparto de comida.

PROLIFIC   /prəˈlɪfɪk/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  prolífico/a

Meaning:  producing a large number of works, results, or offspring

Example:  She's a prolific writer, publishing a book almost every year.

→  Ella es una escritora prolífica, publica un libro casi cada año.

PROLONG   /prəˈlɔːŋ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  prolongar

Meaning:  to make something last longer

Example:  The treatment can help prolong the patient's life.

→  El tratamiento puede ayudar a prolongar la vida del paciente.

PROPAGATE   /ˈprɒpəɡeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  propagar; propagarse

Meaning:  to spread an idea or belief widely; to reproduce a plant

Example:  The rumor propagated quickly through the office.

→  El rumor se propagó rápidamente por la oficina.

PROSECUTE   /ˈprɒsɪkjuːt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  procesar; enjuiciar

Meaning:  to formally accuse someone of a crime in a court of law

Example:  He was prosecuted for tax evasion.

→  Fue procesado por evasión fiscal.

PROSPECTUS   /prəˈspɛktəs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el prospecto; el folleto informativo

Meaning:  a printed document giving details about a school, company, or investment

Example:  The university sent us a prospectus with course details.

→  La universidad nos envió un prospecto con los detalles de los cursos.

PROTOCOL   /ˈproʊtəkɒl/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el protocolo

Meaning:  a set of official rules for how something should be done

Example:  They followed the safety protocol during the fire drill.

→  Siguieron el protocolo de seguridad durante el simulacro de incendio.

PROVISION   /prəˈvɪʒən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la provisión; la disposición (legal)

Meaning:  the act of supplying something needed; a clause in a legal document

Example:  The contract includes a provision for early termination.

→  El contrato incluye una disposición para la rescisión anticipada.

PROVOKE   /prəˈvoʊk/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  provocar

Meaning:  to cause a reaction, especially anger; to deliberately annoy someone

Example:  The comment provoked an angry response.

→  El comentario provocó una respuesta furiosa.

PROXIMITY   /prɒkˈsɪməti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la proximidad; la cercanía

Meaning:  nearness in space or time

Example:  The hotel's proximity to the beach was very convenient.

→  La cercanía del hotel a la playa fue muy conveniente.

RADIATE   /ˈreɪdieɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  irradiar

Meaning:  to send out light, heat, or energy; to clearly show a feeling

Example:  She radiates confidence whenever she speaks.

→  Ella irradia confianza cada vez que habla.

RAMPANT   /ˈræmpənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  desenfrenado/a; rampante

Meaning:  spreading quickly and uncontrollably, especially something bad

Example:  Corruption was rampant in the old regime.

→  La corrupción era desenfrenada en el antiguo régimen.

RATIFY   /ˈrætɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  ratificar

Meaning:  to formally approve an agreement, treaty, or decision

Example:  The government ratified the new trade agreement.

→  El gobierno ratificó el nuevo acuerdo comercial.

RATIONALE   /ˌræʃəˈnæl/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la razón fundamental; el fundamento

Meaning:  the underlying reasons for a decision or course of action

Example:  Can you explain the rationale behind this policy?

→  ¿Puedes explicar el fundamento de esta política?

RECIPROCITY   /ˌrɛsɪˈprɒsəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la reciprocidad

Meaning:  the practice of exchanging things with others for mutual benefit

Example:  The trade deal is based on reciprocity between the two countries.

→  El acuerdo comercial se basa en la reciprocidad entre los dos países.

RECTIFY   /ˈrɛktɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  rectificar; corregir

Meaning:  to correct something that is wrong

Example:  We're working to rectify the error in the report.

→  Estamos trabajando para rectificar el error en el informe.

REDUNDANCY   /rɪˈdʌndənsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la redundancia; el despido (Reino Unido)

Meaning:  the state of being unnecessary because something is repeated; loss of a job because it's no longer needed

Example:  There's some redundancy in how the report repeats information.

→  Hay cierta redundancia en cómo el informe repite la información.

REDUNDANT   /rɪˈdʌndənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  redundante; despedido/a (Reino Unido)

Meaning:  no longer needed because it's unnecessary or repeated

Example:  Some of the information in the second chapter feels redundant.

→  Parte de la información del segundo capítulo se siente redundante.

REFERENDUM   /ˌrɛfəˈrɛndəm/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el referéndum

Meaning:  a public vote on a single political question

Example:  The country held a referendum on the new law.

→  El país celebró un referéndum sobre la nueva ley.

REFINE   /rɪˈfaɪn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  refinar; perfeccionar

Meaning:  to improve something by making small changes; to remove impurities from a substance

Example:  We need to refine our marketing strategy.

→  Necesitamos perfeccionar nuestra estrategia de marketing.

REFINEMENT   /rɪˈfaɪnmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el refinamiento; la mejora

Meaning:  a small improvement or change made to something

Example:  The new model includes several refinements over the old one.

→  El nuevo modelo incluye varias mejoras respecto al anterior.

REFRAIN   /rɪˈfreɪn/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  abstenerse; el estribillo

Meaning:  to stop yourself from doing something; a repeated part of a song

Example:  Please refrain from using your phone during the meeting.

→  Por favor, absténganse de usar el móvil durante la reunión.

Common:  refrain from

REGULATE   /ˈrɛɡjəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  regular

Meaning:  to control something using rules; to control the rate or speed of something

Example:  This agency regulates the banking industry.

→  Esta agencia regula el sector bancario.

REINFORCEMENT   /ˌriːɪnˈfɔːrsmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el refuerzo

Meaning:  something added to strengthen or support; extra people or equipment sent to help

Example:  The bridge needed extra reinforcement to support the weight.

→  El puente necesitaba un refuerzo adicional para soportar el peso.

REINSTATE   /ˌriːɪnˈsteɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  reincorporar; restablecer

Meaning:  to give someone back their previous job or position; to bring something back

Example:  He was reinstated to his position after the investigation.

→  Fue reincorporado a su puesto tras la investigación.

REITERATE   /riˈɪtəreɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  reiterar

Meaning:  to say something again for emphasis or clarity

Example:  She reiterated her commitment to the project.

→  Ella reiteró su compromiso con el proyecto.

RELIABILITY   /rɪˌlaɪəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la fiabilidad

Meaning:  the quality of being trustworthy and consistent

Example:  This car is known for its reliability.

→  Este coche es conocido por su fiabilidad.

RELINQUISH   /rɪˈlɪŋkwɪʃ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  renunciar a; ceder

Meaning:  to give up a right, position, or possession

Example:  He relinquished his position as CEO last year.

→  Renunció a su cargo de director ejecutivo el año pasado.

RENDER   /ˈrɛndər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  hacer que; volver; prestar (ayuda)

Meaning:  to cause something to be in a particular state; to provide a service

Example:  The storm rendered the road impassable.

→  La tormenta volvió intransitable la carretera.

RENOUNCE   /rɪˈnaʊns/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  renunciar a

Meaning:  to formally give up a right, title, or belief

Example:  He renounced his citizenship after moving abroad.

→  Renunció a su ciudadanía tras mudarse al extranjero.

REPERCUSSION   /ˌriːpərˈkʌʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la repercusión

Meaning:  an indirect effect or consequence of an action

Example:  The decision had serious repercussions for the whole industry.

→  La decisión tuvo serias repercusiones para toda la industria.

REPLICATE   /ˈrɛplɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  replicar; reproducir

Meaning:  to make an exact copy of something, or to repeat a result

Example:  Scientists were able to replicate the experiment's results.

→  Los científicos pudieron replicar los resultados del experimento.

REPRESS   /rɪˈprɛs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  reprimir

Meaning:  to control or hold back a feeling, or to control people by force

Example:  She tried to repress her anger during the meeting.

→  Ella intentó reprimir su enfado durante la reunión.

REPRODUCE   /ˌriːprəˈduːs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  reproducir; reproducirse

Meaning:  to make a copy of something; to produce offspring

Example:  Rabbits reproduce very quickly.

→  Los conejos se reproducen muy rápido.

RESENT   /rɪˈzɛnt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  resentirse por; guardar rencor

Meaning:  to feel bitter or angry about something unfair

Example:  She resented having to work every weekend.

→  Ella se resentía por tener que trabajar todos los fines de semana.

RESIGN   /rɪˈzaɪn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  dimitir; renunciar

Meaning:  to formally give up a job or position

Example:  He resigned from his position last week.

→  Dimitió de su puesto la semana pasada.

RESILIENCE   /rɪˈzɪliəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la resiliencia

Meaning:  the ability to recover quickly from difficulties

Example:  She showed great resilience after losing her job.

→  Ella mostró gran resiliencia tras perder su trabajo.

RESILIENT   /rɪˈzɪliənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  resiliente

Meaning:  able to recover quickly from difficulties

Example:  Children can be surprisingly resilient after hard times.

→  Los niños pueden ser sorprendentemente resilientes después de momentos difíciles.

RESONANCE   /ˈrɛzənəns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la resonancia

Meaning:  the power of a message or idea to have a strong effect on people; a deep, rich sound

Example:  Her speech had real resonance with the audience.

→  Su discurso tuvo una gran resonancia entre el público.

RESONATE   /ˈrɛzəneɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  resonar; tener eco

Meaning:  to have a strong emotional effect on someone; to produce a deep, ringing sound

Example:  Her story resonated with many young readers.

→  Su historia resonó entre muchos lectores jóvenes.

Common:  resonate with

RESTRAIN   /rɪˈstreɪn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  contener; refrenar

Meaning:  to hold someone or something back from doing something

Example:  He could barely restrain his anger.

→  Apenas podía contener su enfado.

RETRIEVE   /rɪˈtriːv/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  recuperar

Meaning:  to get or bring back something

Example:  She retrieved her bag from the lost and found.

→  Ella recuperó su bolso en objetos perdidos.

REVENUE   /ˈrɛvənuː/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  los ingresos

Meaning:  the money a business or government receives regularly

Example:  The company's revenue increased by ten percent last year.

→  Los ingresos de la empresa aumentaron un diez por ciento el año pasado.

REVOKE   /rɪˈvoʊk/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  revocar

Meaning:  to officially cancel a right, permission, or agreement

Example:  His driving license was revoked after the accident.

→  Le revocaron el carnet de conducir tras el accidente.

RHETORIC   /ˈrɛtərɪk/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la retórica

Meaning:  language designed to persuade or influence people, sometimes without real substance

Example:  His speech was full of empty rhetoric.

→  Su discurso estaba lleno de retórica vacía.

RIGOR   /ˈrɪɡər/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el rigor

Meaning:  strict care and thoroughness, especially in intellectual work

Example:  The study was conducted with great scientific rigor.

→  El estudio se realizó con gran rigor científico.

Note:  British spelling: "rigour".

RIGOROUS   /ˈrɪɡərəs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  riguroso/a

Meaning:  very careful, thorough, and strict

Example:  The product went through rigorous testing before release.

→  El producto pasó por pruebas rigurosas antes de su lanzamiento.

ROBUST   /roʊˈbʌst/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  robusto/a; sólido/a

Meaning:  strong and healthy; not easily broken or weakened

Example:  The economy showed robust growth this quarter.

→  La economía mostró un crecimiento sólido este trimestre.

SANCTION   /ˈsæŋkʃən/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  la sanción; sancionar; aprobar

Meaning:  an official penalty against a country or organization; to officially approve something

Example:  The country faces economic sanctions from other nations.

→  El país enfrenta sanciones económicas de otras naciones.

SCARCITY   /ˈskɛrsəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la escasez

Meaning:  a lack of something that is needed

Example:  There's a scarcity of affordable housing in the city.

→  Hay escasez de vivienda asequible en la ciudad.

SCOPE   /skoʊp/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el alcance

Meaning:  the range of things a subject, project, or activity covers

Example:  The scope of the project has grown since we started.

→  El alcance del proyecto ha crecido desde que empezamos.

SCRUTINIZE   /ˈskruːtənaɪz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  escudriñar; examinar minuciosamente

Meaning:  to examine something very carefully

Example:  The auditors scrutinized every transaction.

→  Los auditores examinaron minuciosamente cada transacción.

Note:  British spelling: "scrutinise".

SCRUTINY   /ˈskruːtəni/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el escrutinio

Meaning:  careful and thorough examination of something

Example:  The company's finances are under close scrutiny.

→  Las finanzas de la empresa están bajo un escrutinio riguroso.

SENTIMENT   /ˈsɛntəmənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el sentimiento; la opinión

Meaning:  a feeling or opinion, especially one shared by a group of people

Example:  Public sentiment has shifted in favor of the new law.

→  El sentimiento público ha cambiado a favor de la nueva ley.

SEVER   /ˈsɛvər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  cortar; romper (relaciones)

Meaning:  to cut off completely, or to end a relationship abruptly

Example:  The company severed all ties with the supplier.

→  La empresa rompió todos los lazos con el proveedor.

SHATTER   /ˈʃætər/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  hacer añicos; romperse en pedazos; destrozar

Meaning:  to break suddenly into many small pieces; to destroy something completely, like hope

Example:  The glass shattered when it hit the floor.

→  El vaso se hizo añicos al caer al suelo.

SIMULATE   /ˈsɪmjəleɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  simular

Meaning:  to create a situation that looks or feels like a real one, for training or testing

Example:  The training program simulates real emergency situations.

→  El programa de formación simula situaciones de emergencia reales.

SIMULATION   /ˌsɪmjəˈleɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la simulación

Meaning:  an imitation of a real situation or process, used for study or training

Example:  Pilots train using flight simulations.

→  Los pilotos se entrenan usando simulaciones de vuelo.

SOLICIT   /səˈlɪsɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  solicitar

Meaning:  to ask for something, such as money, help, or opinions

Example:  The charity solicited donations from local businesses.

→  La organización benéfica solicitó donaciones de empresas locales.

SOLIDARITY   /ˌsɒlɪˈdærəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la solidaridad

Meaning:  unity and mutual support among a group of people

Example:  Workers showed solidarity by going on strike together.

→  Los trabajadores mostraron solidaridad haciendo huelga juntos.

SOOTHE   /suːð/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  calmar; aliviar

Meaning:  to make someone feel calmer, or to relieve pain

Example:  She sang softly to soothe the crying baby.

→  Ella cantó suavemente para calmar al bebé que lloraba.

SPECTRUM   /ˈspɛktrəm/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el espectro

Meaning:  a wide range of related qualities, ideas, or things

Example:  The party represents a broad spectrum of political views.

→  El partido representa un amplio espectro de opiniones políticas.

SPECULATION   /ˌspɛkjəˈleɪʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la especulación

Meaning:  guessing about something without knowing all the facts

Example:  There's a lot of speculation about who will be promoted.

→  Hay mucha especulación sobre quién será ascendido.

SPONTANEITY   /ˌspɒntəˈneɪəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la espontaneidad

Meaning:  the quality of doing things naturally, without planning

→  Me encanta la espontaneidad de un viaje por carretera de última hora.

SPORADIC   /spəˈrædɪk/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  esporádico/a

Meaning:  happening only occasionally and without a regular pattern

Example:  There were sporadic showers throughout the day.

→  Hubo lluvias esporádicas durante todo el día.

STAGNATE   /ˈstæɡneɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  estancarse

Meaning:  to stop developing or growing

Example:  Wages have stagnated for the past few years.

→  Los salarios se han estancado en los últimos años.

STANCE   /stæns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la postura; la posición

Meaning:  a publicly stated opinion or position on an issue

Example:  She has a firm stance against animal testing.

→  Ella tiene una postura firme en contra de la experimentación con animales.

STIGMA   /ˈstɪɡmə/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el estigma

Meaning:  a feeling of shame or disapproval associated with something

Example:  There's still a stigma around discussing mental health.

→  Todavía existe un estigma en torno a hablar de salud mental.

STIMULUS   /ˈstɪmjələs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el estímulo

Example:  The government introduced a stimulus package for small businesses.

→  El gobierno introdujo un paquete de estímulo para pequeñas empresas.

STRIVE   /straɪv/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  esforzarse

Meaning:  to make a great effort to achieve something

Example:  We always strive to improve our service.

→  Siempre nos esforzamos por mejorar nuestro servicio.

Common:  strive for

Note:  Irregular: strive → strove → striven.

SUBDUE   /səbˈduː/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  someter; dominar; contener

Meaning:  to bring under control by force, or to make less intense

Example:  Police managed to subdue the suspect quickly.

→  La policía logró someter al sospechoso rápidamente.

SUBORDINATE   /səˈbɔːrdɪnət/  •  adjective; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  subordinado/a; el subordinado

Meaning:  lower in rank or importance; a person under the authority of another

Example:  He treats all his subordinates with respect.

→  Él trata a todos sus subordinados con respeto.

SUBSIDY   /ˈsʌbsɪdi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el subsidio

Meaning:  money given by a government to support an industry or activity

Example:  Farmers receive government subsidies for certain crops.

→  Los agricultores reciben subsidios del gobierno para ciertos cultivos.

SUBSTITUTE   /ˈsʌbstɪtuːt/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  el sustituto; sustituir

Meaning:  a person or thing used instead of another; to use one thing instead of another

Example:  You can use yogurt as a substitute for cream.

→  Puedes usar yogur como sustituto de la nata.

SUBTLE   /ˈsʌtəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  sutil

Meaning:  not obvious or easy to notice; delicate

Example:  There's a subtle difference between the two colors.

→  Hay una diferencia sutil entre los dos colores.

Note:  The "b" is silent.

SUBTLETY   /ˈsʌtəlti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la sutileza

Meaning:  a small detail or quality that is not obvious

Example:  The film captures the subtleties of everyday life.

→  La película capta las sutilezas de la vida cotidiana.

SUBVERT   /səbˈvɜːrt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  subvertir

Meaning:  to try to weaken or destroy an established system or authority

Example:  The scandal subverted public trust in the institution.

→  El escándalo subvirtió la confianza pública en la institución.

SUPPLEMENT   /ˈsʌplɪmənt/  •  noun; verb  •  B2

Spanish:  el suplemento; complementar

Meaning:  something added to complete or improve something else; to add to something

Example:  She takes a vitamin supplement every morning.

→  Ella toma un suplemento vitamínico cada mañana.

SUPPRESS   /səˈprɛs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  reprimir; suprimir

Meaning:  to stop something from being expressed or known; to prevent a feeling from being shown

Example:  He struggled to suppress a smile.

→  Le costó reprimir una sonrisa.

SURPASS   /sərˈpæs/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  superar

Meaning:  to do or be better than someone or something else

Example:  Sales this year have surpassed all expectations.

→  Las ventas de este año han superado todas las expectativas.

SURPLUS   /ˈsɜːrplʌs/  •  noun; adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  el excedente; el superávit

Meaning:  an amount left over after needs have been met

Example:  The country has a trade surplus this year.

→  El país tiene un superávit comercial este año.

SUSCEPTIBILITY   /səˌsɛptəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la susceptibilidad; la vulnerabilidad

Meaning:  the state of being easily affected or harmed by something

SUSTAIN   /səˈsteɪn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  sostener; mantener

Meaning:  to keep something going over time; to suffer an injury or loss

Example:  It's hard to sustain this level of growth long-term.

SYNTHESIS   /ˈsɪnθəsɪs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la síntesis

Meaning:  the combination of separate ideas or elements to form a whole

Example:  Her essay is a synthesis of several different theories.

→  Su ensayo es una síntesis de varias teorías distintas.

SYSTEMIC   /sɪˈstɛmɪk/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  sistémico/a

Meaning:  affecting an entire system, not just one part

Example:  The report identifies systemic problems in the healthcare system.

→  El informe identifica problemas sistémicos en el sistema de salud.

TACKLE   /ˈtækəl/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  abordar; enfrentar

Meaning:  to make a determined effort to deal with a difficult problem

Example:  The city is trying to tackle the housing shortage.

→  La ciudad está intentando abordar la escasez de vivienda.

TANGENT   /ˈtændʒənt/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la tangente

Meaning:  a completely different topic from what was being discussed

Example:  He went off on a tangent during the meeting.

→  Se fue por las ramas durante la reunión.

TANGIBLE   /ˈtændʒəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  tangible

Meaning:  able to be touched or clearly perceived; real and definite

Example:  We need to see tangible results from this project.

→  Necesitamos ver resultados tangibles de este proyecto.

TENSION   /ˈtɛnʃən/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la tensión

Meaning:  a feeling of nervousness or unease; a strained relationship between people

Example:  There was a lot of tension in the room after the announcement.

→  Había mucha tensión en la sala después del anuncio.

TENTATIVE   /ˈtɛntətɪv/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  provisional; tentativo/a

Meaning:  not certain or fixed; done with hesitation

Example:  We have a tentative date for the wedding.

→  Tenemos una fecha provisional para la boda.

TERMINATE   /ˈtɜːrmɪneɪt/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  terminar; rescindir

Meaning:  to bring something to an end, especially formally

Example:  The company terminated his contract early.

→  La empresa rescindió su contrato anticipadamente.

TESTIMONY   /ˈtɛstɪmoʊni/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el testimonio

Meaning:  a formal statement, especially given in court

Example:  Her testimony was crucial to the trial.

→  Su testimonio fue crucial para el juicio.

THRIVE   /θraɪv/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  prosperar; florecer

Meaning:  to grow, develop, or succeed vigorously

Example:  The business has thrived since it moved online.

→  El negocio ha prosperado desde que se mudó a internet.

TRANSPARENCY   /trænsˈpærənsi/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la transparencia

Meaning:  openness and honesty, especially in an organization's actions

Example:  The company promised more transparency about its pricing.

→  La empresa prometió más transparencia sobre sus precios.

TRANSPARENT   /trænsˈpærənt/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  transparente

Meaning:  easy to see through; open and honest

Example:  The government promised to be more transparent about spending.

→  El gobierno prometió ser más transparente sobre el gasto.

TURBULENCE   /ˈtɜːrbjələns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la turbulencia

Meaning:  sudden, violent movement of air; instability or confusion in a situation

Example:  The flight experienced some turbulence over the mountains.

→  El vuelo experimentó algo de turbulencia sobre las montañas.

UBIQUITY   /juːˈbɪkwəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la ubicuidad

Meaning:  the fact of appearing everywhere or being very common

Example:  The ubiquity of smartphones has changed daily life.

→  La ubicuidad de los smartphones ha cambiado la vida diaria.

ULTIMATUM   /ˌʌltɪˈmeɪtəm/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el ultimátum

Meaning:  a final demand or warning, with a threat if not met

Example:  She gave him an ultimatum: change or she would leave.

→  Ella le dio un ultimátum: cambiar o se iría.

UNANIMITY   /ˌjuːnəˈnɪməti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la unanimidad

Meaning:  complete agreement among all members of a group

Example:  There was surprising unanimity on the final decision.

→  Hubo una sorprendente unanimidad en la decisión final.

UNANIMOUS   /juːˈnænɪməs/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  unánime

Meaning:  agreed on by everyone in a group

Example:  The board reached a unanimous decision.

→  La junta llegó a una decisión unánime.

UNDERLYING   /ˌʌndərˈlaɪɪŋ/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  subyacente

Meaning:  forming the basis or cause of something, though not immediately obvious

Example:  We need to address the underlying cause of the problem.

→  Necesitamos abordar la causa subyacente del problema.

UNDERMINE   /ˌʌndərˈmaɪn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  socavar; debilitar

Meaning:  to gradually weaken something, such as trust or authority

Example:  His comments undermined her confidence.

→  Sus comentarios socavaron la confianza de ella.

UNDERPIN   /ˌʌndərˈpɪn/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  sustentar; apuntalar

Meaning:  to support or form the basis of something

Example:  Trust underpins every successful relationship.

→  La confianza sustenta toda relación exitosa.

UNDERTONE   /ˈʌndərtoʊn/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  el matiz; el trasfondo

Meaning:  an underlying quality, feeling, or meaning not directly stated

Example:  There was a slight undertone of sarcasm in her voice.

→  Había un ligero trasfondo de sarcasmo en su voz.

UNFOLD   /ʌnˈfoʊld/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  desarrollarse; desplegar; desplegarse

Meaning:  to gradually develop or become known; to open something out flat

Example:  We watched the story unfold on the news.

→  Vimos cómo se desarrollaba la historia en las noticias.

UNIFORMITY   /ˌjuːnɪˈfɔːrməti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la uniformidad

Meaning:  the state of being the same or consistent throughout

Example:  The company aims for uniformity across all its stores.

→  La empresa busca la uniformidad en todas sus tiendas.

UNIFY   /ˈjuːnɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  unificar

Meaning:  to bring separate parts together into a single whole

Example:  The new leader tried to unify the divided party.

→  El nuevo líder intentó unificar el partido dividido.

UNPRECEDENTED   /ʌnˈprɛsɪdɛntɪd/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  sin precedentes

Meaning:  never having happened before

Example:  The company saw unprecedented growth this year.

→  La empresa vio un crecimiento sin precedentes este año.

UPHOLD   /ʌpˈhoʊld/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  defender; sostener; confirmar

Meaning:  to support or maintain a law, principle, or decision

Example:  The court upheld the original ruling.

→  El tribunal confirmó el fallo original.

Note:  Irregular: uphold → upheld → upheld.

UTILITY   /juːˈtɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la utilidad; el servicio público

Meaning:  usefulness; a public service such as water or electricity

Example:  The app has real practical utility for travelers.

→  La aplicación tiene una utilidad práctica real para los viajeros.

UTILIZE   /ˈjuːtəlaɪz/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  utilizar

Meaning:  to make practical use of something

Example:  We should utilize the resources we already have.

→  Deberíamos utilizar los recursos que ya tenemos.

Note:  British spelling: "utilise".

VAGUENESS   /ˈveɪɡnəs/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la vaguedad; la imprecisión

Meaning:  the quality of being unclear or not specific

Example:  The vagueness of the instructions confused everyone.

→  La vaguedad de las instrucciones confundió a todos.

VARIABLE   /ˈvɛriəbəl/  •  adjective; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  variable

Meaning:  likely to change; something that can change or vary

Example:  The weather here is highly variable in spring.

→  El tiempo aquí es muy variable en primavera.

VERIFY   /ˈvɛrɪfaɪ/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  verificar

Meaning:  to check that something is true or accurate

Example:  Please verify your email address to continue.

→  Verifica tu dirección de correo para continuar.

VESTED   /ˈvɛstɪd/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  personal; creado (interés)

Meaning:  used especially in "vested interest": a strong personal reason for wanting something to happen

Example:  She has a vested interest in the company's success.

→  Ella tiene un interés personal en el éxito de la empresa.

Common:  vested interest

VIABILITY   /ˌvaɪəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la viabilidad

Meaning:  the ability of something to work successfully

Example:  They're testing the viability of the new business model.

→  Están probando la viabilidad del nuevo modelo de negocio.

VIABLE   /ˈvaɪəbəl/  •  adjective  •  B2

Spanish:  viable

Meaning:  capable of working successfully; practical

Example:  Is this plan financially viable?

→  ¿Es este plan viable económicamente?

VICINITY   /vɪˈsɪnəti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la vecindad; las inmediaciones

Meaning:  the area near or surrounding a particular place

Example:  There are several restaurants in the vicinity of the hotel.

→  Hay varios restaurantes en las inmediaciones del hotel.

VIGILANCE   /ˈvɪdʒələns/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la vigilancia

Meaning:  careful attention to possible danger or problems

Example:  The guards showed constant vigilance at the entrance.

→  Los guardias mostraron una vigilancia constante en la entrada.

VULNERABILITY   /ˌvʌlnərəˈbɪləti/  •  noun  •  B2

Spanish:  la vulnerabilidad

Meaning:  the state of being easily harmed or affected

Example:  The report highlights the vulnerability of coastal towns to flooding.

→  El informe destaca la vulnerabilidad de los pueblos costeros ante las inundaciones.

WITHHOLD   /wɪðˈhoʊld/  •  verb  •  B2

Spanish:  retener; ocultar (información)

Meaning:  to refuse to give or keep back something, such as information or payment

Example:  She withheld her opinion until everyone had spoken.

→  Ella se guardó su opinión hasta que todos hubieran hablado.

Note:  Irregular: withhold → withheld → withheld.

YIELD   /jiːld/  •  verb; noun  •  B2

Spanish:  ceder; producir; el rendimiento

Meaning:  to give way to pressure or demand; to produce a result or crop

Example:  The negotiations finally yielded a compromise.

→  Las negociaciones finalmente produjeron un acuerdo.

# C1 Vocabulary

Advanced vocabulary — 352 words, alphabetical.

ABDICATE   /ˈæbdɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  abdicar

Meaning:  to give up a position of power or a responsibility, often formally

Example:  The king abdicated the throne in favor of his son.

→  El rey abdicó el trono en favor de su hijo.

ABERRATION   /ˌæbəˈreɪʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la aberración; la anomalía

Meaning:  something that differs from what is normal or expected

Example:  His outburst was an aberration; he's usually very calm.

→  Su arrebato fue una anomalía; normalmente es muy tranquilo.

ABHOR   /əbˈhɔːr/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  aborrecer; detestar

Meaning:  to hate something very strongly

Example:  She abhors any form of cruelty to animals.

→  Ella aborrece cualquier forma de crueldad hacia los animales.

ABSTRACT   /ˈæbstrækt/  •  adjective; noun  •  C1

Spanish:  abstracto/a; el resumen

Meaning:  existing as an idea rather than a physical thing; a short summary of a paper

Example:  Justice is a rather abstract concept for young children.

→  La justicia es un concepto bastante abstracto para los niños pequeños.

ABSTRACTION   /æbˈstrækʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la abstracción

Meaning:  an idea or quality considered apart from any specific example

Example:  The lecture dealt with abstraction in modern art.

→  La conferencia trató sobre la abstracción en el arte moderno.

ACQUIESCE   /ˌækwiˈɛs/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  acceder; consentir

Meaning:  to accept something reluctantly, without protest

Example:  He eventually acquiesced to their demands.

→  Finalmente accedió a sus demandas.

Common:  acquiesce to

ADAGE   /ˈædɪdʒ/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el refrán; el dicho

Meaning:  a traditional saying that expresses a general truth

Example:  As the old adage goes, practice makes perfect.

→  Como dice el viejo refrán, la práctica hace al maestro.

ADEPT   /əˈdɛpt/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  experto/a; hábil

Meaning:  very skilled or proficient at something

Example:  She's adept at handling difficult clients.

→  Ella es hábil manejando a clientes difíciles.

Common:  adept at

ADHERENT   /ədˈhɪrənt/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el partidario; la partidaria; el adepto

Meaning:  a person who supports a particular idea, party, or belief

Example:  The movement gained many adherents over the years.

→  El movimiento ganó muchos partidarios con los años.

ADJUDICATE   /əˈdʒuːdɪkeɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  arbitrar; juzgar

Meaning:  to make an official decision about a dispute or competition

Example:  A panel of judges will adjudicate the final round.

→  Un panel de jueces arbitrará la ronda final.

ADMONISH   /ədˈmɒnɪʃ/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  amonestar; reprender

Meaning:  to firmly warn or reprimand someone

Example:  The teacher admonished the students for being late.

→  El profesor amonestó a los estudiantes por llegar tarde.

ADMONITION   /ˌædməˈnɪʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la amonestación; la advertencia

Meaning:  a firm warning or reprimand

Example:  He ignored his doctor's admonition to rest.

→  Ignoró la advertencia de su médico de descansar.

AFFINITY   /əˈfɪnəti/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la afinidad

Meaning:  a natural liking for or attraction to something

Example:  She has always had an affinity for the sea.

→  Ella siempre ha tenido una afinidad por el mar.

Common:  affinity for/with

ALACRITY   /əˈlækrəti/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la presteza; el entusiasmo

Meaning:  eager and cheerful readiness to do something

Example:  She agreed to help with alacrity.

→  Ella accedió a ayudar con entusiasmo.

ALLEVIATE   /əˈliːvieɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  aliviar

Meaning:  to make suffering or a problem less severe

Example:  The medicine helped alleviate her pain.

→  La medicina ayudó a aliviar su dolor.

ALLUSION   /əˈluːʒən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la alusión

Meaning:  an indirect reference to something

Example:  The poem makes several allusions to Greek mythology.

→  El poema hace varias alusiones a la mitología griega.

ALTRUISTIC   /ˌæltruˈɪstɪk/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  altruista

Meaning:  showing selfless concern for the well-being of others

Example:  Her decision to donate the money was purely altruistic.

→  Su decisión de donar el dinero fue puramente altruista.

AMALGAMATE   /əˈmælɡəmeɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  fusionar; amalgamar

Meaning:  to combine or merge different things into one

Example:  The two departments were amalgamated last year.

→  Los dos departamentos se fusionaron el año pasado.

AMBIVALENCE   /æmˈbɪvələns/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la ambivalencia

Meaning:  having mixed or contradictory feelings about something

Example:  She felt a deep ambivalence about leaving her job.

→  Sintió una profunda ambivalencia sobre dejar su trabajo.

AMBIVALENT   /æmˈbɪvələnt/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  ambivalente

Meaning:  having mixed feelings or contradictory ideas about something

Example:  He seemed ambivalent about the whole plan.

→  Él parecía ambivalente respecto a todo el plan.

ANACHRONISM   /əˈnækrənɪzəm/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el anacronismo

Meaning:  something placed in the wrong time period, or that seems outdated

Example:  The old law feels like an anachronism today.

→  La antigua ley parece un anacronismo hoy en día.

ANOINT   /əˈnɔɪnt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  ungir; nombrar (formalmente)

Example:  He was anointed as the party's next leader.

ANTAGONIZE   /ænˈtæɡənaɪz/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  provocar; enemistarse con

Example:  He didn't mean to antagonize his colleagues.

Note:  British spelling: "antagonise".

APHORISM   /ˈæfərɪzəm/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el aforismo

Meaning:  a short, wise saying that expresses a general truth

Example:  "Knowledge is power" is a well-known aphorism.

→  "El conocimiento es poder" es un aforismo muy conocido.

APPEASE   /əˈpiːz/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  apaciguar; aplacar

Meaning:  to calm someone down or satisfy their demands to avoid conflict

Example:  They offered a refund to appease the angry customer.

→  Ofrecieron un reembolso para apaciguar al cliente enfadado.

APPREHENSION   /ˌæprɪˈhɛnʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la aprensión; el temor

Meaning:  anxiety or fear about something that might happen

Example:  She felt a growing sense of apprehension before the interview.

→  Ella sintió una creciente sensación de aprensión antes de la entrevista.

ARCHETYPE   /ˈɑːrkɪtaɪp/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el arquetipo

Meaning:  a typical or perfect example of a certain type of person or thing

ARTICULATION   /ɑːrˌtɪkjəˈleɪʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la articulación; la expresión clara

ASCENDANCY   /əˈsɛndənsi/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el ascendiente; la supremacía

Meaning:  a position of power or dominance over others

Example:  The party gained ascendancy after the election.

ASCERTAIN   /ˌæsərˈteɪn/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  averiguar; determinar

Meaning:  to find out or establish something for certain

Example:  We need to ascertain the exact cause of the delay.

ASSUAGE   /əˈsweɪdʒ/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  aliviar; mitigar; calmar

Meaning:  to make an unpleasant feeling less intense

Example:  Her words helped assuage his fears.

→  Sus palabras ayudaron a aliviar sus miedos.

ASTUTE   /əˈstuːt/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  astuto/a; perspicaz

Meaning:  showing sharp judgment and good understanding of situations

Example:  She made an astute business decision.

→  Ella tomó una decisión de negocios astuta.

ATTRITION   /əˈtrɪʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el desgaste

Meaning:  a gradual reduction in numbers or strength through continuous pressure

Example:  The company suffers from high staff attrition.

→  La empresa sufre un alto desgaste de personal.

AUDACIOUS   /ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  audaz

Meaning:  showing bold, often risky, confidence

Example:  It was an audacious plan, but it worked.

→  Fue un plan audaz, pero funcionó.

AUDACITY   /ɔːˈdæsəti/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la audacia; el descaro

Meaning:  bold or daring behavior, sometimes seen as disrespectful

Example:  He had the audacity to ask for a raise after being late all week.

→  Tuvo el descaro de pedir un aumento después de llegar tarde toda la semana.

AUSTERE   /ɔːˈstɪr/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  austero/a

Meaning:  simple and plain, without any luxury or decoration

Example:  The monks lived an austere life in the mountains.

→  Los monjes llevaban una vida austera en las montañas.

AUSTERITY   /ɔːˈstɛrəti/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la austeridad

Meaning:  a simple and plain style of living; strict economic policies with reduced spending

Example:  The government introduced austerity measures to cut spending.

→  El gobierno introdujo medidas de austeridad para reducir el gasto.

AXIOM   /ˈæksiəm/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el axioma

Meaning:  a statement widely accepted as true without needing proof

Example:  It's almost an axiom that hard work leads to success.

→  Es casi un axioma que el trabajo duro lleva al éxito.

BELIE   /bɪˈlaɪ/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  desmentir; contradecir

Meaning:  to give a false or misleading impression of something

Example:  Her calm expression belied her nervousness.

→  Su expresión tranquila desmentía su nerviosismo.

BENEVOLENCE   /bəˈnɛvələns/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la benevolencia

Meaning:  kindness and generosity toward others

Example:  The charity relies on the benevolence of donors.

→  La organización benéfica depende de la benevolencia de los donantes.

BENEVOLENT   /bəˈnɛvələnt/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  benévolo/a

Meaning:  kind, generous, and well-meaning

Example:  The benevolent old man donated his fortune to the school.

→  El benévolo anciano donó su fortuna a la escuela.

BERATE   /bɪˈreɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  reprender; regañar

Meaning:  to criticize someone angrily

Example:  She berated him for forgetting the meeting.

→  Ella lo regañó por olvidar la reunión.

BOLSTER   /ˈboʊlstər/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  reforzar; fortalecer

Meaning:  to support or strengthen something

Example:  The good news helped bolster investor confidence.

→  La buena noticia ayudó a fortalecer la confianza de los inversores.

BREVITY   /ˈbrɛvəti/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la brevedad

Meaning:  the quality of being short and concise

Example:  His speech was appreciated for its brevity.

→  Su discurso fue apreciado por su brevedad.

CANDID   /ˈkændɪd/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  franco/a; sincero/a

Meaning:  honest and direct, even about difficult topics

Example:  She gave a candid account of her struggles.

→  Ella dio un relato franco de sus dificultades.

CANDOR   /ˈkændər/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la franqueza

Meaning:  the quality of being open and honest

Example:  He spoke with real candor about his mistakes.

→  Él habló con verdadera franqueza sobre sus errores.

Note:  British spelling: "candour".

CAPITULATE   /kəˈpɪtʃəleɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  capitular; rendirse

Meaning:  to stop resisting and accept an opponent's demands

Example:  The company eventually capitulated to the union's demands.

→  La empresa finalmente capituló ante las demandas del sindicato.

CAPITULATION   /kəˌpɪtʃəˈleɪʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la capitulación; la rendición

Meaning:  the act of giving up resistance or accepting demands

Example:  The capitulation of the enemy ended the war.

→  La capitulación del enemigo puso fin a la guerra.

CASTIGATE   /ˈkæstɪɡeɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  castigar; criticar severamente

Meaning:  to criticize someone severely

Example:  The report castigated the government for its inaction.

→  El informe criticó severamente al gobierno por su inacción.

CATHARSIS   /kəˈθɑːrsɪs/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la catarsis

Meaning:  a release of strong emotions, often bringing relief

Example:  Writing about the loss was a form of catharsis for her.

→  Escribir sobre la pérdida fue una forma de catarsis para ella.

CAVEAT   /ˈkæviæt/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la advertencia; la salvedad

Meaning:  a warning or condition attached to an agreement or statement

Example:  I'll help, with the caveat that I can't stay too late.

→  Ayudaré, con la salvedad de que no puedo quedarme hasta muy tarde.

CIRCUMSPECTION   /ˌsɜːrkəmˈspɛkʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la circunspección; la cautela

Meaning:  careful thought before acting or speaking

Example:  He handled the delicate matter with great circumspection.

→  Él manejó el delicado asunto con gran circunspección.

CLARITY   /ˈklærəti/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  la claridad

Meaning:  the quality of being clear and easy to understand

Example:  She explained the process with great clarity.

→  Ella explicó el proceso con gran claridad.

COERCE   /koʊˈɜːrs/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  coaccionar; obligar

Meaning:  to force someone to do something using threats or pressure

Example:  He was coerced into signing the contract.

→  Lo obligaron a firmar el contrato.

COGNIZANCE   /ˈkɒɡnɪzəns/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el conocimiento; la conciencia (de algo)

Meaning:  awareness or knowledge of something

Example:  The committee took cognizance of the new evidence.

→  El comité tomó conocimiento de las nuevas pruebas.

COMMEMORATE   /kəˈmɛməreɪt/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  conmemorar

Meaning:  to remember and honor an important event or person

Example:  The monument commemorates soldiers who died in the war.

→  El monumento conmemora a los soldados que murieron en la guerra.

COMPARTMENTALIZE   /kəmˌpɑːrtˈmɛntəlaɪz/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  compartimentar

Meaning:  to separate things into different categories, especially feelings from actions

Example:  She's good at compartmentalizing work and personal life.

→  Ella es buena compartimentando el trabajo y la vida personal.

Note:  British spelling: "compartmentalise".

COMPLACENT   /kəmˈpleɪsənt/  •  adjective  •  C1

Spanish:  complaciente; autocomplaciente

Meaning:  feeling too satisfied with yourself or a situation, and not worried enough about problems

Example:  The team became complacent after winning several games in a row.

→  El equipo se volvió complaciente tras ganar varios partidos seguidos.

COMPUNCTION   /kəmˈpʌŋkʃən/  •  noun  •  C1

Spanish:  el escrúpulo; el remordimiento

Meaning:  a feeling of guilt or hesitation about doing something wrong

Example:  He showed no compunction about lying to his boss.

→  No mostró ningún escrúpulo al mentirle a su jefe.

CONDONE   /kənˈdoʊn/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  tolerar; condonar

Meaning:  to accept or allow behavior that is considered wrong

Example:  The school does not condone any form of bullying.

→  La escuela no tolera ninguna forma de acoso.

CONFOUND   /kənˈfaʊnd/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  desconcertar; confundir

Meaning:  to confuse or surprise someone, especially by being unexpected

Example:  The result confounded even the experts.

→  El resultado desconcertó incluso a los expertos.

CONJECTURE   /kənˈdʒɛktʃər/  •  noun; verb  •  C1

Spanish:  la conjetura; conjeturar

Meaning:  a conclusion formed on the basis of incomplete information; to guess

Example:  It's just conjecture at this point — we have no real proof.

→  Es solo una conjetura por ahora; no tenemos pruebas reales.

CONJURE   /ˈkɒndʒər/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  evocar; hacer aparecer (como por magia)

Meaning:  to make something appear as if by magic; to bring an image to mind

Example:  The song conjures up memories of my childhood.

→  La canción evoca recuerdos de mi infancia.

Common:  conjure up

CONNIVE   /kəˈnaɪv/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  conspirar; confabularse

Meaning:  to secretly plan to do something wrong with others

Example:  They connived to keep the information hidden.

→  Se confabularon para mantener la información oculta.

CONSPIRE   /kənˈspaɪər/  •  verb  •  C1

Spanish:  conspirar

Meaning:  to plan secretly with others to do something harmful or illegal

Example:  They were accused of conspiring to commit fraud.
